# State

**Milestone:** P1 Persistence with SQLite complete (T-067 export/import dropped by the owner); U1 User accounts complete; C1 CaCi controller complete (T-085 to T-095); C2 Circles complete (T-096 to T-108); W1 PWA first slice complete (T-109 to T-116); W2 the web app with circles complete (T-117 to T-127); W3 the web app with notes complete (T-128 to T-138); S1 shared circle brains (R-008) complete (T-139 to T-152); S2 the shared circle brain (R-009) complete (T-153 to T-170); D1 Raspberry Pi deployment complete (T-171 to T-180) and **running on the owner's Pi (confirmed 2026-10-13)**; Q1 status updates (R-011) planned (T-181 to T-194); K1 controllable LLM communication (R-012) complete (T-195 to T-204); B1 brain quality evaluation (R-013) complete (T-205 to T-214), first real run done 2026-10-14; V1 the current-view brain (R-014) complete (T-215 to T-231), awaiting the owner's real run; F1 the fact-level brain (R-015) planned (T-232 to T-241)
**Current task:** none (next: T-264 The brain-eval scenario draft)
**Last updated:** 2026-10-21

## Position
R-001 (Bipartite Graph Store library) is specified in `specs/` and planned through M3 in PLAN.md. Package scaffolded and verification gate live (`npm run gate`: tsc, eslint, vitest); M0 complete; spec, decisions and architecture docs are in sync. `Result`/`GraphError` types exist (T-005); mutation v1 Zod schema exists (T-006, strict objects, internal until T-013); query v1 Zod schema exists (T-007); `parseMutation`/`parseQuery` exist and are exported (T-008); JSON Schema generated from Zod into `schema/` (T-009, `npm run schema`); configurable limits enforced in the parsers (T-010); `StorageAdapter`/`AdapterTx` contract types defined and exported (T-011); `write`/`query`/`createGraphClient` exist as parse-then-not-implemented stubs (T-012); public API locked by API Extractor report in `api/` and purity enforced by lint (T-013). M1 Contracts complete. All graph store code now lives under `src/graph_store/` (T-023). Memory adapter has graphs and nodes with copy-on-write transactions (T-014); edges are indexed from both ends with idempotent upsert and keyset paging (T-015). Write planning has a pure graph-resolution step plus I/O shell, internal and not yet wired into `write()` (T-016). `upsertNode`/`deleteNode` ops with cascade exist as internal functional core + transactional shell, not yet wired into `write()` (T-017). `link`/`unlink` ops exist the same way (T-018); `write()` is real (T-019): it applies a mutation's ops in order inside one adapter transaction, all-or-nothing, and returns `{ graphId, applied, graphCreated }`. `query()` is still a stub. `createGraph`, `dropGraph`, `listGraphs`, `describeGraph` are public and on the client (T-024 to T-026); API report re-baselined for them. M2b complete. `runAdapterConformance` harness exists with a 5-case smoke group, passes on the memory adapter, and is proven to catch nine broken adapters (T-020). AC-02 to AC-06 now live in the shared suite's `write` group (T-021), so every future adapter must pass them. The `isolation` group (AC-01, AC-12) and an independent fixture adapter proving AC-15 are in too (T-022); the README has an adapter-author guide. The `lifecycle` group (FR-01) is in too (T-027). Graph ids are restricted to a safe character set (T-029). A local-only visual tester lives in `dev/graph-explorer/` (T-030, `npm run dev:explorer`). M4–M7 are outlined, not yet broken into tasks.

## Decisions
- 2026-10-01 — Schema validator is **Zod** — largest ecosystem, one runtime dep per NFR-03, JSON Schema derivable.
- 2026-10-01 — Toolchain is **npm + Vitest + ESLint (flat config) + `tsc --noEmit`** — gate commands to be written into system_prompt.md in T-003.
- 2026-10-01 — `link` **fails on missing endpoints by default**; `ensureNodes: true` opts in — strict and explicit, as in the spec.
- 2026-10-01 — **Lean v1**: `requestId` accepted in the schema but not implemented (no replay); caller-supplied ids only; strict bipartite, no category hierarchy.
- 2026-10-01 — **TypeScript pinned to `~6.0`** (T-002 had installed 7.0) — `typescript-eslint` peer range is `<6.1.0`; revisit when it supports 7.
- 2026-10-01 — Schemas use **strict objects** (unknown keys rejected) so typos in LLM-written instructions fail loudly; `version` is a literal checked separately for `UNSUPPORTED_VERSION` in T-008.
- 2026-10-01 — Query schema has **no `kind` field** (spec examples omit it); a mutation sent to it fails on unknown keys. `where` keys must be `data.<path>`.
- 2026-10-01 — Parsers check wrong-endpoint input before `version`, and report only the first Zod issue (one `path` per `GraphError`). All parse paths run inside a `guarded()` wrapper so hostile input cannot throw.
- 2026-10-01 — JSON Schema uses Zod 4 built-in `z.toJSONSchema` (draft 2020-12, `io: 'input'` so defaults are optional); generated files are committed and a drift test fails if they are stale. `ajv` is a dev-only dependency for tests.
- 2026-10-01 — Limits (NFR-06) are enforced in the parsers via an optional `{ limits }` argument, not in the static schema; `data` size is measured in UTF-8 bytes; op count is checked on raw input before the schema parse; limits also apply to query ids.
- 2026-10-01 — Supporting adapter types are our own definitions (spec leaves them open): `Paged<T>` = `{ items, nextCursor }`, `NodeRecord` = `{ partition, id, data? }`, `EdgeRecord` = `{ item, category, weight?, data? }`, `SetClause` = `{ all?, any?, none? }`. `AdapterTx` is scoped to one graph by `transaction(graphId, …)`.
- 2026-10-01 — Endpoint stubs return `STORAGE_ERROR` "not implemented" for valid input (error enum is closed, nothing fits better); `WriteOutput`/`QueryOutput` are placeholders until M2/M4.
- 2026-10-01 — Public instruction types (`Mutation`, `Op`, `Query`, …) are hand-written in `src/graph_store/types.ts` so the API report records their shape; `types.test.ts` asserts mutual assignability with the Zod-inferred `Parsed*` types. Changing a schema means updating both.
- 2026-10-01 — API Extractor (`npm run api` to update the report, `api:check` is part of the gate) bundles TS 5.9 and warns about TS 6.0; output is still correct. Purity (NFR-02) is a lint rule on `src/**` non-test files plus a test that proves the rule fires.
- 2026-10-01 — **Graph store isolation**: everything lives in `src/graph_store/` (user decision, matches the container diagram); package entry points build to `dist/graph_store/`; generated JSON Schema moved to `schema/graph_store/` and `$id` now ends `/schema/graph_store/<name>.v1.schema.json`. Public API report unchanged by the move. Test map is in the R-001 spec.
- 2026-10-01 — Memory adapter: transactions work on a shallow copy of one graph and swap on success; a per-graph promise queue serialises transactions, `create` and `drop` (no lost updates); node data is deep-copied in and out; `graphs.create` and `graphs.drop` are idempotent (spec silent; conformance suite in M3 should state this); keyset cursor is the base64url of the last key, ordering is UTF-16 code-unit order.
- 2026-10-01 — Adapters are deliberately dumb about integrity: the memory adapter does not check that edge endpoints exist and `deleteNodes` does not remove edges; the core must cascade using `edgesOf` + `deleteEdges` + `deleteNodes` in one transaction (FR-07). `putEdges` replaces the whole edge record. Conformance suite (M3) must encode this.
- 2026-10-01 — Write is built as functional core + imperative shell: `planGraphResolution` is pure, `resolveGraph` does the I/O and returns `{ created }`. Public `write()` stays a stub until T-019 wires everything, so no half-applied mutation is ever observable. Adapter failures map to `STORAGE_ERROR` via a shared `storageError` helper.
- 2026-10-01 — Node ops (T-017): `upsertNode` `replace` sets data to exactly the op data (omitted data clears it, PUT semantics) — PROVISIONAL, see Blockers; `merge` is shallow (op keys win, `null` overwrites, nested objects replaced); `deleteNode` removes the node's edges page by page (1000 per page) then the node, and is a no-op for a missing node (idempotent, no `NODE_NOT_FOUND`).
- 2026-10-01 — Link ops (T-018): `link` replaces the whole edge (an omitted weight/data clears the old one; same PUT semantics as upsertNode `replace`, follows that provisional decision); `unlink` of a missing edge or nodes is a no-op; `NODE_NOT_FOUND` reports the item first and its path is relative to the op (runner prefixes `['ops', i]`). AC-03 holds by construction (the format has one `item` and one `category` field) and is proven by parser tests rather than a runtime check.
- 2026-10-01 — Atomic write (T-019): a failing op throws inside the transaction so the adapter rolls back; errors get an `['ops', i]` path prefix. A graph created by a failed call is dropped only if still empty (protects a concurrent writer; a small race remains, see PLAN Backlog). Adapters reporting `transactions: false` are refused with `STORAGE_ERROR`. `WriteOutput` = `{ graphId, applied, graphCreated }`; API report re-baselined for it.
- 2026-10-01 — **FR-01 graph lifecycle (user decision, option 2)**: graphs are managed by four top-level functions beside the two endpoints — `createGraph`, `dropGraph`, `listGraphs`, `describeGraph` — not by extra ops in the mutation/query formats. This widens the public API deliberately; the v1 mutation and query schemas stay unchanged. Planned as M2b (T-024 to T-026), scheduled before the conformance suite so M3 can include lifecycle behaviour. Proposed semantics, to confirm: `createGraph` on an existing graph gives `CONFLICT` (whereas `createIfMissing` in `write` stays idempotent); `dropGraph`/`describeGraph` on a missing graph give `GRAPH_NOT_FOUND`; `describeGraph` returns `{ graphId, itemCount, categoryCount, edgeCount }`, counted by paging (O(n) in v1).
- 2026-10-01 — Lifecycle (T-024): the confirmed semantics are implemented — `createGraph` on an existing graph gives `CONFLICT`, `dropGraph` on a missing graph gives `GRAPH_NOT_FOUND`, graph ids are validated (non-empty string, within the id length limit) before any adapter call. Known race: existence check and create are two adapter calls (see PLAN Backlog, wants an atomic create-if-absent primitive).
- 2026-10-01 — List/describe (T-025): `listGraphs(adapter, page?)` validates `page` strictly (`undefined` or `{ limit?, cursor? }`, limit 1 to 1000 default 50, unknown keys rejected). `describeGraph` counts by walking the graph in one transaction (O(n), blocks writers to that graph meanwhile). `DEFAULT_PAGE_LIMIT`/`MAX_PAGE_LIMIT` now live in `limits.ts` and are shared with the query schema.
- 2026-10-01 — Public client (T-026): `createGraphClient(adapter)` returns a frozen `{ write, query, createGraph, dropGraph, listGraphs, describeGraph }`; the API report change was purely additive (4 functions, `GraphInfo`, `GraphRef`, 4 client members).
- 2026-10-02 — Conformance harness (T-020): signature is `runAdapterConformance(makeAdapter, { describe, it }, { dispose? })` — the caller passes their runner's functions, so the library depends on no test framework and a forgotten runner cannot silently register nothing (deviates from the spec's one-argument form; spec updated). Assertions use `node:assert/strict`. A fresh adapter is made per test and disposed after it, pass or fail. Cases are data (`conformanceGroups()`), built by functions to satisfy the purity lint.
- 2026-10-02 — API reports now cover all three entry points (`.`, `./adapters/memory`, `./testing`) via `api-extractor.memory.json` and `api-extractor.testing.json`; `npm run api` / `api:check` run all three. `StorageAdapter` is intentionally imported from the main entry, not re-exported by the sub-entries.
- 2026-10-02 — Suite scope (T-021): suite cases use only the public API (`write`, graph functions) plus observable adapter state, so file and SQLite adapters can run them unchanged. The 16 duplicated AC-02..06 tests were removed from the core test files; pure-logic unit tests and the memory adapter's own primitive tests stay (retitled as adapter primitives). Each moved behaviour was mutation-checked against the core and caught by the shared suite. Primitive-level cases to be promoted into the suite before M5 (PLAN Backlog).
- 2026-10-02 — Isolation and adapter-swap (T-022): cases receive `makeAnother` from the harness (it tracks and disposes every adapter it hands out), so cases can use two independent adapters; `MakeAdapter` must return adapters that share nothing. AC-15 is evidenced by `testing/alternative-adapter.test.ts`, a fixture adapter built with a different strategy that passes the same suite with no core changes; it is a test fixture and does not serialise concurrent transactions (concurrency is covered only by the memory adapter's own tests until T-028). Two follow-up suite groups were planned as T-027 (lifecycle) and T-028 (primitives, before M5).
- 2026-10-02 — Lifecycle group (T-027): beyond create/drop/list/describe it requires that graph and node ids are opaque and case-sensitive — any string up to 256 characters including `/`, `..`, spaces, backslashes and non-ASCII must round-trip unchanged, and `Graph` and `graph` are different graphs. This is our reading of "ids are strings" (spec silent); it exists so the file and SQLite adapters (M5/M6) must encode ids instead of using them raw as file names. Six deliberately broken adapters are caught, each by the targeted case.
- 2026-10-02 — **Graph ids restricted (user decision, T-029)**: a `graphId` is 1 to 128 characters of `[a-z0-9_-]`, starting with a letter or digit, enforced in both schemas (and so in the published JSON Schema, which now carries the pattern), `createGraph`, `dropGraph` and `describeGraph`, always before any adapter call. Reason: graph ids become file names and keys; lowercase-only also removes case-collision on case-insensitive file systems (macOS, Windows). Strict first on purpose: loosening later is non-breaking, tightening is not. This SUPERSEDES the T-027 note that graph ids may be any string and are case-sensitive; **node ids remain opaque and case-sensitive** and the suite still requires them to round-trip. The lifecycle group now tests legal graph-id extremes and awkward node ids; two broken-adapter fixtures were replaced to match.
- 2026-10-02 — **Graph explorer (user request, T-030)**: `dev/graph-explorer/`, a local-only visual tester. Plain Node `http` server (no new dependencies) around the real library and memory adapter, bound to 127.0.0.1 with Host/Origin/Content-Type checks and a refusal to run under NODE_ENV=production; vanilla SVG UI with animated build-up and removal, scenarios, a request/result log and one-click rejected examples. It reads graph contents through the adapter's own primitives because `query` is still a stub. Kept out of releases three ways: outside `src/` (not compiled), outside the `files` allow-list, and a test that runs `npm pack --dry-run` and fails if anything from `dev/` is in it. Run with `npm run dev:explorer`.
- 2026-10-02 — Primitives group (T-028): the shared suite now holds the whole storage contract the core relies on — node and edge round trips, replace-on-write, copied (never shared) data, atomic and **serialised** transactions with read-your-writes, a transaction on a missing graph rejecting without creating it, cascade from `edgesOf` + `deleteEdges` + `deleteNodes`, and keyset paging (stable under inserts and deletes; a page that exactly fills the limit has a null cursor). Its first run correctly failed the fixture adapter, which did not serialise transactions; the fixture was fixed, not the case. Paging loops in the suite use a capped `walk` so a broken cursor fails a test instead of hanging. The memory adapter keeps only tests of what is specific to it; 29 deliberately broken adapters are caught, and four deliberate breaks of the memory adapter itself were each caught by the shared suite.
- 2026-10-02 — **LLM integration (user decision)**: build it as separate components beside the graph store — `src/llm/` (categoriser plus a `ModelClient` port; the real Anthropic client is its own entry point and the only place the SDK dependency lives) and `src/app/` (the controller that connects input, context, categoriser, preview and `write`). Order chosen: M4a basic reads first (the categoriser needs the existing categories as context), then L1, A1, L2, A1b; M4b matching and presets follow. **Preview, then approve**: nothing the model proposes is written until a person approves it. Planned as T-031 to T-050. Guardrails: structured output only, an op allow-list (`upsertNode` and `link` only, no deletes), tighter caps via the new `{ limits }` option, item ids minted by the controller, user text kept in a delimited untrusted block, one bounded repair attempt, and no network in the gate (a scripted model stands in).
- 2026-10-02 — `return.includeData` defaults to **false** (provisional, my call: the spec's own first example sets it to true explicitly, which implies false is the default; smaller payloads; callers who want data ask for it). The categoriser's context reads ask for data explicitly. Change before T-031 ships if you disagree.
- 2026-10-02 — **LLM component API and model choice (user direction)**: the module exposes capability-specific functions — `createLlm({ client, config })` returns a frozen object with one method per capability, today `categorise()`; each capability lives in its own folder under `src/llm/capabilities/`, so adding `answer()` or others later changes nothing that exists. The model is configuration, not code: three tiers (`fast` = Haiku 4.5, `balanced` = Sonnet 5.5, `deep` = Opus 5.5, overridable) and per-capability routing (`categorise` → `fast`), with precedence per-call model > capability setting > tier default; every `ModelClient` request names its model. Env/file reading stays in the app and dev tools. A second local-only tool, the **LLM lab** (`dev/llm-lab/`, T-052), lets you try a capability against any model, see the prompt, raw output, guard checks, proposal, tokens and latency, and compare models side by side; a shared dev-server module (T-053) is factored out of the graph explorer first. Planned as T-051 to T-053.
- 2026-10-02 — Query results and cursors (T-031): four result shapes as explicit public types (`nodes`, `ids`, `count`, `subgraph`), told apart by their keys; no tag field, so the spec's JSON is unchanged. One fixed order: items then categories, each by id in UTF-16 order. `page.limit` counts nodes; in a `subgraph` each edge travels with its item end and only if its category end is in the whole result, so a page can carry more edges than `limit`. `truncated` means a size cap cut the result (reached-node cap, default 10,000; implemented in T-034), never paging. Edge `weight` is always reported (1 when none is stored, as the spec's example shows); `data` follows `includeData`, default false (provisional). Cursors are opaque, versioned, checksummed and bound to a fingerprint of the query (everything except `page`), so a cursor from another query, or a corrupted or edited one, is a `VALIDATION_ERROR` at `page.cursor`.
- 2026-10-02 — Query planner (T-032): `planQuery` is pure and internal. It refuses features that the schema accepts but nothing executes yet — `from.where`, `filter.where`, and the `all`/`any`/`none` clauses, **including empty ones** (an empty set clause is not read as "no constraint", because the spec does not say what it means and a wrong guess gives answers that look right) — with `VALIDATION_ERROR` "not supported yet" and a path, in the fixed order from.where, filter.where, all, any, none; M4b removes these refusals. `from: all` ignores `traverse` (plan depth 0). Seed ids are de-duplicated and ordered. A cursor on a `count` is refused (a count is not paged). `excludeSeeds` on `from: all` is passed through (the result is empty).
- 2026-10-02 — Read executor (T-033): `executePlan` reads only through a frozen handle with `getNodes`, `listNodes` and `edgesOf` (no write methods exist on it); the shell `executeQuery` checks the graph exists (`GRAPH_NOT_FOUND`), runs inside one adapter transaction and turns any failure into `STORAGE_ERROR`. Handles `from: all` and named seeds at depth 0, the partition filter, `nodes`/`ids`/`count`, `includeData` and paging. Paging is by position: the executor skips adapter pages until it passes the cursor's node (correct and stable, but each page costs more the further in it is; see Backlog). Depth above 0 and `subgraph` are refused by name until T-034/T-035, so named seeds currently need an explicit `depth: 0`. `Limits` gained `maxReachedNodes` (default 10,000): `count` stops there as a lower bound, and named seeds beyond it are cut and marked `truncated`. The executor stops with `STORAGE_ERROR` when an adapter's cursor does not advance, instead of looping.
- 2026-10-02 — Traversal (T-034): breadth-first from the named seeds, `depth` hops, each hop crossing to the other partition; the result is cumulative (everything within the depth, seeds included) with each node once. The spec's sentence about hops was ambiguous ("depth 2 [from a category] gives items sharing a category"); by its own rule that each hop flips partition, that is depth 3 from a category or depth 2 from an item, so the unambiguous flip rule is implemented and the spec sentence and the T-034 plan text were corrected. `excludeSeeds` drops the seeds but keeps what they led to; the partition filter narrows the reached set after the walk (the walk still passes through the other kind). The reached-node cap stops the walk, including partway through a hub's edge listing (a 5,000-item hub with cap 100 takes at most 2 edge-page reads), and sets `truncated`; which nodes survive a cut is deterministic. An edge to a node that is gone is skipped. Cycles cannot loop (depth is at most 3 and results are keyed). `subgraph` is still refused until T-035.
- 2026-10-02 — Subgraph shape (T-035): the executor now handles all four shapes. A page's edges are those of its item nodes whose category end is also in the whole result (named seeds: the reached set after `excludeSeeds` and the partition filter; whole graph: the category exists, and no edges at all under a partition filter), so each edge appears once, on its item's page, and a page can carry more edges than `limit`. Edges are ordered by item then category. Weight is always reported (1 when none stored, a stored 0 kept); edge `data` follows `includeData`. A page carries at most `maxReachedNodes` edges (default 10,000, the same limit as reached nodes) and is marked `truncated` beyond that, so one item with a huge edge list cannot exhaust memory. The executor now refuses nothing; the planner still refuses data matching and set clauses until M4b. `query()` itself is still a stub until T-036.
- 2026-10-02 — `query()` wired (T-036): parse, plan, execute, with every refusal returned as a result before the adapter is touched (proved with an adapter that records any property read, across 17 invalid inputs). Options: `write`, `query`, `createGraph`, `dropGraph`, `describeGraph` and `createGraphClient` take an optional `GraphOptions` (**renamed from `ParseOptions`**, since it now configures reads, writes and graph ids). Only `limits` exists (maxOps, maxDataBytes, maxIdLength, maxReachedNodes); it is validated strictly — unknown options or limit names and non-positive-integer values are `VALIDATION_ERROR` with an `options` path before the adapter is touched, and `createGraphClient` throws `RangeError` for them since that is a coding mistake, not a request. A client keeps its own frozen copy of its options. `listGraphs` takes no options (it validates a page, not ids). The placeholder `QueryOutput` and the "decide how options reach the endpoints" Backlog item are gone.
- 2026-10-02 — Reads group (T-037): the shared suite now holds `query()` end to end (17 cases: AC-09, 13, 17, 19, 21, 22 plus FR-14 paging, seeds, filters, caps, isolation), built only from the public `write`/`query` with adapter primitives used solely to prove the store unchanged. Both the memory adapter and the independent fixture adapter pass; six deliberately broken adapters are caught (wrong order, a repeating cursor, a read that writes, subgraphs losing edges, a listing returning both kinds, a cursor that never advances — which fails promptly instead of hanging), and eight deliberate breaks of the core were each caught by the shared suite. **Ordering contract made explicit**: listings and results are ordered by id in UTF-16 code-unit order (JavaScript's `<`), not by locale and not by UTF-8 byte order, which differs for characters outside the basic plane; a case with `😀` and U+FF5E pins it, and the README says so. This matters for the SQLite adapter (M6).
- 2026-10-02 — Explorer reads through `query()` (T-038): the picture is one whole-graph subgraph query, paged, so the tool exercises the public API; `POST /api/query` forwards any query; the page has a Query panel (JSON editor, seven presets, Next page, highlight of matching nodes and links, refusals shown in red). Verified by hand in Chrome; it has no automated browser test (see Backlog). Note for scripted browser checks: a background tab throttles timers and never fires `requestAnimationFrame`, so wait on server round trips. **M4a (core reads) is complete**: the query engine, its conformance group and the explorer all work end to end; M4b (data matching, set clauses, presets) is deferred until after the LLM work.
- 2026-10-03 — LLM component scaffold (T-039): `src/llm/` is a sibling of `graph_store` with its own `./llm` entry point and API report (the gate now checks four reports). Shared kernel: `errors` (closed `LlmError` codes TIMEOUT, RATE_LIMITED, REFUSED, BAD_OUTPUT, MODEL_ERROR, CONFIG; `retryable` true by default only for timeouts and rate limits, overridable; frozen), `usage` (token counts, `addUsage`, no cost because prices change) and `model-client` (the `ModelClient` port: every request names its model, max output tokens and timeout, may carry an output schema and an `AbortSignal`; the reply is text or parsed JSON plus usage and the model that actually answered; clients never throw). `capabilities/` is an empty folder whose README states the rule that a capability never imports another. `src/llm/boundary.test.ts` enforces: the graph store never imports the LLM component; the LLM reaches the graph store only through `graph_store/index`; the LLM never imports the app; capabilities do not import each other; the kernel does not import a capability. The checker is itself tested on made-up sources and on three deliberate real violations. The LLM component reuses the graph store's `Result`, `ok`, `err` and `JsonValue` types through that public entry.
- 2026-10-03 — Model tiers and routing (T-051): `createLlmConfig` and `resolveModel` in `src/llm/config.ts`, pure and never throwing. Tiers `fast` = `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`, `balanced` = `claude-sonnet-5-5`, `deep` = `claude-opus-5-5`, all overridable; `categorise` routes to `fast` by default (provisional until the T-048 evaluation). A capability route is an object, `{ tier }` or `{ model }` (never a bare string, so a typo like "fats" is an error rather than a model id) with exactly one of the two. Precedence: a model chosen for the call, then a tier chosen for the call, then the capability's route; a model beats a tier given for the same call (documented, not an error); tier names always resolve through the tier table, so changing a tier's model moves every capability routed to it, while a capability pinned to an exact model does not move. Anything wrong (unknown tier, capability or field, empty or malformed model id) is an `LlmError` `CONFIG` that says where. Model ids are checked loosely (non-empty, at most 200 characters, no whitespace or control characters) because providers write them very differently. A new boundary test fails if the LLM component reads `process.env`, `process.argv` or files: the app and dev tools read the environment and pass the result in. Configs are frozen and independent of the caller's input object.
- 2026-10-03 — Scripted client and model-client contract (T-040): **`CANCELLED` added as a seventh `LlmError` code** (not retryable) because a cancelled call needed a code and none of the six fit. Shared helpers, exported from `./llm`: `checkRequest` (validates a `ModelRequest`: model id rules, non-empty messages with valid roles and text, positive whole `maxOutputTokens` and `timeoutMs`, optional object schema, a real signal; unknown fields refused so a misspelt setting is not ignored; failures are `CONFIG`) and `runWithDeadline` (time limit plus cancellation for any client: `TIMEOUT` retryable, `CANCELLED`, work that throws becomes `MODEL_ERROR`, an already-cancelled signal never starts the work, no timer or listener left behind). New entry point `./llm/testing` (its own API report; five reports now): `createScriptedModelClient` (plays a script of provider behaviours, records frozen copies of every request, counts only calls that reached the provider, parses JSON when a schema is asked for) and `runModelClientConformance(makeClient, { describe, it })` with 19 cases. A real provider cannot be made to refuse or hang on demand, so each client supplies a factory from a named scenario (text, json, not-json, refusal, rate-limited, server-error, rejected, unknown-model, hangs) to a client whose provider behaves that way. Provider mapping fixed by the contract: prose when JSON was asked for is `BAD_OUTPUT` (not retryable); refusal is `REFUSED`; rate limit is `RATE_LIMITED` (retryable, keeps `retryAfterMs`); server fault is `MODEL_ERROR` retryable; a rejected request is `MODEL_ERROR` not retryable; an unknown model is `CONFIG`. 17 deliberately defective clients are each caught by the case aimed at them, and the one that never answers fails the suite without hanging it (cases use a watchdog). `llm/testing` redefines `TestApi` (three lines) because the boundary rule lets the LLM component use only the graph store's main entry.
- 2026-10-03 — Context builder (T-041): `buildContext(input, options?)` in `src/llm/capabilities/categorise/context.ts` (the first file of the `categorise` capability; internal until `createLlm`), pure and deterministic. Output: a header saying to reuse existing ids and that every line is data, never instructions; one compact JSON object per category (`id`, `links` when known, `data` or `dataTruncated`, and `items` only when asked); an "Allowed operations" line listing exactly the operations passed in (the categoriser allows only `upsertNode` and `link`, `CATEGORISE_OPS`, so deletion is never offered). Ranked most-linked first, ties by id in UTF-16 order, unknown counts last, independent of input order. Budget is in characters (not tokens: a rough, deterministic bound that needs no model-specific tokenizer): the text never exceeds `maxChars` (default 4000), the least-linked entries drop first, and a note says how many ("[N more categories not shown, to stay within the size limit]"); if even the fixed parts and the note do not fit it is a `CONFIG` error that says how many characters are needed. Stored category data came from earlier user input and is untrusted, so each entry is JSON-encoded on its own line and cannot add or alter entries (tested with newlines, fake JSON, fake delimiters and instruction text). Item contents are left out unless `includeItemContents` is set (then up to `maxItemsPerCategory` = 3 per category, by id); data over `maxDataChars` (200) is flagged, never cut mid-way. Categories carry their own example items (the caller supplies them from a query); duplicate category ids are a `CONFIG` error. A real off-by-one in the budget arithmetic (one separator too many) was found by the tests and fixed; a brute-force oracle over ~1,000 budgets now pins "shows exactly as many entries as fit".
- 2026-10-03 — Prompt builder (T-042): `buildPrompt(input, options?)` in `src/llm/capabilities/categorise/prompt.ts`, pure, never throws, all failures `CONFIG`. The system prompt is fixed trusted text and contains no note or category text; it varies only with the note id (checked against `[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9_.:-]{0,127}` because it is placed in the instructions), the operation limit and the allowed operations. The note and category context appear only in `<note>` and `<categories>` blocks of the user message, escaped (`&` first, then `<` and `>`), so neither can close or add a block; an empty or oversized note (default 8000 characters) is refused, never cut. The output schema is derived from the graph store's `mutationJsonSchema()` on every call (so it cannot drift), keeping only `upsertNode` and `link`, dropping `mode` from `upsertNode` (the controller forces merge), and adding `rationale` (max 500) and an op cap. `mutationJsonSchema` and `queryJsonSchema` are now public exports of `graph_store` (additive; API report and spec updated). Exact wording is pinned by golden files in `__snapshots__/`. Provider structured-output may support only a subset of JSON Schema; check when T-047 is built.
- 2026-10-03 — Output guard (T-043): `guardReply(output, { graphId, itemId, requestId? }, options?)` in `src/llm/capabilities/categorise/guard.ts` (internal until `createLlm`), pure, never throws, every rejection is a non-retryable `BAD_OUTPUT` whose message lists up to five numbered problems (so one repair attempt can fix them together). Strict: text must be exactly one JSON object (no prose, no code fences); top level may hold only `ops` and `rationale`; only `upsertNode` and `link`, each with only the fields the model may set; **the model cannot set `mode`, `ensureNodes`, `graphId`, `requestId`, `createIfMissing`, `version` or `kind`** (presence is an error that says the system sets it, rather than being silently overridden); the item upsert and every link must use the controller-minted item id, exactly one item upsert and at least one link are required; weight 0 to 1. The final mutation is built with `createIfMissing: false`, every upsert `mode: 'merge'`, every link `ensureNodes: false`, and checked by the real `parseMutation` under tighter limits (25 ops, 2048 bytes of data, 128-character ids; 32,000-character reply, 500-character rationale). A link to a category that neither exists nor is created in the same reply therefore fails at write time with `NODE_NOT_FOUND`, which the preview will show (no silent node creation). Because of this the T-042 output schema now also omits `ensureNodes` from `link` (golden file updated). Not checked by the guard: whether a linked category exists (the guard is pure and does not see the graph; see Backlog).
- 2026-10-03 — `createLlm` and `categorise()` (T-044): `createLlm({ client, config?, now? })` returns a frozen object with one method per capability (`categorise`); a missing client throws `TypeError` (a coding mistake, like `createGraphClient`), everything after returns a `Result`. `categorise(input, options?)` takes `{ text, graphId, itemId, requestId?, categories }` (the controller supplies ids and reads the categories) and returns `Proposal { mutation, rationale?, usage, model, attempts }`, frozen. Order: validate options and input (including the ids, by running the guard on a made-up good reply, so a bad id fails before the model is paid for), `resolveModel`, `buildContext`, `buildPrompt`, model call, `guardReply`. **One time limit covers both attempts** (the repair gets what is left; none left is `TIMEOUT`; the clock is injectable for tests). A guard rejection triggers exactly one repair: the conversation is the original user message, the rejected reply (cut to 4,000 characters) as an assistant turn, and the guard's numbered reasons; a second rejection is `BAD_OUTPUT` "still not accepted after one repair attempt". Errors from the client (refusal, rate limit, timeout, server fault, prose when JSON was asked for) are returned as they are and never repaired. A client that throws, returns a non-result, or reports invalid token usage is `MODEL_ERROR`. `maxOps` is one option applied to both the prompt and the guard so they cannot disagree. Defaults: 30,000 ms, 4,000 output tokens, 25 operations. Usage is counted across both attempts on success; a failed call returns an error without usage. **Boundary rule relaxed deliberately**: `src/llm/index.ts` and `src/llm/create-llm.ts` (the assembly files) may import capabilities; every other file outside `capabilities/` still may not. New public API: `createLlm`, `Llm`, `LlmInit`, `CategoriseInput`, `CategoriseOptions`, `CategoryEntry`, `ContextOptions`, `Proposal`.
- 2026-10-03 — Application scaffold (T-045): `src/app/` is the third component, with its own entry point `./app` and API report (six reports now). Boundary rules extended and the test **moved to `src/boundary.test.ts`** since it now governs three components: the graph store imports neither the LLM component nor the app; the LLM component does not import the app; the app uses the graph store and the LLM component only through `graph_store/index` and `llm/index`; `src/llm` and `src/app` may not read the environment or files (the controller takes configuration as arguments; dev tools and a future CLI read the environment). Pieces: `Input` union (`text`, `image`, `audio`) with `parseInput` (strict, frozen copy, `INVALID_INPUT`); `AppError` with a closed code set (`INVALID_INPUT`, `UNSUPPORTED_INPUT`, `NORMALISER_FAILED`; T-046 adds its own); `normaliseInput(input, normalisers?)` is the seam: one optional normaliser per kind, text defaults to `normaliseText` (blank is `INVALID_INPUT`), image and audio are `UNSUPPORTED_INPUT` until supplied; a throwing or non-text-returning normaliser is `NORMALISER_FAILED` and its message is not leaked. `createItemIdGenerator`: ids are `<prefix>-<9-digit base-36 ms>-<2-digit counter>-<6 random>` (default prefix `note`, at most 29 characters, lowercase, accepted by the prompt's id rule), monotonic per generator (a counter within a millisecond; after 1,296 in one ms it borrows the next ms; a backwards clock step keeps order), random tail from `crypto.getRandomValues` unless injected; a bad prefix or non-function throws `TypeError`, a clock outside whole milliseconds since 1970 throws `RangeError` (coding mistakes). Image and audio carry bytes (`Uint8Array`) and a media type; size limits are for the normalisers that arrive later.
- 2026-10-03 — T-046 split (stopped at `/next`, per the loop rules): propose, preview and approve is three concerns with different test strategies, so it is now T-046 (pure proposal summary), T-054 (propose: normalise, mint id, read context with `query()`, categorise, hold a pending proposal; nothing written) and T-055 (approve, reject, expiry, double-approval safety, failed-write handling). Ids T-054 and T-055 continue the numbering; the order is T-046, T-054, T-055. The summary's `existing` input and its link-without-category warning also settle the earlier Backlog question about checking linked categories: the check lives in the preview (visible to the person) rather than in the guard.
- 2026-10-03 — Proposal summary (T-046): `summarise(mutation, existing)` and `describeSummary(summary)` in `src/app/summary.ts`, pure, never throw (`INVALID_INPUT` for anything that is not a list of `upsertNode`/`link` operations, so a `deleteNode` or `unlink` is refused rather than summarised). `existing` is `{ items, categories }`: the ids of the mutation's nodes that are already in the graph. Operations are read in write order with a running view of what exists, so a link to a category (or from an item) that neither exists nor is created *earlier* is a **problem** (the real write fails with `NODE_NOT_FOUND`; the controller should refuse to approve it); a link repeated, or a link from an item that already exists (it may replace an existing edge, which `existing` cannot show), is a **note**. Lists are in the order first seen; an upsert of an existing id is "updated", not "new"; "reused" means an existing category that is linked to. Odd ids are quoted with `JSON.stringify` in the text so they cannot inject lines. A randomised test (400 proposals) checks the predictions against the real memory adapter: a problem is reported exactly when `write` fails, and at the same operation. **Boundary rule widened**: the app may also import the memory adapter's public entry point (`graph_store/adapters/memory/index`), which the LLM component still may not; nothing deeper and not the conformance suite. Existing edges are not part of `existing` yet; if the preview needs to say "this link is already there", extend `existing` in T-054.
- 2026-10-03 — Propose (T-054): `createController({ adapter, llm, ids?, proposalIds?, normalisers?, graphOptions?, now?, ttlMs?, maxPending?, maxCategories? })` returns a frozen `{ propose, get }` (bad settings throw `TypeError`). `propose(graphId, input, { categorise? })`: **read the categories first** (cheap, and a note should not be transcribed or sent to a model for a missing graph; GRAPH_NOT_FOUND and bad graph ids surface before anything else), normalise, mint the proposal id (prefix `prop`, also sent as the mutation's `requestId` for tracing) and the item id, `llm.categorise()`, read which of the proposal's ids exist (`query` with named seeds, `ids` shape), `summarise`, hold. Categories are read with `query()` (whole-graph, partition filter, data included, 200 per page) up to `maxCategories` (500) in id order; the model gets no link counts yet, so when more than that exist the shown ones are the first by id, not the most used (Backlog). Errors are `{ source: 'app' | 'graph' | 'llm', error }` so each keeps its own type. A proposal whose summary has problems is still held and returned (the person sees why; the write itself is atomic and would refuse). Pending proposals live in an in-memory store per controller: `expiresAt = createdAt + ttlMs` (default 15 min; expired from that instant), `maxPending` 100 and a full store gives `TOO_MANY_PENDING` instead of dropping someone's proposal; expired proposals are swept on each add. New `AppError` codes `TOO_MANY_PENDING` and `UNEXPECTED`. Whole-graph listings never report `truncated` (verified), so only the `maxCategories` cap marks a read as capped. **Boundary rule tightened**: production app code may import only `graph_store/index` and `llm/index`; app tests and `*.test-util.ts` files may also use the memory adapter and `llm/testing/index`. `*.test-util.ts` files (shared test helpers, e.g. `recording-adapter.test-util.ts`, which records every write primitive called) are excluded from the build and from the purity lint.
- 2026-10-03 — Approve, reject, expire (T-055): `approve(id)` **takes** the proposal out of the store (`take`: one caller gets it) before calling `write(adapter, mutation, graphOptions)`, then returns `{ proposal, written }`; on a failed write it `restore`s the proposal with its original expiry and returns the graph store's error (`source: 'graph'`); if `write` ever threw, the proposal is also restored and the error is `UNEXPECTED` without the message. Two approvals at once: one wins, the other is `PROPOSAL_NOT_FOUND` (checked against a reference run: identical writes to a single approval). `reject(id)` takes it and returns it; neither approve nor reject needs the model. Errors: `PROPOSAL_NOT_FOUND` (unknown, already approved or rejected, or expired and forgotten) and `PROPOSAL_EXPIRED`; the store remembers the last 1,000 expired ids so a proposal reads as expired consistently whether it was swept by an add, dropped by `get`, or tried directly (a bounded FIFO, so memory stays bounded). Expiry is checked when approval starts: a write already running finishes. Reject of an expired proposal reports `PROPOSAL_EXPIRED` although it is gone either way. Approval does not pre-refuse proposals with `problems`: the write is the authority (state may have changed since the preview, in either direction) and is atomic. `restore` may take the store one over `maxPending` rather than lose a proposal. A fault found by the tests: `propose` used to pay for a model call before discovering the store was full; `hasRoom()` now refuses first (a full store costs no tokens and mints no ids). Test helpers shared by the controller tests live in `controller-fixture.test-util.ts`, and the recording adapter also counts transactions. The A1 milestone (controller) is complete.
- 2026-10-03 — Anthropic client (T-047; dependency approved by you): `@anthropic-ai/sdk` ^0.131.0 is a **devDependency and an optional peer dependency** (not a runtime `dependency`), so the graph store keeps one runtime dependency (`zod`) and consumers install the SDK only if they use the new entry point `bipartite-graph/llm/anthropic` (`src/llm/anthropic/`, its own API report; seven reports now). A boundary test proves the SDK is imported only inside `llm/anthropic/` and that `llm/index.ts` cannot reach it. `createAnthropicClient({ apiKey, baseURL?, fetch?, maxRetries? })` returns a frozen `{ complete }`: it checks the request (`checkRequest`), converts the schema, runs the call under `runWithDeadline`, and maps the reply. **Key handling**: the library never reads the environment; `readAnthropicKey(env)` takes an environment object and returns the key or a `CONFIG` error that never echoes it; the key sits in a closure; the SDK is built with an explicit `apiKey`, `authToken: null`, `webhookKey: null`, an explicit base URL and `logLevel: 'off'`, so none of its environment or credential-file lookups apply (tested by setting hostile `ANTHROPIC_*` variables); every error message is written by us from the status class plus the provider's short explanation, with the key and anything shaped like `sk-ant-…` removed and the text cut to 300 characters; the SDK's own message (which carries the raw body) is never used. **Error mapping** (all tested over a fake provider): 429 `RATE_LIMITED` with `retry-after-ms`, or `retry-after` as seconds or an HTTP date; 404 `CONFIG` (unknown model); 401 and 403 `CONFIG`; other 4xx `MODEL_ERROR` not retryable; 5xx including 529 `MODEL_ERROR` retryable; network failure `MODEL_ERROR` retryable; refusal `stop_reason` `REFUSED` with the category; `max_tokens` with a schema `BAD_OUTPUT` saying the JSON was cut off; prose when JSON was asked `BAD_OUTPUT`. Usage counts cache-creation and cache-read tokens as input. SDK retries default to 0. The client passes the 19-case model-client contract suite over the fake provider. **Provider schema subset (found while testing)**: providers that enforce a schema need every object closed and accept only part of JSON Schema, so `toProviderSchema` keeps types, properties, required, items, `anyOf` (from `oneOf`), `$defs`/`$ref`, descriptions, a few string formats and `minItems` 0 or 1, and moves everything else (`maxItems`, `maxLength`, `const`, `enum`, ranges) into the field's `description`. This exposed a real defect in the categoriser's schema: its free-form `data` would have become an object that may hold no fields at all. T-042's schema now gives each node kind a closed `data` shape (item: `title` and `summary`, both required; category: `name`) as two `upsertNode` variants, drops `data` from `link`, and omits unused `$defs`; the guard no longer allows `data` on a link. The golden schema file changed (reviewed). Pairing a partition with its data shape cannot be enforced by the provider (a `const` becomes text), so the guard stays the authority. `npm run llm:try` (script `scripts/llm-try.mjs`, outside the gate) sends one sample note to the real API; I have not run it (no key, and it spends money and sends text out), so **the real provider has not been exercised yet**: please run it once with your key and tell me what it prints.
- 2026-10-03 — Shared dev-server kit (T-053): `dev/shared/server-kit.mjs` holds everything the local tools need to be safe, once: `createLoopbackServer({ files, publicDir, api, maxBodyBytes })` (binds `127.0.0.1` only; answers only `127.0.0.1:<port>` or `localhost:<port>` as Host, so a missing or look-alike Host is 403 (DNS rebinding); for any `/api/` request that is not GET, refuses a cross-origin `Origin` (403) and a non-`application/json` post (415); serves only the listed files, to GET only; JSON bodies capped in bytes (413) and bad JSON is 400; `HttpError` reaches the browser with its status, anything else is a 500; every reply is `no-store` and `nosniff`), plus `assertLocalDevelopment(tool, env)`, `send`, `readJson` and `HttpError`. The graph explorer is now only its routes on top of the kit; its tests pass unchanged, and a manual start confirmed the page, the Host refusal, a write and the production refusal. The "never published" test moved to `dev/not-published.test.mjs` and now covers every tool: nothing from `dev/` in the package, no library code importing dev code, no dev source naming a wider address (`0.0.0.0`, `::`), and **only the shared kit may import the network modules** (so the LLM lab cannot start a server of its own with weaker checks). The kit has 55 tests of its own (path-traversal and prototype-key attempts, look-alike hosts and origins, byte-exact body limit, a request with no Host sent over a raw socket); 28 deliberate defects in the kit were each caught (three needed new tests: loose Host matching, loose content-type matching, and `/api` prefix matching).
- 2026-10-04 — T-052 split (stopped at `/next`, per the loop rules): the LLM lab needs the prompt, raw output, guard verdicts and repair step of a `categorise` call, which the component does not expose, so it is now three tasks in order: T-052 (a `trace` callback on `categorise`, plus the guard's problems as a list; a small, general observability feature that T-048's evaluation harness can reuse), T-056 (the lab's server: run, compare, status, history, scripted scenarios, the real model only with a key and an explicit switch) and T-057 (the page). Ids T-056 and T-057 continue the numbering. Reason for building the trace into the library rather than having the lab re-implement the pipeline: a copy could drift from what `categorise` really does, which is exactly what a lab must not show.
- 2026-10-04 — Trace for `categorise` (T-052): `CategoriseOptions.trace` is called with `CategoriseEvent`s: `prompt`, then per attempt `request` (attempt 1 or 2, model, time left, the messages sent), `response` (raw output, tokens, answering model, elapsed ms) or `failure` (error, elapsed ms), `verdict` (accepted, and the complete list of problems), `repair` (the feedback), and `done` (always last, once per call, even for a call refused before anything was built: `ok`, `attempts` started, total elapsed, and the `error` when it failed). Events are `structuredClone`d and frozen before the callback sees them, so a callback that edits one cannot change the real request or result; a callback that throws, or returns a rejected promise, is ignored (the rejection is handled so it cannot become an unhandled rejection); an event that cannot be copied is dropped rather than breaking the call. Passing a `trace` that is not a function is a `CONFIG` error. The guard gained `inspectReply` (returns the whole problem list; `guardReply` is now `inspectReply` plus the five-problem message, and `rejectionError` is exported), which is how `verdict` can list every failing check instead of the first five. With no callback nothing changes: the 786 existing tests in `src/llm` and `src/app` pass unmodified. `categorise` was split into a thin wrapper (timing, `done`) and an inner `run`. Lesson for my own mutation runs: a mutant that is a syntax error crashes whole test files and shows as fewer tests rather than as failures; the helper now compares file counts too.
- 2026-10-04 — LLM lab server (T-056): `dev/llm-lab/` (`app.mjs`, `scenarios.mjs`, `server.mjs`, `public/index.html` as a placeholder until T-057, `README.md`), started with `npm run dev:lab` (port 4318, `LLM_LAB_PORT`), built on the shared kit. Routes: `GET /api/status` (capabilities, tiers and their models, routes, scenarios with descriptions, limits, whether a real call is possible), `POST /api/run`, `POST /api/compare` (1 to 6 models, in turn), `GET`/`DELETE /api/history` (last 50, newest first, each with its full trace). A run's record holds the trace, the proposal with its summary (`summarise` against the categories the request supplied, so reused/new categories are shown) and plain text, tokens (also for failed runs, from the last response), latency, the model actually asked and how the request chose it. Only a badly shaped request is an HTTP error (400); anything the component reports, including CONFIG errors for a bad note or category, is data in the record. Strict bodies (unknown fields refused, text up to 20,000 characters, up to 200 categories, time limit 100 ms to 120 s). **Ten scripted scenarios** (good, good-new-category, repaired, rejected-twice, link-missing-category, prose, refusal, rate-limited, server-error, hangs) so every path through `categorise` is visible for free; each run gets a fresh scripted model. **Real model gate**: used only when the key was in the environment at start-up (`server.mjs` reads it once with `readAnthropicKey`) AND the request carries `network: true`; `network: true` without a key is 409 and creates no client; a scenario with `network: true` is 400; the key is held in the app's closure, is never in `/api/status`, and every record is scrubbed of it before it is stored or sent, as a second line of defence behind the client's own redaction (a test has the provider echo the key back). The real client is created per run, by an injectable factory in tests so no test touches the network. The lab sends the note and category names to Anthropic in network mode; the README says so. 59 tests; 33 deliberate defects were each caught (one needed a new assertion on the default graph id, and one of my mutants crashed the file instead of testing sharing, so I redid it). Smoke-tested on the built library with curl, including the refusal of a real call without a key and of `NODE_ENV=production`.
- 2026-10-04 — LLM lab page (T-057): `dev/llm-lab/public/` is `index.html`, `style.css`, `app.js` and `view.js`, all served from an explicit allow-list (app.mjs `FILES`). `view.js` is DOM-free and unit-tested on real records produced by the real server code with the scripted model (formatting, `statusOf`, `attemptsOf` which folds the trace into attempts with the repair feedback attached to the next one, `promptOf`, `compareRows`, `historyLabel`, `parseCategories`, `choiceFrom`, `compareChoices`, `buildRequest`, `networkSwitch`). `app.js` builds the DOM with an `el()` helper that only ever sets `textContent`/attributes; **nothing the model wrote is ever parsed as HTML**, and a test fails if either script uses `innerHTML`, `outerHTML`, `insertAdjacentHTML`, `document.write`, `eval`, `new Function`, string timers, `srcdoc` or `DOMParser`, if the page references anything off-origin or imports a stylesheet, or if `app.js` looks up an element id that `index.html` lacks. Panels: proposal (reason, plain summary, operations), attempts (asked/limit/took/answered-by/tokens, raw output, accepted or numbered problems, repair feedback), prompt (system, user message, schema), plus facts, compare table with a detail view, and history. The "real model" switch is unchecked and disabled with the reason when the server has no key; ticking it disables the scenario picker and shows a warning that the note and category names go to Anthropic and cost money. **Hand check in Chrome** (127.0.0.1, scripted, no key): the page loads with no console errors; Run on `repaired` showed an accepted-after-repair proposal, both attempts (the first rejected with its raw output) and the history entry; Compare over fast and balanced produced two rows and a detail; an empty note and a bad categories list showed their inline errors; a note, category id and category name containing `<img onerror>`, `<script>` and `</note>` rendered as plain text (no elements created, nothing executed) and the prompt panel showed the note escaped; the network switch was disabled with its reason. Not verified by eye: the single-column layout below 1150 px (the browser would not narrow the viewport), the dark theme, and the real-model path. One cosmetic fix came out of the check (run ids wrapped in the compare table). 118 lab tests; 35 deliberate defects in `view.js` were each caught (two edge cases needed hand-built traces). Screenshots from the check were saved to the Chrome screenshots folder.
- 2026-10-04 — **Scope decision (owner): the proof of concept stays on Anthropic models only.** TypeSafe's Jev (a typed-decision classifier) was assessed as a good fit for matching a note to existing categories but not for creating categories or writing titles, and not a `ModelClient`; the owner chose to defer it as a later optimisation for speed and cost, to keep development simple. It is recorded in the PLAN Backlog with the API facts, the proposed hybrid (Jev first, frontier on escalation) and the open points, so it can be picked up without redoing the research. No code was written for it and no Jev dependency or key is needed now.
- 2026-10-04 — **Scope decision (owner): the proof of concept holds only the owner's own data**, so sending notes to Anthropic is accepted for now and pseudonymisation is deferred to the Backlog (a privacy milestone with the design, the decisions and a test idea recorded there). This resolves the earlier privacy blocker *for the PoC only*: it must be reopened before any other person's data is used, before the tool is offered to others, and before it is described as privacy-safe. Do not claim in docs or UI that notes are anonymised or protected from the model provider. The lab already warns when a real call sends the note and category names to Anthropic.
- 2026-10-05 — Evaluation harness (T-048): `dev/eval/` (never published; `npm run eval`, built on the dist like `llm:try` and the lab). **Golden set**: 15 invented notes (`golden.mjs`, frozen) with loose expectations: `reuse` / `avoidReuse` (existing category ids), `mustNotCreate` (words; a new category fails if any whole word of its id matches, so `doctor` catches `doctor-visits` but `gp` does not catch `shopping`), `newCategories` and `links` ranges; every case also needs the call to succeed and the preview (`summarise`) to show no problems. Coverage: plain reuse, multi-topic, nothing fits, empty graph, 60 categories, very short, rambling, French, a prompt-injection note, a near-duplicate trap. `validateGolden` checks a set is sound (unique ids, expectations name real categories, ranges consistent), which also guards user-supplied sets (`--golden`). **Pieces**: `evaluate.mjs` (pure checks, every check reported pass or fail), `runner.mjs` (case by case, model by model, one call at a time through the real `createLlm`/`categorise` with the T-052 trace; records checks, tokens (also for failed calls, from the trace), latency, attempts, repair flag, answering model, the proposal), `report.mjs` (per-model pass rate, answered, repairs, latency mean/median/p95/max, tokens, failed-check counts, errors by code; a case-by-model grid; failure reasons capped at 20), `args.mjs`, `cli.mjs` (everything injected, so it is tested without a model, key, disk or clock), `run.mjs`. Tokens are reported, not prices (prices change). **Deviation from the plan's wording, deliberate**: a real run prints its plan and stops unless `--yes` is given, because it costs money and sends the notes and category names to Anthropic; `--scripted` (a scripted ideal model) checks the plumbing for free, and `--scripted --yes` is refused. Results are saved as JSON in `eval-results/` (git-ignored); the key is read once from `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` by `run.mjs`, handed to the client, and never printed or saved (tested on every path). Ctrl+C stops between calls and still reports and saves what was done. **What the tests prove about the set itself**: an ideal scripted model passes all 15 cases through the real `categorise` (so every case is satisfiable), while a model that always links the first category, one that invents a duplicate category, and one that links to everything each fail many (so the checks discriminate). 126 tests; 65 deliberate defects in the harness were each caught (one needed new tests for the ideal-reply builder, one redundant condition was removed). **Not done, needs you**: the real comparison. Nothing has been sent to a real model; run `source ./set-key.sh && npm run eval -- --models fast,balanced,deep` to see the plan, then add `--yes` (about 45 calls) and tell me the report, so the provisional default tier for `categorise` can be decided on evidence.
- 2026-10-05 — **First real evaluation (owner ran it; `eval-results/2026-10-05T09-42-43-099Z-real.json`, git-ignored)**: 15 cases × fast/balanced/deep × 1 run = 45 real calls. Pass rate fast 100%, balanced 100%, deep 93% (one failure: deep made 3 new categories on `empty-graph` against a cap of 2, arguably reasonable, so partly my arbitrary limit). Latency median/p95: fast 2.17 s / 6.20 s, balanced 2.77 s / 4.70 s, deep 3.85 s / 6.74 s. Tokens per run: fast 1,835, balanced 2,285, deep 2,290 (fast's input is about 18% lower for the same prompts, so it tokenises differently). **Zero repairs, zero errors, all 45 first-attempt**, and all three resisted the prompt-injection note. **This is the first exercise of the real Anthropic client, and it passed: the converted schema with closed `data` shapes is accepted by the real API, usage and the answering model come back as expected, and the model ids in `DEFAULT_TIERS` are valid.** Decision: `fast` stays the default for `categorise` (level on quality, quickest, cheapest on this set); the "provisional" wording on the defaults was updated to say so. Caveats, stated honestly: one run per model; the set is invented, easy and written before the results; fast and balanced both hit 100% so it cannot separate them; the checks say what is *allowed*, not what is *good* (fast filed a photography club under `ideas` and an insurance renewal under a new `car-maintenance` category, both passing). Follow-ups are in the Backlog. Blocker "real provider unverified" is closed.
- 2026-10-05 — Capture panel in the explorer (T-049): the explorer gains **Capture a note** (propose, see it drawn, approve or reject). **Server** (`dev/graph-explorer/app.mjs`): `createApp(lib, { capture })`; without `capture` the routes are 404 and the rest is unchanged. Routes `GET /api/capture/status`, `POST /api/capture/propose` (`graphId`, `text`, optional `network`), `/approve` and `/reject` (`id`), strict bodies (unknown fields, wrong types and text over 20,000 characters are 400). It builds the real **controller** (`createController`) over the explorer's adapter, one for the demo model and one for the real model; a map records which controller holds which proposal; both are rebuilt and the map cleared on reset. Controller errors keep their `source` (`app`, `graph`, `llm`) and are flattened for the page. A failed write leaves the proposal pending (retry or reject); proposals the controller reports gone or expired are dropped from the map at once. **Real model gate**: used only when the server was started with `--real-model` AND `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` was found (read once by `server.mjs`), and then only for a request with `network: true`; otherwise 409 and nothing is created; the key is held in the app and never in a response (tested on every path). **Demo model** (`capture-model.mjs`): a `ModelClient` built on the scripted client that reads the real prompt (note id, escaped note, category lines) and answers by word overlap with category ids and names (top two, weights 0.9 and 0.6, ties by link count then id), or a new category from the most common word (capped at 30 characters), or `inbox`; a free stand-in, not a classifier. Testing it found a real bug (a note that is one huge word overflowed the reason and name limits), now capped. **Page**: `public/capture.js` (pure: `applyGhosts` lays the proposal over the real graph for drawing only; `proposalHeadline`, `describeFailure`, `realModelSwitch`) and the panel in `app.js`/`index.html`. Ghosts are merged only into what is laid out and drawn; counts, lists, diffs and the selection use the real data, so nothing proposed leaks into the graph's state. Styles: dashed proposed nodes and links, ringed reused categories. Long minted ids are shortened on the graph (`shortLabel`: start and end kept, full id in the tooltip) after the hand check showed them clipped. **Hand check in Chrome** (scripted demo model, seeded graph): the panel showed with the real-model switch disabled and its reason; a note matching two categories drew a dashed item with dashed links to two ringed categories, the plain summary and reason, real counts unchanged and "Nothing is written until you approve"; Approve wrote exactly 3 operations (counts 3 items, 3 categories, 4 links; dashes gone; panel closed; nothing pending); a note fitting nothing drew a dashed item and a dashed new category, and Reject left the server and page unchanged with "Rejected. Nothing was written."; a hostile note (`<img onerror>`, `<script>`, `</note>`) stayed inert text; no console errors. Not checked by eye: the real-model path and the dark theme. 107 capture tests and 5 label tests; about 70 deliberate defects across the server routes, the demo model, `capture.js` and `shortLabel` were each caught (five needed new tests, among them reject forgetting the proposal immediately and expiry through a `controllerOptions` passthrough used only by tests). A guard test now checks the explorer's scripts never build HTML from text, every element id the script uses exists, and nothing is loaded from the network.
- 2026-10-05 — End-to-end test and docs (T-050): `src/app/capture-flow.test.ts` runs text through the real controller, a scripted model that files by keyword by reading the real prompt, the preview, approve and the real memory store, and reads the final graph back through the public `query()` (a sorted whole-graph snapshot) with a consistency check (every link joins an item and a category that exist). Success paths: nothing is written until approval (a recording adapter shows no write primitive and the snapshot is unchanged), then exactly the previewed items, categories and weighted links; a second note reuses the category the first created (the prompt shows it); five notes build a graph; existing data is kept; approving twice writes once; a hostile note produces only allowed operations. Failure paths, each asserting the snapshot equals the one before and no write was made: the model answers badly twice (BAD_OUTPUT after one repair; 3 model calls in total), refuses, is rate limited, fails, answers in prose, never answers (TIMEOUT); a blank note, a picture and voice are refused before the model is asked; a graph that does not exist; a rejected proposal; an expired one; the graph dropped between preview and approval (then the same proposal succeeds once the graph is back); a link to a missing category (the preview warns, approving writes none of it, the proposal stays); and the store failing part-way through a write (the item and category put first are rolled back). 23 tests. To check the test earns its place, the real code was broken in seven places and each break was caught here: approve skipping the write, approve not taking the proposal (double approval), a failed write losing the proposal, reject not discarding, the controller showing the model no categories (two ways), the preview no longer reporting missing categories, and the memory store keeping a half-written transaction. Two of my own expectations were wrong at first and were corrected (link counts are not yet passed to the model; a title is cut at 30 characters). README gains **Capturing text**: the propose/preview/approve flow, a code example, the dev tools that try it, a privacy section stating what is sent (the note and the existing categories, to Anthropic) and that **nothing is anonymised or pseudonymised and the proof of concept is for the owner's own data**, a table of where real calls happen, and what is not done yet. The A1b milestone, and the whole PoC capture path, is complete.
- 2026-10-05 — **Persistence planned (P1), owner decisions**: SQLite first, through **`node:sqlite`** (built into Node, no new dependency; experimental, needs Node 22.13 or later; kept behind one internal wrapper so it can be swapped); the **file (JSON) adapter is deferred**, not removed. Facts verified on Node 22.19 (SQLite 3.50.4): JSON functions work; **there is no custom collation, and SQLite's default text order differs from the UTF-16 order the contract requires** (`😀` sorts last in SQLite, before `U+E000` in JavaScript), so ids are stored as **UTF-16 big-endian blobs**, whose byte order equals JavaScript's `<` (verified); **text storage corrupts a lone surrogate** (it comes back as U+FFFD), so the blob is also the identity and ids decode from it exactly; at most 32,766 variables per statement, so `IN` lists are batched; foreign keys are on by default and must not link edges to nodes (adapters stay dumb). Design points in the plan: one connection with all transactions serialised (`node:sqlite` is synchronous and the transaction callback is asynchronous, so interleaving would corrupt isolation), `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, WAL, `user_version` migrations, atomic `create` (T-059, settles two Backlog races), core paging-loop guards (T-060, settles a hardening item), conformance on `:memory:` and on files, kill-9 and two-connection tests, owner-only file mode. Ten tasks, T-058 to T-067: spec, atomic create, loop guards, foundation, adapter, durability, capture on SQLite, explorer `--db`, NFR-05 benchmark (the three-clause set query waits for M4b), and optional export/import (FR-16, AC-11 re-based on memory and SQLite). Backlog triage: promoted two items to T-059 and T-060; three items are measured by T-066 and decided on evidence; added data-at-rest, `node:sqlite` experimental status, push-down timing, and link counts. A pending proposal is still held in memory, by design, and is lost on restart.
