Recorded with what exists today, dependencies and open questions in the PLAN Backlog. Two things to remember when they are planned: "support circle" is undefined in the repo and must be specified first, and circles are where other people's data enters, which turns the pseudonymisation milestone and the legal questions into prerequisites and ends the "owner's own data only" proof-of-concept scope. P1 (SQLite persistence) remains the next work.
- 2026-10-05 — Spec update for SQLite-first persistence (T-058, documentation only). **R-001 spec**: the overview, goal 4, the entry-point list and the adapter table now say memory and SQLite, with the **file (JSON) adapter deferred** (kept as an idea; its concurrency risk paragraph is kept for when it is revived); a new subsection *SQLite adapter design decisions* records each decision with its reason (driver `node:sqlite`, UTF-16 big-endian blob ids and why, one connection with serialised transactions, adapters stay dumb with no foreign keys to nodes, `user_version` versioning, atomic create planned as T-059, push-down waits for M4b, data at rest unencrypted with mode 0600, one process at a time); NFR-01 notes Node 22.13 or later for the SQLite adapter, NFR-03 notes `node:sqlite` adds no dependency, NFR-05 says the three-clause set query is measured after M4b; **AC-11 is re-based** on memory and SQLite (both directions) with a note saying why; the phase list has SQLite as phase 5 (exit: conformance on memory and file databases, AC-01, AC-02 and AC-15 on SQLite, durability shown, benchmark) and the file adapter as phase 6 deferred, export/import (phase 7) may come earlier as the backup path. **spec.md**: build order, a Persistence line, the SQLite row of the architecture table, and the milestones section, which was badly out of date (it still said M4a was next), now lists what is done, P1 as next, and the later items including the Backlog roadmap. **README**: the stale status line (it still said M0 to M2) is corrected. **Not changed, for the owner to decide**: the Constitution (`system_prompt.md`, line 45) says to add the conformance suite to the gate "once adapters exist (M5 onward)"; the suite has been in the gate since M3 and M5 is deferred, so that wording is merely out of date, and I do not edit the Constitution unasked. Suggested replacement: "(from the SQLite adapter onward, it runs on every adapter)".
- 2026-10-05 — Atomic graph creation (T-059): the adapter contract changed: `graphs.create(id)` now returns `Promise<boolean>`, `true` if this call created the graph and `false` if it already existed (left untouched); of simultaneous creates of one id exactly one returns `true`. **Core**: `createGraph` makes one atomic `create` call and is `CONFLICT` exactly when it returns `false` (no separate `exists` check, so the check-then-create race is gone); the pure `planCreateGraph(graphId, exists)` became `confirmCreated(graphId, answer)`, where an answer that is not a boolean is a `STORAGE_ERROR` ("did not say whether it created the graph") rather than a guess; `write` with `createIfMissing` makes one atomic `create` call and takes `created` from its answer (so a failed call removes only a graph it really made), without a prior `exists`; without `createIfMissing` it only checks `exists` and is `GRAPH_NOT_FOUND` (AC-02). The pure `planGraphResolution` now returns `{ graphId, strategy: 'create-if-missing' | 'require-existing' }` (it no longer needs to know whether the graph exists). **Adapters**: the memory adapter and the independent fixture adapter return the boolean (both serialised per id). **Conformance**: four new lifecycle cases (true then false then true after a drop; 8 simultaneous `graphs.create` give exactly one `true` and all answers are booleans; 8 simultaneous `createGraph` give one success and seven `CONFLICT`; 6 simultaneous creating `write`s give exactly one `graphCreated` and lose no data), and five deliberately broken adapters (always true, always false, check-then-create, silent, text instead of boolean), each shown to be caught by a creation case (the non-atomic one only by the three simultaneous cases). API report re-baselined (one line: the return type); README adapter guide and the R-001 spec updated. 48 existing tests were touched only where they spoke the old contract (test wrappers that returned nothing; the old "no create call for an existing graph" expectation became "one atomic call, no exists check"). Mutation-checked: 15 deliberate defects across `graphs.ts`, `write-plan.ts`, the memory adapter and the fixture adapter were each caught (two of my first mutants were syntax errors that crashed whole files, so I redid them properly). Narrows the Backlog `createIfMissing` limitation; the remaining part (a concurrent writer that sees a graph a failed call then removes) stays there.
- 2026-10-05 — Paging-loop guards (T-060): the core's remaining unguarded loops that trusted an adapter's cursor (`describeGraph`'s counting, which has two nested loops, and the `deleteNode` edge cascade) could run for ever on a broken cursor, and the query executor's own guard only caught a cursor equal to the one just before it, not a cycle (page A, B, A, B...). One shared walker now does all of it: `src/graph_store/paging.ts` `walkPages(fetch, what, limit)`, an async generator that hands over each page and then throws if the adapter's next cursor is the same as the current one ("did not advance while listing ...") or is one it already gave in this walk ("went back to an earlier page while listing ..."), naming the listing (for example `edges of item "i1"`). It is used by the executor (replacing its local copy; `READ_PAGE` moved there), `describeGraph` and the delete cascade. The throw becomes `STORAGE_ERROR` through each caller's existing shell, and inside `write` it happens in the transaction so the whole mutation is rolled back (tested: the item and every edge it had are still there). A page is handed over before its cursor is checked, so the delete cascade deletes the page it has read. Set of seen cursors is per walk (one string per page, a few thousand at most for a large graph). Tests (`paging.test.ts`, 26): `walkPages` itself; `describeGraph` and the delete cascade (for items and for categories) against four broken adapters (a `listNodes` or `edgesOf` cursor that never advances, or one that repeats a page); queries; honest long listings (2,700 items, a node with 2,500 edges over three pages) still work; and the whole conformance suite run against each of the four broken adapters finishes and fails (it does not hang). 10 deliberate defects were each caught, and six of those show the point of the task: without the guard the run spins for ever (a loop of already-resolved promises never lets a timer fire, so a test timeout cannot save it), so they were run under a hard 40-second kill. Lesson for future mutation runs that can hang: use worker threads (`--pool=threads`) under the alarm, because a killed main process leaves its fork workers spinning at full CPU. The README adapter guide and the R-001 spec (code layout and test map) are updated. Settles the Backlog hardening item.
- 2026-10-05 — SQLite foundation (T-061): internal modules in `src/graph_store/adapters/sqlite/` — `db.ts` (the only `node:sqlite` importer, enforced by a scan test; `DbError` codes; `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`; statement cache of at most 128; WAL only after the schema check), `schema.ts` (v1 migration, `user_version` plus `application_id` = "CaCi"; refuses foreign, newer or incomplete files untouched; failed migration rolls back), `keys.ts` (UTF-16BE blob keys, cursors). **Found by the tests: `node:sqlite` binds a zero-length byte array as NULL**, so the empty id would have become a NULL key; every key therefore starts with one fixed byte `0x01` (order unchanged) and the `db.ts` wrapper throws on any bare empty byte array. Mutation-checked; surviving mutants are equivalent (foreign keys are on by default in `node:sqlite`; WAL on `:memory:` is a no-op; the modulo-4 cursor check is subsumed by the re-encode check).
- 2026-10-05 — SQLite adapter (T-062): `createSqliteAdapter({ path?, busyTimeoutMs? })` in `src/graph_store/adapters/sqlite/` (`sqlite-adapter.ts`, `tx.ts`), new entry point `bipartite-graph/adapters/sqlite` (eighth API report; also exports `DbError`). **It passed the whole shared conformance suite on `:memory:` first time (95 tests with its own specific ones)**, so the contract and the memory/fixture adapters agree. Every operation, including `graphs.*`, goes through one queue (the driver is synchronous, the transaction callback is not); consequence: a transaction callback must not call `graphs.*` on its own adapter (it would wait for itself; the core never does). Transactions: `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, graph-exists check inside it (missing graph rejects, creates nothing), rollback if the callback throws even after awaiting, handle refused after the transaction ends. `close()` waits for queued work, is idempotent, and calls made after it fail with `DbError` `CLOSED` (decided at call time). Paging fetches `limit + 1` rows so an exactly-full page has no cursor; a limit that is not a positive whole number, or a cursor the adapter did not give out, is a `RangeError` (as the memory adapter does for limits). Graph ids are compared as plain text, which equals the contract order only because the core restricts them to ASCII. 21 deliberate defects were caught (one survivor, aliasing of repeated ids in `getNodes`, led to a new test). `capabilities`: transactions true, idempotency false, nativeSetQueries false.
- 2026-10-05 — T-063 split (stopped at `/next`, per the loop rules): it mixed single-file durability with multi-connection concurrency and the docs. T-063 is now files and durability (conformance on files, reopen, rollback, kill -9, file mode, error cases, no leftover `-wal`/`-shm`, git-ignore); new T-068 is two connections on one file (visibility, 100 concurrent updates, busy timeout error) plus the README Persistence section. Order: T-063, T-068, T-064.
- 2026-10-05 — Files and durability (T-063): the full conformance suite also passes on a real database file per test (temp directory removed after each). **A new database file is created owner-only (mode 0600)** by `db.ts` before SQLite opens it (`open` with `wx`; an existing file is never touched, and keeps whatever mode its owner gave it); SQLite gives `-wal` and `-shm` the same mode; ignored on Windows. `.gitignore` gained `data/`, `*.db`, `*.db-wal`, `*.db-shm`, `*.db-journal`. Tested: identical data and order after close and reopen (lone surrogates, astral ids, nested data, weight 0, two graphs); no leftover `-wal`/`-shm`/journal after a clean close (while open the log really exists), the file header records write-ahead mode; a thrown or failed (`NODE_NOT_FOUND`) transaction leaves nothing after reopening; **kill -9**: a child process (the real adapter, run from TypeScript source through `ts-loader.mjs`, a tiny resolve hook, and `kill-child.mjs`; both are test support, not published) writes 2,000 nodes and edges inside an open transaction, prints `ready` and is SIGKILLed; the file then opens, holds only the earlier committed node, lists its graph, and accepts new writes (a companion run that finishes proves the child really writes 2,001 nodes). Error cases at the adapter level (missing directory names the path and creates nothing, a directory, a non-database file, another application's SQLite file, a newer schema), the last three proved byte-for-byte unchanged with no leftover files. Mutation-checked: 11 defects, 7 caught; the survivors are equivalent or out of reach here — the `EEXIST` race branch (needs two processes creating at once), `synchronous` (only visible after power loss; the pragma value is asserted in `db.test.ts`), `BEGIN` versus `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` (only matters with a second connection; T-068 tests it), and the creation-error wrapper (SQLite then fails with the same code). Not a durability claim against power loss on every disk: WAL with `synchronous = NORMAL` can lose the last transactions on power failure (never corrupt the file); recorded for the README in T-068.
- 2026-10-05 — Two connections and Persistence docs (T-068): new `DbError` code **`BUSY`** (public, API report changed by one line) for a lock that outlived `busyTimeoutMs`, with a message naming the file and the wait; it is thrown by `begin`, `commit`, `run`, `get`, `all` and `exec`, and `write()` reports it as `STORAGE_ERROR` containing that message. **Found while designing the test: `node:sqlite` is synchronous, so a second connection in the SAME process waiting for a lock blocks the whole event loop** (the holder cannot make progress meanwhile); the cross-process case works because the waiting process only blocks itself. Hence real concurrency is tested with child processes (`rmw-child.mjs`): 50 + 50 updates from two processes, and 50 more from the test process at the same time (150 in total), lose none; in-process tests cover visibility between two adapters (both directions, graph create and drop included, an uncommitted or rolled-back transaction never visible) and the BUSY error (after the timeout, via `write()`, and from `graphs.create`; nothing changed, and the waiter works once the lock is free). The README says: one process at a time is the intended use, do not hold transactions open across awaits on two adapters of one file in the same process. README **Persistence** section added (usage, Node 22.13 and the experimental warning, where the file lives and its mode, refusals, crash and power-cut behaviour, concurrency, backup, **unencrypted at rest**, local file systems only).
- 2026-10-05 — Capture flow on SQLite (T-064): `src/app/capture-flow.test.ts` now runs every one of its 23 cases twice, on the memory adapter and on a fresh SQLite file per test, with identical assertions (including every failure path leaving the graph unchanged and the part-way write rolling back inside real SQLite), plus four cases only a file can have: three approved notes are all there after `close()` and reopening (and the graph is consistent); a new controller on the reopened file shows the model the earlier category and reuses it (its new item ids use another prefix, since the id generator restarts per controller); a proposal pending at close is gone (`PROPOSAL_NOT_FOUND`, nothing written); and a write that failed part-way leaves nothing in the file after reopening. The memory expectations are unchanged. **Boundary rule widened**: app tests (not production app code, not the LLM component) may import `graph_store/adapters/sqlite/index`; the boundary test pins that and refuses production use, internals (`db.js`) and LLM use. **Real bug found by the full-gate run: `openDb` read the file header before setting `busy_timeout`**, so a process opening a database while another process held a lock failed at once with `OPEN_FAILED` instead of waiting (the two-process counter test failed once under load); the timeout is now set first, and the switch to write-ahead mode is guarded like the other statements (8 repeats of the sharing tests and 3 full gates passed afterwards; the race is not deterministic, so there is no dedicated regression test). Mutation-checked: 6 defects in the adapter through the flow; 3 caught (commit on error, no commit, wrong order); the 3 survivors (edge replace, graph create replace, journal mode) are not exercised by the flow and are caught by the shared conformance suite and the file tests.
- 2026-10-05 — Explorer on SQLite (T-065): `npm run dev:explorer -- --db <file>` uses the SQLite adapter; without the flag nothing changes (memory). New `dev/graph-explorer/storage.mjs` (`dbPathFrom`, `openStorage`, testable without the build): a missing `--db` value, a bad path, a non-database file or a newer schema print a clear message naming the path and `server.mjs` exits 1 before any server exists; a missing folder is created owner-only (0700) and the start-up note says so, along with the file, that the data persists, and that it is **not encrypted**. `createApp` takes `adapter` and `storage` options; new `GET /api/storage`; the page (`public/storage.js`, pure and tested) shows `saved to <file>` in the header, and **Reset everything** becomes *Delete all graphs in the file* with a second click saying *Click again to DELETE every graph in the file* and a tooltip naming the file. **Reset is now the same on both stores: drop every graph** through the public `dropGraph` (a file cannot be swapped for a new store), reporting the first failure instead of claiming success; controllers and pending proposals are cleared. Ctrl+C closes the database cleanly (no `-wal`/`-shm` left). Tests (`storage.test.mjs`, 20): argument parsing, memory default, file, folder creation and mode, bad paths, non-database file untouched, data surviving a stop and start, a capture approved before the restart still there, an unapproved proposal gone and writing nothing, reset emptying the file (including more than one page of graphs) and a failing drop reported, the route, the labels; 11 deliberate defects caught after one gap (a failing drop being ignored) got a test. **Hand check in Chrome** on a real file made by the server: header shows the file and the delete button; a captured note was proposed (dashed) and approved; the server was stopped with Ctrl+C and restarted on the same file; after reload the item, category and link were still there; two clicks on the delete button emptied the graph; no console errors; a bad path (`/etc/hosts/x.db`) printed the message and exited 1; the file and folder were owner-only. Not checked by eye: the dark theme.
- 2026-10-05 — **User accounts planned (U1, T-069 to T-083), owner decisions**: password plus server-side sessions (opaque 256-bit token in an HttpOnly cookie, only its SHA-256 stored, so revocable and not replayable from a copy of the database); **open self-registration** (the owner's choice over admin-created accounts, so throttling, a configuration switch and enumeration-safe errors are part of the plan); **one graph per user**, id derived from the user id and never taken from a client; a new component `src/users/` (logic over SQLite, no HTTP) and a thin API on `node:http` in `src/api/`; no new dependency (`node:crypto` scrypt). Confirmed by the owner on 2026-10-06 (T-069): the first account ever created is the admin, roles are only `user` and `admin`, user details are username, display name and an optional unverified email, there is no emailed reset (an admin resets passwords, with a server-side recovery command if the only admin is locked out), registration can be switched off. This **changes spec section 4**, which says the host application owns auth (T-069 rewrites it so the user controller is optional). T-070 first moves the SQLite driver wrapper to a shared `src/sqlite/` so the users component does not reach into the graph store. The API server must not be loopback-only in production (the Pi), so it defaults to 127.0.0.1 and refuses non-loopback with insecure cookies unless told. Order: finish P1 (T-066, T-067 optional), then U1. The capture controller is not yet bound to a session; that is the CaCi controller and PWA work, which U1 prepares through `graphIdOf(session)`.
- 2026-10-06 — SQLite benchmark (T-066): `npm run bench:sqlite` (`dev/bench/`, never published, built on the dist; options `--size full|smoke`, `--adapters`, `--samples`, `--seed`, `--no-save`; results in git-ignored `bench-results/`). A deterministic generator (seeded mulberry32; category popularity 1/rank; the first 5,000 items all link to category 0 so a deep page exists) builds exactly 10,000 items, 1,000 categories and 100,000 edges through the real `write` in batches of 500 nodes or 1,000 links on a real database file in a temp folder, then times seven operations (the gate runs only a 300-item smoke of the plumbing, 25 tests; the timing clock is injected). **Baseline, SQLite on a file, Node 22.19, this laptop, seed 1** (p50 / p95 ms): items of one random category, page of 50: 8.5 / 14.8 (**NFR-05 target of 20 ms met**); categories of one item: 0.30 / 0.38; first page of the 5,000-item hub: 96 / 102; page 100 of the hub: 101 / 107; `describeGraph`: 525 / 571; one single-operation write: 0.08 / 0.16; one 1,000-operation write: 35 / 49; building the whole graph took 7.7 s. Memory reference: lookup 29 / 46 (over 20 ms), single write 28 / 34 (the copy-on-write cost), describe 135 / 151. **Findings**: the deep-page cost is not the skip-to-cursor, it is that a depth-1 query from a category reads that whole category before paging (hub first page about 100 ms whatever the page size); `describeGraph` is O(n) as expected. Both recorded in the Backlog with their numbers; neither blocks anything. Honest caveats: one machine, one seed, a laptop with other things running, 30 samples (5 for the 1,000-operation write); the three-clause set query is still unmeasured (after M4b). Mutation-checked: 14 defects, 5 survived at first and each led to a stronger test (flat skew, missing hub, batching beyond 500 nodes, sample caps, the memory reference leaking into findings); all caught.

- 2026-10-06 — Password hashing (T-072): `createPasswordHasher({ params?, randomBytes?, derive? })` in `src/users/password.ts` (public: `createPasswordHasher`, `DEFAULT_SCRYPT`, the types), scrypt from `node:crypto`, asynchronous so the event loop is not blocked, no dependency. Stored form `scrypt$N$r$p$salt$key` in base64url, 16-byte random salt per hash, 32-byte key; default cost N=2^15, r=8, p=1 (about 32 MiB, roughly 100 ms; `maxmem` is raised to twice the need so Node's 32 MiB default does not refuse it, tested with the real default). Passwords are NFKC-normalised before deriving (a full-width or decomposed form matches). `verify` compares in constant time (`timingSafeEqual`) and is **false, never a throw**, for anything not in our exact form: other schemes, wrong part counts, N not a power of two or below 16, r or p of 0, p above 16, a request for more than 64 MiB (128*N*r; so a tampered database row cannot make the server allocate gigabytes), salt outside 4 to 64 bytes, key not 32 bytes, a second spelling of the same bytes, values over 512 characters, non-text; a password over 1,024 characters or not text is false without hashing. A hash is verified at **its own** stored cost, and `needsRehash` is true when any of N, r, p is below the current setting (false for something that cannot be verified). `verifyAbsent(password)` is the unknown-user path: one derivation at the current cost, always false; a test proves by call counts that a wrong password and a missing user each cause exactly one derivation with the same parameters and the same (normalised) input. A derivation failure is rethrown as the fixed message `password hashing failed`, so a message that carries the password (an out-of-memory text, say) never escapes (tested on hash, verify and verifyAbsent, message and stack). Checked against **RFC 7914's published test vector** (first 32 bytes), so the format and the maths are the real scrypt. Settings (`N` power of two from 16, `r` at least 1, `p` 1 to 16, 128*N*r at most 64 MiB) are validated with a `TypeError`; the cost object is copied and frozen. 52 tests; 23 deliberate defects: after the first run 5 survived because the malformed-hash cases failed for the wrong reason (a wrong key never matches anyway), so cases that build **real** hashes in the forbidden shapes (3- and 65-byte salts, 31- and 33-byte keys, a second spelling) were added and then caught; the two that remain are equivalent pairs (the key length check and the derived-length check back each other up; the 512-character cap is backed up by the salt cap). Not decided here: rate limiting of verification is T-076.
- 2026-10-06 — User store (T-073): `UserStore` port in `src/users/store.ts` (public types), `createMemoryUserStore()`, and **`bipartite-graph/users/testing`** (tenth API report; the eleventh counting `users` itself) with `runUserStoreConformance(make, { describe, it }, { dispose? })` (26 cases). Port, all async and returning copies: `create(record, { adminIfFirst? })`, `get`, `getByUsername`, `credentialOf`, `credentialByUsername`, `update(id, patch, { protectLastAdmin? })`, `delete(id, { protectLastAdmin? })`, `list({ limit, cursor })`, `count`. **Design decisions**: (1) the store lower-cases usernames itself, so uniqueness and lookup cannot depend on the caller; (2) the only way a hash leaves a store is `credentialOf` / `credentialByUsername`; `get`, `getByUsername`, `list` and `update` never include one (tested by searching the JSON for the hash, `scrypt` and `passwordhash`); (3) the two atomic rules of R-002 live in the store so they hold under concurrency: `adminIfFirst` (first user, atomic with the insert) and `protectLastAdmin` (refuses a delete or demotion that would leave no admin, `LAST_ADMIN`); the controller will pass them on every such call; (4) `update` never changes id, username or `createdAt`, `email: null` removes the email, an omitted `passwordHash` keeps the old one; (5) `delete` is idempotent (`ok(false)` for a missing user); (6) `list` is by username in plain code-unit order with keyset paging (usernames are ASCII and immutable, so the order is stable), a limit that is not a positive whole number or a cursor the store did not give is a `RangeError`, and a page that exactly fills the limit has a null cursor, as in the graph store contract. Suite cases include 8 simultaneous creates of one username (exactly one wins), 8 simultaneous first creates (exactly one admin), two simultaneous guarded deletes or demotions of the only two admins (exactly one succeeds), stable paging under inserts and deletes, copies in and out (including the record passed to `create`), and two stores sharing nothing. Sixteen deliberately broken stores (a small naive store with one switchable defect each) are each caught by the case aimed at it, and the defect-free naive store passes everything. The memory store itself was mutation-checked with 22 defects; the 2 survivors were one equivalent pair (cursor prefix and canonical-form checks cover each other) and one that got a memory-specific test (a second spelling of a cursor). Next: T-074, the SQLite store, must pass this suite unchanged.
- 2026-10-06 — SQLite user store (T-074): `createSqliteUserStore({ path?, busyTimeoutMs? })` in `src/users/sqlite/` with its own entry point **`bipartite-graph/users/sqlite`** (so `bipartite-graph/users` never loads `node:sqlite`; twelfth API report; also exports `DbError`). **It passed the whole 26-case user-store conformance suite unchanged, on `:memory:` and on a real file per test** (both runs in `sqlite-store.test.ts`). Schema v1: one `users` table, `WITHOUT ROWID`, `username UNIQUE` with `CHECK (username <> '' AND username = lower(username))` (so a plain UNIQUE is case-insensitive without a collation), `CHECK` on the role, an index on role; **application id `0x43615573` ("CaUs")** against the graph database's "CaCi", so each refuses the other's file untouched (tested both ways). Atomic rules are in SQL: `create` is one `INSERT ... SELECT` that decides the first-admin role by counting in the same statement; uniqueness is the constraint (a failed insert is mapped to `CONFLICT` for the username or the id); `update` and `delete` run in one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction that also does the last-admin count. Every method runs without awaiting, so calls never interleave in this process, and `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` covers other processes. Failures come back as `STORAGE_ERROR` with a fixed message (the `DbError` text for a lock; never SQL or a value); a statement that fails inside a transaction is rolled back and the store keeps working (tested). A file is created 0600, closed cleanly leaves one file, a lock held past the timeout is a clear `STORAGE_ERROR`; a **kill -9** inside a write transaction leaves the file intact with only the committed user (and a companion run proves the child really wrote 501); **two processes** each creating 25 users plus the same name `dup` lose none and exactly one `dup` wins. **Refactor needed and done**: the generic schema logic moved from `graph_store/adapters/sqlite/schema.ts` into `src/sqlite/prepare.ts` (`prepareSchema(db, { applicationId, migrations, tables, foreignCode })`, `Migration`, `PrepareResult`), with the graph schema now a thin wrapper (its tests pass untouched); `DbErrorCode` gained `NOT_A_USERS_DATABASE` (additive; graph SQLite API report updated); the page cursor and limit check moved to `src/users/cursor.ts` and are shared by both stores; `ts-loader.mjs` moved to `src/sqlite/` as shared test support. Boundary: users tests may use the SQLite adapter's entry point (graph file versus user file test). `src/graph_store/purity.test.ts` timed out once at 5 s under load (it lints every source file); its timeout is now 60 s. 28 deliberate defects in the store, schema and shared prepare code; two survived at first (rollback after a failing statement, and the application id) and got tests. Default location of the user file (beside the graph file) is configuration for T-082, not here.
- 2026-10-06 — Session store (T-075): `SessionStore` port (`create(userId, now)` returns the token, `resolve(token, now)` the user id, `revoke`, `revokeAllFor(userId, { except? })`, `purgeExpired(now)`), `createMemorySessionStore`, `createSqliteSessionStore` (in `bipartite-graph/users/sqlite`, same file as the users), and `runSessionStoreConformance(make(options), { describe, it })` in `bipartite-graph/users/testing` (17 cases; the suite asks each store for idle 100 s, absolute 1,000 s, renew 60 s, 3 sessions per user and also builds stores with other settings). **Tokens** (`src/users/tokens.ts`): 32 random bytes as 43 base64url characters; `isWellFormedToken` accepts only that, in its one canonical spelling; **only `hashToken` (SHA-256, hex) is ever stored**, so a copy of the file cannot be replayed (tested on SQLite: the token is not found anywhere in the file or its -wal, in either text or byte form, the hash is, and presenting the stored hash or its base64 as a token fails). **Rules** (also in R-002): a session ends `idleMs` after last use (default 30 minutes) or `absoluteMs` after login (default 7 days); use renews the idle clock at most once per `renewEveryMs` (default 1 minute) so reading does not write every time (observable and tested at the boundaries); at most `maxPerUser` (default 20) sessions per user, a new login ends the least recently used (bounds storage against repeated logins); an expired session found by `resolve` is removed; a clock that steps backwards neither errors nor extends a session; malformed tokens are turned away before any store access and never throw. Settings are validated (`TypeError`: positive whole numbers, renew not above idle, idle not above absolute). **Schema v2** of the user database adds `sessions` (token hash primary key, user id, created, last used; index by user) and a **trigger that deletes a user's sessions when the user row goes** (so UA-FR-08/UA-AC-08 hold in the database whoever deletes the user); sessions deliberately have no foreign key, so the store works without a user row. Tested: sessions survive close and reopen (with the renewed time), two stores on one file share them, deleting a user through the user store ends their sessions, the per-user cap holds in the database, a version 1 file is upgraded to 2 without touching its users. **A real bug found by that upgrade test and fixed in the shared `prepareSchema`**: it checked that the file had every table of the LATEST schema before migrating, so an older file was refused as "missing its table". Each `Migration` now says what it `creates`, and a file is checked only against the tables its own version should have (the `tables` field of `SchemaSpec` is gone; the graph schema declares its tables on version 1; two graph schema tests pin it, including that a version 2 file missing version 2's table is refused). Mutation-checked: 28 defects; the survivors were a missing rollback test (a create that fails halfway, via a repeated token) and the token shape checks, which looked equivalent through the stores (a different spelling just hashes to nothing) but are exported, so `isWellFormedToken` got direct tests; all caught. Not in this task: sessions are not yet used by anything; T-077 wires login to `create` and `resolve`.
- 2026-10-06 — Throttling (T-076): `src/users/throttle.ts`, pure and clock-injected (time is an argument; the only state is the throttle's own bounded memory). **`createLoginThrottle`** (`check`, `recordFailure`, `recordSuccess`, `purge`, `size`): two keys, the normalised username (lower-cased, cut at 128 characters) and the client key the caller supplies. Per username: after 5 failures the next attempt is held back 1 s, then 2 s, 4 s, 8 s ... capped at 15 minutes, measured from the last failure; a failure older than 15 minutes starts the count again; a success clears that username. Per client, over all usernames: the same shape but 20 failures (a household shares an address), and a success does **not** forgive the client. `check` takes the longer of the two waits and **changes nothing**, so a caller that checks first and does not record throttled attempts cannot make a hold longer by hammering (the controller must do exactly that: check, refuse with `throttledError(retryAfterMs)`, record only real attempts). The throttle never sees the user store, so an unknown username is held back exactly like a real one provided the controller records failures for both (tested with 6 odd usernames: empty, spaces, path-like, NUL, 10,000 characters, non-Latin). **Bounded memory**: each table keeps at most `maxEntries` (10,000), and when full makes room in the order: an entry past its window, then one that never reached the threshold, then the oldest, so a flood of made-up names cannot push a lock that is really holding someone back out (tested: 2,000 made-up names against a table of 20). **`createRegistrationThrottle`**: 10 attempts an hour per client, sliding, `retryAfterMs` counted from the oldest attempt that counts; keeps only the latest `max` attempts per client (a test found the wait would be wrong if it kept more), at most `maxEntries` clients. **`throttledError(ms)`** is the `THROTTLED` error, rounded up to whole seconds, saying nothing about which key was over. Settings validated with `TypeError`. 27 tests; 29 deliberate defects, one survivor (the per-client attempt list was unbounded and the wait wrong) got a test. **Known limits, stated honestly**: (1) throttling by username lets an attacker who knows a username keep that account held back (up to 15 minutes at a time) by failing on purpose: the price of not revealing accounts; the owner can still log in from nowhere else during a hold, so an admin's account is the one to watch; recorded in the Backlog; (2) a distributed attacker using many client keys evades the per-client limit, and many usernames evade the per-username one: that is what password policy and scrypt cost are for; (3) the state is in memory, so a restart forgets it (acceptable for a household service, noted for the Pi item); (4) the client key is whatever the API passes (the socket address, or a trusted proxy header only when configured: T-080 and T-082).
- 2026-10-06 — User controller part 1 (T-077): `createUserController({ users, sessions, graphAdapter, hasher?, loginThrottle?, registrationThrottle?, clock?, config?, newUserId? })` in `src/users/controller.ts` (public: the factory and its types). Methods never throw (a store failing is `STORAGE_ERROR` with the fixed text "the user service could not complete the request", the cause never shown): **`register`** (closed switch → `FORBIDDEN` before anything is counted or hashed; registration throttle per client, counting every attempt including refused ones; validation in the order username, display name, email, password-with-username, first error returned with its field; hash; `users.create` with `adminIfFirst`; a taken id is redrawn up to 3 times; then `createGraph(user graph)`, and **if that fails the account is deleted again** so either both exist or neither, still reporting the failure if the deletion fails too; a failing user creation leaves no graph because the graph is made second); **`login`** (non-text input is `INVALID_INPUT` naming the field; the throttle is checked first and a held-back attempt is not recorded so hammering cannot lengthen a hold; an unknown username runs `verifyAbsent` and a wrong password runs `verify`: **the same single derivation and the same `UNAUTHENTICATED` error object** (tested by call counts and deep equality); failures recorded for both alike, a success clears the username; an out-of-date hash is rehashed at login without changing `updatedAt` (and a failure to do so does not fail the login); the user's graph is created if missing, which repairs a registration that stopped between its two steps; then a session); **`logout`** (ends that session only, never an error, accepts any value); **`resolve`** (`UNAUTHENTICATED` "not signed in", identical for every kind of bad token; a token of a deleted account ends its session and fails); **`graphIdOf(user)`** (`user-<id>`, the only route to a user's graph). Returns `Authenticated = { user, graphId }` and `LoggedIn` adds the token. Tested with real memory stores and graph adapter, an injected clock and a cheap counting hasher: 40 tests including eight simultaneous first registrations (exactly one admin), two users' graphs isolated through the real `write` and `query`, and no password, hash or token-store detail in any result. 30 deliberate defects; one survivor (the rehash `updatedAt` test used the wrong clock) fixed and caught. Not done here: change password, update details, delete account (T-078), admin operations (T-079). Registration reveals that a username exists (CONFLICT) as R-002 accepts; it is throttled.
- 2026-10-06 — User controller part 2, self service (T-078): `getMe`, `updateMe`, `changePassword`, `deleteMe` on the controller. **Each takes the session token and acts only on the person it belongs to; none takes a user id**, so there is nothing to forge. `getMe` = `resolve`. `updateMe` changes display name and/or email (`email: null` removes it); any other key in the input (`username`, `role`, `id`, `createdAt`, `password`...) is refused **by name** (`INVALID_INPUT` naming the key) rather than ignored, and nothing at all is changed when one is present; an empty change is refused. `changePassword` needs the current password: it is checked under the **same login throttle** (key = the account's username and the client), so wrong guesses here and at the login form add up, held-back attempts are not recorded, and a right one clears the count; a wrong one is `INVALID_INPUT` on `currentPassword` (not `UNAUTHENTICATED`, which would make an API log the person out); the new password goes through the policy (errors name `newPassword`) and must differ from the current one; the hash is replaced, then **every other session of that user ends and the current one survives**, other people's sessions are untouched; if ending the others fails the error says the password WAS changed but the other sessions could not be ended (never a silent success). `deleteMe` needs the password (same throttle); order: refuse `LAST_ADMIN` first if this is the only admin (so nothing is touched), then **drop the user's graph** (a missing graph is fine; any other failure keeps the account and says "your data could not be deleted, so the account was kept", the person stays signed in), then delete the account with the store's atomic last-admin guard (if another request made them the only admin in the meantime, an empty graph is given back and `LAST_ADMIN` is reported), then end every session. Shared internals: `whoIs(token)`, `confirmPassword`, `hasOtherAdmin` (pages through the user list, stops at the first other admin). 35 new tests plus the 40 from part 1 (test helpers moved to `controller.test-util.ts`); 24 deliberate defects: two survived at first (a right password not clearing the confirmation failures, and deleting the sessions after account deletion because the test's own `resolve` call had already ended them) and got tests. Open point for T-079/T-081: the admin version of delete must do the same graph-first sequence; the sequence now lives inline in `deleteMe` and should be factored when the second caller exists.
- 2026-10-06 — User controller part 3, admin operations (T-079): **`src/users/authorise.ts`**: `authorise(actor, action, targetId?)` is the whole "who may do what" as one pure function, tested as a table over every role, every action and self versus other (plus no target, unknown roles, lookalike ids, an unknown action). Actions: `listUsers`, `getUser`, `updateUser`, `changeRole`, `resetPassword`, `deleteUser`. An admin may do all; a user may only `getUser` and `updateUser` on **their own** record; **`changeRole` is its own action, so a user can never change a role, not even their own** (no self-promotion). Controller methods (each takes the session token, the target id and the change): `listUsers` (admin; limit 1 to 200, default 50, bad limit or cursor `INVALID_INPUT` naming the field), `getUser`, `updateUser` (display name, email, role; any other key refused by name; a role change by a non-admin is `FORBIDDEN` and **nothing at all** is changed, including the display name sent with it; every update uses the store's atomic `protectLastAdmin`), `resetPassword` (admin only, even on oneself through this route a user gets `FORBIDDEN`; policy applies with the target's username; **all of the target's sessions end**, and an admin resetting their own keeps only the session they are using; other people's untouched; if ending sessions fails the error says the password WAS changed), `deleteUser` (admin only, someone else's account: the shared `removeAccount` does graph first, then account with the atomic guard, then sessions; an admin deleting themselves here is refused with a pointer to `deleteMe`, which asks for the password). **Authorisation is decided before the target is looked up**, so an ordinary user gets the identical `FORBIDDEN` for an account that exists, one that does not and a malformed id (no probing for who exists); an admin gets `NOT_FOUND`. **The caller's role is read from the store on every request**, so a demoted admin loses the power at once on the session they already hold (tested), and a promoted user gains it at once. `removeAccount` was factored out of `deleteMe` (as flagged in T-078), so both delete paths are the same code. Concurrency tested: two admins demoting each other, and deleting each other, at the same moment leave exactly one admin, with a graph, and every user has a graph and every graph a user. 46 new tests (122 in the controller and authorise files, 2,926 in the gate); 38 deliberate defects, two survivors at first (the default page size, and the target's username in the policy) got tests; one remaining mutant is equivalent (sparing the admin's own token when resetting someone else's password changes nothing, since it is not theirs). The API (T-081) will map `FORBIDDEN` to 403 for both missing and forbidden targets and must not add a way to tell them apart.
- 2026-10-06 — HTTP server kit (T-080): new component `src/api/` with entry point **`bipartite-graph/api`** (API report; the only place in the library that imports a network module, enforced by a scan test that also proves it can see `server.ts`; tests that spawn child processes elsewhere are the one allowed exception). Files: `cookies.ts` (`parseCookies` into a prototype-free frozen map: first of a repeated name wins, malformed pairs skipped, header over 8 KB, value over 4 KB, more than 50 cookies ignored; `serialiseCookie` always `Path=/; HttpOnly; SameSite=Strict`, `Max-Age` and `Secure` when asked, throws `TypeError` for any name or value that could inject a header; `clearCookie`), `router.ts` (`matchRoute`: exact, case-sensitive, `:name` parameters of `[A-Za-z0-9_.-]` up to 128, **paths with anything that needs decoding, `..`, `.`, `//`, a trailing `/`, more than 8 segments or over 1,024 characters are 404 and never reach a handler**; a path that exists with another method is 405 with `Allow`), `body.ts` (`parseBody`: an empty body is `undefined`, otherwise `application/json` (UTF-8 charset only) else 415; strict UTF-8; must be a JSON **object**; refuses `__proto__`, `constructor` and `prototype` keys at any depth and nesting beyond 16, each 400), `server.ts` (`createApiServer({ routes, host?, maxBodyBytes?, maxHeaderBytes?, headersTimeoutMs?, requestTimeoutMs?, allowedHosts?, trustedProxies?, log? })`). **Defaults**: bind `127.0.0.1`; body 16,384 bytes (413 by declared length at once, and by counting a chunked body as it arrives); headers 16,384 (431); 10 s for headers and 30 s for the whole request (a client that sends headers and then stalls gets a 408 and is cut off; `connectionsCheckingInterval` had to be set because Node's default checks only every 30 s); every reply carries `Cache-Control: no-store`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'`; no CORS (OPTIONS and HEAD are 405 with `Allow`); `Origin` is checked on **every non-GET, before the body is read**: it must be http or https with a host equal to the request's `Host` (so `null`, other sites, `ftp://` and look-alike hosts are 403; a request with no `Origin` is allowed, as it is not a browser's cross-site request); `Host` must be this server's own loopback names when bound to loopback without a proxy (against DNS rebinding), or exactly `allowedHosts`, and is not checked behind a trusted proxy unless `allowedHosts` is given (a public name cannot be known here); errors are always `{ error: { code, message } }`, a throwing handler is a fixed 500 `internal error` (no stack, path or message, tested for sync and async throws); the log hook gets method, path **without the query**, status and milliseconds, never a body, cookie or query (tested with secrets in each). **Client key** (what throttling counts by): the socket address (IPv4-mapped IPv6 shown as IPv4); `X-Forwarded-For` is ignored unless `trustedProxies` is 1 to 5, then the entry that many from the END is used (the one a trusted proxy wrote, not what the client claimed first), and anything that is not shaped like an address falls back to the socket. Handlers get `{ method, path, params, query, body, cookies, clientKey }` and return `{ status, body?, cookies?, retryAfterSeconds? }`; DELETE bodies are read (deleting your own account carries the password). The kit has no knowledge of users: routes arrive in T-081. Boundary rules: the API may use `users/index` and `graph_store/index` only (its tests may also use the memory and SQLite adapters, the SQLite user store and the users test suite), not the LLM, the application or the SQLite wrapper; nothing may import the API. 97 API tests (3,029 in the gate); 47 deliberate defects. Equivalent survivors, stated plainly: the path character whitelist in `segmentsOf` is backed by the parameter pattern and exact literal matching (defence in depth); ignoring `X-Forwarded-For` when `trustedProxies` is 0 is also what falls out of the lookup, the explicit line is clarity. Four others got tests (declared length refused at once, the header limit setting, forwarded values that are not addresses, non-http `Origin` schemes). Dev lesson: `socket.on('close')` never fires on a paused socket whose peer sent a final reply, so tests that wait for a close must `resume()`.
- 2026-10-06 — Account routes (T-081): `createAccountRoutes({ controller, cookieName?, secureCookies?, cookieMaxAgeSeconds? })` in `src/api/routes.ts` (public with `errorResponse` and `STATUS_OF`) returns the eleven routes of R-002: `POST /api/register` (201, `{ user }`, no cookie: you log in next), `POST /api/login` (200 `{ user, graphId }` and the session cookie; **the token is in the cookie only, never in a body**), `POST /api/logout` (204, clears the cookie, works with or without a session), `GET|PATCH|DELETE /api/me`, `POST /api/me/password`, `GET /api/users` (limit and cursor from the query string; a limit that is not a number is passed on as NaN and refused by name), `GET|PATCH|DELETE /api/users/:id`, `POST /api/users/:id/password`. **Statuses** (a fixed table, each pinned by a test): `INVALID_INPUT` 422 (with `field`), `CONFLICT` 409, `NOT_FOUND` 404, `UNAUTHENTICATED` 401, `FORBIDDEN` 403, `THROTTLED` 429 with `Retry-After`, `LAST_ADMIN` 409, `STORAGE_ERROR` 500; every failure body is `{ error: { code, message, field? } }`. The cookie defaults: `caci_session`, `HttpOnly; SameSite=Strict; Path=/`, `Max-Age` 7 days (the session's absolute lifetime), `Secure` when configured. **The cookie is the only credential**: an `Authorization` header, a `?token=` query and a token in a body are all ignored (tested). **Unknown body fields are refused by name on every body route** (`role` sent to register, a typo, `remember` on login: 422 with the key), so nothing is silently dropped. A 401 on a route that had a cookie also clears the cookie, so a stale session stops being sent. An ordinary user gets the same 403 for another account whether it exists or not (checked through HTTP). The client key reaches the throttles for login, change-password and delete-account (tested with a spy through a trusted proxy header). The table test walks eight routes as nobody, an ordinary user and an admin with exact statuses; a cross-origin write is 403 with no account made and no cookie; a form post is 415; hostile cookie values are 401. **Secrets scan over the whole file**: every response body and every log line produced by all 35 tests (over 100 of them) is searched at the end for every password used, every session token ever issued, `scrypt$` and `passwordHash`; none appears. The request log is shown to hold only method, path, status and milliseconds. 35 route tests (3,066 in the gate); 36 deliberate defects: two survivors (the client key passed to change-password and delete-account) got a test; two are equivalent (an empty `limit=` becomes 0 or NaN, both refused; a mutant that did not compile). not the LLM, the application or the SQLite wrapper; nothing may import it (seven new rule cases). **Settings** (all `CACI_*`, read from an environment object passed in, never from `process.env` inside the library; unset and empty are the same): `PORT` 0 to 65535 (8080), `BIND` an IPv4, IPv6 or `localhost` address (127.0.0.1), `DATA_DIR` (`./data`), `ALLOW_REGISTRATION` (true), `COOKIE_SECURE` (false), `TRUSTED_PROXIES` 0 to 5 (0), `ALLOWED_HOSTS` up to 20 comma separated names (none), `ALLOW_INSECURE` (false); booleans are exactly `true` or `false`. **Every problem is reported at once, by variable name, and no message repeats a value** (tested with a secret-looking value in each). **An unknown `CACI_` variable is an error** (so `CACI_ALLOW_REGISTERATION=false` is not silently ignored). **The safety rule**: a bind address that is not loopback with `COOKIE_SECURE` off is refused, naming `CACI_COOKIE_SECURE`, unless `CACI_ALLOW_INSECURE=true` says the risk is accepted (so exposing the service on a network with plain cookies is a deliberate act). **`startService`**: makes the data folder owner-only (0700, parents included), opens `graphs.db` and `users.db` (0600; the users file is shared by the user store and the session store through two connections), builds the controller with real scrypt cost, routes and server, listens; **if any step fails everything already opened is closed again** (a taken port is reported as `port N on HOST is already in use`; a data folder that is a file, or a database file that is not a database, is refused with the file left as it was); `close()` stops the server then the databases and is idempotent; after a clean close the folder holds exactly the two `.db` files. **`serve`** returns exit code 2 for bad settings (all errors listed, nothing created), 1 for a failed start, 0 with the service; it prints where it listens (IPv6 in brackets), where the data is and that it is not encrypted, whether registration is open (with the reminder that **the first account made becomes the admin, so register yourself first**) and, when cookies are not Secure, that this is for this machine or behind HTTPS; never a secret. **Real check**: `npm run serve` was run for real: registered an owner over HTTP with curl (201, the security headers present), `/api/me` without a cookie was 401, SIGINT closed it cleanly, the folder was 0700 with `graphs.db` and `users.db` at 0600 and nothing else, and a bad environment exited 2 naming the unknown variable. 76 service tests (3,112 in the gate) including a restart that keeps the account and a still-valid session, and client counting by a trusted proxy's address versus the socket address (11 registrations from 11 forwarded addresses all pass behind a proxy, the 11th from one socket is 429); 37 deliberate defects, survivors: the data-folder-is-a-folder check and the close order (equivalent here: `mkdir` already fails on a file; no request is in flight in the tests), idempotence of `close` (the second call has nothing left to close), and the loopback re-validation (an invalid bind is refused elsewhere). It works on `users.db` in `CACI_DATA_DIR` directly (the same environment as the service; bad settings are exit 2 naming the variable), **never creates a database** (a missing `users.db` is exit 1 naming the path), accepts the username in any case, and for a missing name, a malformed one and an ordinary non-admin account prints the **same sentence** and asks for no password, so a typo costs no typing and a probe learns nothing. **The password is never an argument**: the script reads standard input (one trailing newline dropped) or, on a terminal, prompts without echo and asks twice; `recoverAdmin` only asks for it after the admin is found. It applies the password policy with the account's username (nothing changed if refused), hashes with the default scrypt cost, **ends only that account's sessions**, never prints the password in any outcome (tested on success and on refusal), works while the service runs (the database serialises the two) and leaves just `graphs.db` and `users.db` behind (stores closed in a `finally`). - 2026-10-06 — Demo model client (T-086): **`createDemoModelClient()`** in `src/llm/demo-client.ts`, exported from `llm/index` (public API report updated), ported from the explorer's JavaScript demo into typed, tested library code (the explorer keeps its own copy for now; Backlog). It is a real `ModelClient`: it checks the request (`CONFIG` for a bad one), runs under `runWithDeadline` (time limit, a signal already cancelled never starts), never throws, answers at once so cannot hang, is deterministic (ties broken by plain code-unit order, never by the machine's locale), and reports itself as model `demo` with plausible token counts (about four characters a token, never zero) whatever model name was asked for; JSON when the request carries a schema, text otherwise; an unrecognised prompt is a non-retryable `MODEL_ERROR`. **Rule**: link to the one or two existing categories whose id or name shares a word with the note (a word is 3 or more letters, not a stop word; a prefix counts only when both words have 4 or more letters, so `car` is not `cargo` but `garden` matches `gardening`; ranked by shared words, then by how many items the category holds, then by id; weights 0.9 and 0.6), else one new category from the commonest word (longest, then alphabetical, as a tie-break; at most 30 characters), else `inbox`; title at most 60 and summary 120 characters. **Cutting is by character, not by UTF-16 unit**, so no title, summary or name can end in half an emoji (found while writing the mutation checks: the first test used an even number of units and could not see it). **Every reply passes the output guard, for any note**: 2,000 random notes (a pool of awkward fragments: markup, escapes, injection phrases, non-Latin, emoji, 90-character words) with random category lists (odd ids and names, 100-character ids) through the real `categorise`, none refused and none needing the repair attempt, plus 8,000-character notes of one word, one huge word, symbols, emoji and CJK, and a hostile note that tries to order a deletion and files only itself. 27 tests (3,250 in the gate); 29 deliberate defects, 3 survivors at first (the id cap shown in the reason, the half-emoji cut, the 4-letter prefix rule, and the model name) each got a test and are now caught.
- 2026-10-06 — CaCi controller: scaffold and propose (T-087): new component `src/caci/` with entry point **`bipartite-graph/caci`** (fifteenth API report; `createCaciController`, `viewOf`, `MAX_NOTE_CHARS`, `caciError`, the types). **Boundary**: it may use only the indexes of `users`, `app`, `graph_store` and `llm` (its tests may also use the memory adapter), never the SQLite wrapper, the API or the service; only the API and the service may import it (eleven new rule cases). `createCaciController({ users, capture, mode?, clock?, limits? })`; `users` only needs `resolve`. **`propose(token, { text })`**: the session gives the user and **their graph id, which is passed to the capture controller and is never a parameter** (tested: `graphId`, `userId`, `mode`, `model`, `kind` in the input are refused by name before anything happens); text must be a non-blank string of at most **8,000 characters** (the categoriser's own limit; refused here with a clear message and no model call; the R-003 spec said 20,000 and is corrected); the answer is a frozen **`ProposalView`** with exactly `id, createdAt, expiresAt, mode, text, summary, operations, rationale?` (a test lists the keys and searches the JSON for usage, tokens, attempts, system prompt, graph id and model name: none). **Limits**: at most 10 pending per account and 30 new proposals an hour, both configurable (`TypeError` for nonsense), both **checked before the model is called**; a refusal by the pending limit or for a bad note is **not** counted against the hour, a proposal whose model call fails **is** (it cost something); the hourly window is the users component's sliding-window limiter used under a clearer name; pending places are **reserved before the first await**, so 15 simultaneous proposals with room for 10 send exactly 10 to the model; a place is given back when the proposal fails or the capture controller throws (the error still comes out), and expired ones are swept; `TOO_MANY_PENDING` says when the oldest place frees up. **Errors** keep their source: `caci` (UNAUTHENTICATED, NOT_FOUND, EXPIRED, THROTTLED, TOO_MANY_PENDING, INVALID_INPUT, the last three of which T-088 will use), `app` (the capture controller's own global limit, tested), `graph` (a graph that is gone), `llm` (a failing model). Ownership is recorded for each held proposal for T-088. Tested with the real user controller, the real capture controller and the real demo model client on the memory adapter: 29 tests (3,285 in the gate); 24 deliberate defects, all caught. A test-writing lesson: sessions go idle after 30 minutes, so tests that move the clock an hour must keep using each session (or log in again).
- 2026-10-06 — CaCi controller: get, approve and reject with ownership (T-088): `get`, `approve`, `reject` by session token and proposal id, plus `ApprovedView` (`{ id, applied, summary }`: which proposal, how many operations were written, and what they were; never a graph id, usage or tokens). **Ownership**: a proposal is visible to, and actable by, its owner only. **Anyone else (another account, an admin, a made-up id, a non-string, an id of the form that internally names a reserved place, an upper-cased or space-padded copy) gets exactly the same `NOT_FOUND` object a missing proposal gets**, checked by deep equality for get, approve and reject; an admin has no exception (tested with the first account, who is the admin). **Expiry**: the owner is told `EXPIRED` (get, approve, reject), everyone else `NOT_FOUND`; expired owners are remembered for the last 1,000 expired proposals only (a bounded map; tested: the oldest is forgotten and becomes `NOT_FOUND`, the newest is still `EXPIRED`); if the capture controller thinks a proposal is stale before this one does (clock skew), the owner is still told `EXPIRED` once and the place is given back. **Approve** writes through the capture controller to the graph the proposal was made for (the owner's) and nobody else's, with two or three simultaneous approvals writing once (the losers get `NOT_FOUND`), approve against reject at the same moment giving exactly one winner, and a second approval later `NOT_FOUND`; a failed write (a store failing after the item and category were put, a link to a category that does not exist, a graph that has gone) **changes nothing and leaves the proposal pending and still counted against the limit**, so it can be retried or rejected, and a retry after the store recovers succeeds; approved and rejected proposals free their place at once. A session that has ended, or whose account was deleted, can do nothing, and a new session of the same person sees their proposals. A restart (new controllers over the same stores) forgets every pending proposal and keeps everything approved. **Internal change**: places reserved for in-flight proposals moved out of the proposal map into their own (so no id a client sends can ever name a reservation). Test helpers moved to `controller.test-util.ts`. 21 new tests (3,306 in the gate); 41 deliberate defects: survivors at first were the capture controller's own expired and not-found answers (needed a clock-skew test) and a type check that is equivalent (a non-string id misses the map anyway).
- 2026-10-06 — Browsing your own graph (T-089): `summary`, `categories`, `categoryItems` and `item` on the CaCi controller (`src/caci/browse.ts`, public types and `capData`), every one by session token and every one reading **only the session's graph** through the public `query` and `describeGraph`; `createCaciController` now needs a `graphAdapter` (a `TypeError` without it). **Summary**: the counts. **Categories**: by id (plain code-unit order), keyset paged (`limit` 1 to 100, default 50, `cursor` the previous `nextCursor`; bad values are `INVALID_INPUT` naming the field, a cursor from another kind of listing too), each with `id`, `name` (from the category's data when it is text, at most 100 characters) and `itemCount` (one count query per category on the page), with `itemCountCapped: true` when the category is too big to count exactly (the walk stops at 10,000 nodes: tested with a category of 10,050 items, which reports 9,999 as a lower bound). **Category items**: the items filed under one category, paged, each `{ id, data }` with `dataTruncated: true` when its data was shortened. **One item**: its data and its categories with names and weights (a weight never set reads 1), at most 200 categories with `moreCategories: true` beyond that. **Data cap**: node data over 4,096 characters of JSON is shortened (each text value cut to 300 characters, by character so no emoji is cut in half) and, if still over, dropped to `{}`, always flagged; exactly at the cap it is returned whole. **Ids are opaque**: spaces, capitals, markup, quotes, slashes, `..`, emoji, CJK and 128- and 256-character ids all work for categories, their items and items (one test walks 13 awkward ids end to end); an id that is not text (or empty) is `INVALID_INPUT` naming `id`; an id that is missing, belongs to the other partition (an item's id asked as a category), differs in case or padding, is too long to exist, or **exists only in someone else's graph** is the same `NOT_FOUND`. **Isolation**: two accounts with the same category and item ids (different data) each see only their own, a graph-looking id and an extra `graphId` argument change nothing. A missing graph is reported as a graph error, a signed-out session as `UNAUTHENTICATED` on all four. 53 new tests (3,335 in the gate); 54 deliberate defects: survivors were the default page size and the capped flag (both got tests, the latter with 10,050 real items, about a quarter of a second), and four equivalents (an empty cursor is also refused by the store and mapped to the same error; the edges-of-this-item filter and the count of edges beyond 200 are backed up by the page's own cursor and by the graph's rule that an item page carries only its own edges).
- 2026-10-06 — Capture routes (T-090): `createCaptureRoutes({ caci, cookieName?, secureCookies? })` and `mapCaciError` in `src/api/capture-routes.ts` (public). Routes: `POST /api/capture/propose` (`{ text }` → 201 `{ proposal }`), `GET /api/capture/proposals/:id` (200 `{ proposal }`), `POST /api/capture/proposals/:id/approve` (200 `{ written: { id, applied, summary } }`), `POST /api/capture/proposals/:id/reject` (204, no body). Same session cookie, same cross-origin / JSON / size rules as the account routes (inherited from the server kit; tested through the capture routes too); a 401 clears a stale cookie; unknown body fields are refused by name (propose takes only `text`; approve and reject take nothing; a GET's body is never read). **One mapping, one table** (`mapCaciError`, tested over every error the controller can give): `caci` UNAUTHENTICATED 401, NOT_FOUND 404, EXPIRED 410, THROTTLED and TOO_MANY_PENDING 429 with `Retry-After`, INVALID_INPUT 422 with `field`; the capture controller's own errors: its bad note 422, not found 404, expired 410, its **global** pending limit 429 `BUSY` (retry in 60 s); the graph store: a link to a category that no longer fits, a conflict or a validation failure while writing 409 `WRITE_REFUSED` (the proposal stays pending), anything else including a graph that has gone 500 `STORAGE_ERROR`; the model: timeout 502 `MODEL_TIMEOUT`, refusal 502 `MODEL_REFUSED`, any other failure or bad output 502 `MODEL_ERROR`, a provider rate limit **503 `MODEL_BUSY` with Retry-After** (the provider's own wait, else 30 s), our own configuration or cancellation 500. **Messages are fixed per kind, never the text of a provider, a store or a path** (a stand-in model that answers with `provider said sk-ant-... at /var/provider/log` leaks none of it, tested for all seven failure codes). Proposal JSON is exactly `{ id, createdAt, expiresAt, mode, text, summary, operations, rationale }`: no prompt, usage, tokens, attempts, model name or graph id. **A non-owner, an admin, a made-up id and a strange id (`..`, `%2e%2e`, an encoded slash, 200 characters) all get the same 404 body, for get, approve and reject.** An expired proposal is 410 for its owner and 404 for anyone else. **Whole-file scan** at the end of the test run: no response contains a password, a session token, the provider's secret, `inputTokens`, `<note>` or `sk-ant`. **Refactor**: `unknownKey` and `NO_BODY` moved from `routes.ts` to `common.ts` (the account route tests pass untouched). **Boundary**: the API may now use `caci/index` (not its internals); API tests (only) may use the indexes of `app` and `llm` to build a stand-in service; production API code still may not touch the LLM component, the application or the SQLite wrapper. 23 new tests (3,360 in the gate); 31 deliberate defects, all caught.
- 2026-10-06 — Read routes (T-091): `createReadRoutes({ caci, cookieName?, secureCookies? })` in `src/api/read-routes.ts` (public). All GET, all need the session cookie, none can name a graph: **`GET /api/graph`** (`{ summary: { itemCount, categoryCount, edgeCount } }`), **`GET /api/graph/categories`** (`limit`, `cursor`; `{ items, nextCursor }`, each category with `id`, `name`, `itemCount`, `itemCountCapped?`), **`GET /api/graph/category?id=`** (`id`, `limit`, `cursor`; `{ category, items, nextCursor }`), **`GET /api/graph/item?id=`** (`{ item, categories, moreCategories? }`). **Ids travel in the query string, never the path** (an id in the path is simply a 404 route); 14 awkward ids (spaces, capitals, `a/b?c=d&e#f`, `100%`, `+plus+`, quotes, markup, emoji, CJK, `..`, 128 characters) round-trip through listing, a category's items and an item. A `limit` that is not a number (including an empty one) is passed on as NaN and refused by name (422, `field: limit`); **an unknown query parameter is refused by name** (a typo like `limt`, or `graphId` or `userId`, is a 422 naming it, not silently ignored); a missing or empty `id` is 422 naming `id`. Errors use the same mapping as the capture routes (`respond`, now exported from `capture-routes.ts`); `NOT_FOUND` now carries the controller's own fixed words (`no such proposal`, `no such category`, `no such item`) instead of one sentence for all. A missing item, a category's id asked as an item, another account's item and a 300-character id are the same 404 body; a stale cookie on a 401 is cleared; a graph that has gone is the fixed 500; POST, PATCH and DELETE on a read route are 405 with `Allow: GET`; responses hold no graph id (`user-u...`), and nothing in any response of the file holds a session token, the password, `scrypt` or `passwordHash` (whole-file scan). Two people with the same ids see only their own data. 18 new tests (3,378 in the gate); 31 deliberate defects across the read and capture routes; equivalents among the survivors: an empty `limit=` as NaN or as 0 (both refused), a missing cookie reaching the controller as `undefined` (it says UNAUTHENTICATED anyway), and a remap of the store's write-refusal errors on reads, which cannot occur and was **removed** as speculative. **README rows are deferred to T-094**: the service only serves these routes once T-093 wires them, and the README must not describe what `npm run serve` does not yet do (T-091's acceptance in PLAN.md was changed to say so).

Every problem is still reported at once, no message repeats a value, and an unknown `CACI_` name is still an error. **The key is not part of `ServiceConfig`** (a settings object gets printed, serialised and logged; the key never should be; tested: no key-like field, and the JSON of the settings does not contain it); whoever starts the model reads it from the environment at that moment (T-093). **Refactor needed**: the key reading (`readAnthropicKey`, `ANTHROPIC_KEY_VARIABLE`, `ANTHROPIC_KEY_SHAPE`) moved from the Anthropic client into `src/llm/api-key.ts` and is exported from `llm/index` too, so checking a setting does not load the provider's SDK (an optional dependency); the Anthropic entry point re-exports the same names unchanged; the boundary rule that `llm/index` cannot reach `llm/anthropic/*` still holds. **Boundary**: the service may now use `llm/index` (not its internals, not the Anthropic client directly). **Two things deliberately not done here, and why**: `serve` saying which model is in use (and, for `anthropic`, the sentence that every account's notes go to Anthropic and are not anonymised) moves to T-093, because it would be false until the model is wired in; the README rows move to T-094 for the same reason, and `docs.test.ts` carries a list `NOT_DOCUMENTED_YET` of the three settings that are read but do nothing yet, to be emptied at T-094 (the docs test fails if a setting is documented before it is on that list's complement, or missing). A `baseURL` option was added and then removed: no test needed it and the injected `fetch` suffices (a mutation check showed it was dead). **The key travels** `serve` reads it with `readAnthropicKey(env)` only when `CACI_LLM=anthropic` and passes it as `apiKey`; it is not in `ServiceConfig`, not printed and not written. `serve` now prints the model line: the free demo model ("Nothing leaves this machine.") or, for the real one, "Every account's notes, and the names of their categories, are sent to Anthropic, and nothing is anonymised." **Fake-provider test**: the note and category names arrive in the request, the key arrives only in the `x-api-key` header, and an after-all scan finds it in no response, log line, printed output or file; provider 401 gives 500, 429 gives 503 with Retry-After, 500 gives 502, and a provider error that repeats the key is not leaked.
