New **`src/architecture.test.ts`** (24 tests) fails the build if a container's `Code: src/...` folder does not exist (planned containers are exempt), if a container is connected to nothing, or if the diagram mentions a bank or customer; 4 deliberate defects (a typo in a folder, removing the Planned mark early, orphaning the new container, a banking person) were each caught. 3,515 tests in the gate.
- 2026-10-07 — Circles scaffold and permission table (T-097): new component `src/circles/` with entry point **`bipartite-graph/circles`** (sixteenth API report; `api-extractor.circles.json`, package export, both api scripts, and `dist/circles` in the not-published check). Contents: `CirclesError` (nine codes: `INVALID_INPUT`, `NOT_FOUND`, `FORBIDDEN`, `CONFLICT`, `UNAUTHENTICATED`, `LAST_OWNER`, `LIMIT_REACHED`, `THROTTLED`, `STORAGE_ERROR`, frozen, `field` and `retryAfterMs` only when given), the four roles (names checked in a test to say nothing about any setting), circle and invitation ids (`c` and `i` plus 16, made by the unbiased user-id generator with another prefix, matched strictly), validators (`parseCircleName` 1 to 80 characters counted by code points with no control characters; `parseCircleDescription` optional, up to 500, new lines and tabs allowed, empty means none; `parseCreateCircle`, `parseUpdateCircle` (at least one change, `null` or empty clears the description), `parseInvite` (the username is only checked for shape, lower-cased), `parseRoleChange`), all strict: a body must be a plain object and **any other key is refused by name** (the message cuts a long key), and **`authorise(actorRole, action, { role?, target?, self? })`**, the whole permission table as one pure function over nine actions: a non-member gets nothing; members see, list and leave; owners and managers rename; only owners delete; owners invite or revoke any role and managers only `member` and `observer`; owners change anyone else's role and remove anyone else, managers only move `member`/`observer` people between those two and remove only them; nobody changes their own role and removing yourself is leaving (`self` must be explicitly `false` for changeRole and removeMember, so a caller that forgets to say is refused); anything unknown is refused. **The table is tested over every one of 5 x 9 x 5 x 5 x 3 = 3,375 combinations against an independently written expected set**, plus the headline rules in plain statements. **Own `Result` type** in `result.ts` (structurally identical to the graph store's) so the component imports nothing that can read a graph (R-004 CR-NFR-06). **Boundary rules** (81 tests, 15 new violation cases and 5 clean layouts): production circles code may use only `users/index` and `sqlite/db`; its tests may also use `graph_store/index`, the memory adapter, and the users SQLite and testing entry points (to build a user controller); it must not use the LLM component, the application, the CaCi controller, the API or the service; nothing but the API (via `circles/index`, tests also the SQLite and testing entries) and the service (`circles/index` and `circles/sqlite/index`) may import it; the SQLite wrapper rule now names circles beside the graph store and users. Container diagram: the Circle Controller now says "Being built" instead of "Planned", so the architecture test checks that `src/circles` exists (it does now). 37 deliberate defects across the permission function, validators, ids, roles and errors; 36 caught; the survivor is equivalent (wrapping the username error again changes nothing: the users component's error already has the same code, field and message). 3,552 tests in the gate.
- 2026-10-07 — Circle store (T-098): the `CircleStore` port (`src/circles/store.ts`), the reference **memory store**, and the shared **conformance suite** at **`bipartite-graph/circles/testing`** (`runCircleStoreConformance(make, { describe, it }, { dispose? })`; seventeenth API report; 49 cases). **Port**, all async and returning copies: `createCircle` (with the owner's membership, atomically, and the per-person circle cap passed in as an argument so configuration stays in the controller), `getCircle`, `updateCircle`, `deleteCircle`, `membershipOf`, `listCirclesOf` (by circle id, with role, join time and member count), `listMembers` (by user id), `changeRole` and `removeMember` (the **last-owner rule is always on and lives in the store**: `LAST_OWNER`), `createInvitation` (**the store lower-cases the username**; a repeat for the same circle and name keeps the first id and creation time and takes the new role, inviter and expiry, and does not use another place; expired invitations never count toward the cap), `getInvitation`, `listInvitationsOfCircle`, `listInvitationsFor`, `revokeInvitation` (scoped to the circle), `declineInvitation` (scoped to the name), `acceptInvitation` (one atomic step: checks name, expiry, circle, both caps, then adds the member at `now` and closes the invitation; every miss is the **same** `NOT_FOUND`; a full circle or a person at the cap is `LIMIT_REACHED` and **leaves the invitation open**; someone already in keeps their role), `removeUser(userId, username)` (leaves every circle; a circle they solely owned goes to the **longest-standing manager, else member, else observer**, earliest join then lowest user id, or is dissolved if nobody is left; removes invitations addressed to their name and those they sent; idempotent; returns what it did) and `purgeExpired`. An invitation is open while `now < expiresAt`. Listings use keyset cursors (`src/circles/cursor.ts`, the same scheme as the user store: a limit that is not a positive whole number or a cursor the store did not give is a `RangeError`; a page that exactly fills the limit has no next cursor). **Why `removeUser` is in the port**: it fixes the hand-over rules once so the SQLite trigger of T-100 can be proved equal to it. **Suite** (49 cases): circles, caps, listing and paging stability, roles, last-owner including two owners removing or demoting each other at the same moment (exactly one succeeds) and eight owners removing the next one, invitations (repeat, caps, expiry boundary, listings), accept (including eight at once for one invitation, five at once for the last place, and a person accepting six at once under a cap of three), decline, revoke, purge, the hand-over cases, copies in and out, odd characters, and two stores sharing nothing. **33 deliberately broken stores** (a small naive store in `testing/naive-store.test-util.ts` with one switchable defect each, including three racy ones with a gap between check and act) are each caught, and the defect-free naive store passes everything. The memory store itself was mutation-checked with 50 defects; **4 survived at first and each got a case** (an invitation id already used, the hand-over tie by join time, members listed in id order whatever the join order, and a second spelling of a cursor) and are now caught. 3,638 tests in the gate. Next: T-099, the SQLite store, which must pass this suite unchanged.
- 2026-10-07 — SQLite circle store (T-099): `createSqliteCircleStore({ path?, busyTimeoutMs? })` in `src/circles/sqlite/` with its own entry point **`bipartite-graph/circles/sqlite`** (eighteenth API report; `node:sqlite` is only loaded through it, never through `bipartite-graph/circles`). **It passed the whole 50-case conformance suite unchanged, on `:memory:` and on a real file per test**, first time. **Schema decision**: circles live in `users.db` as **version 3 of the one user-database schema, owned by `src/users/sqlite/schema.ts`** (every store on a file must know every migration, or a file upgraded by one store would be refused by the other as "newer"); `USERS_SCHEMA_VERSION` is now 3 and `prepareUsersDatabase` is exported from `users/sqlite/index` for the circle store; the boundary rule now lets only `circles/sqlite/` (not other circles code) use `users/sqlite/index`. Tables: `circles`, `circle_members` (primary key circle and user, role checked to the four, index by user) and `circle_invitations` (**unique per circle and username**, username checked lower-case by the database, indexes by name, inviter and expiry); **members and invitations belong to their circle with `ON DELETE CASCADE`** (so deleting a circle in the database removes them, proved by deleting from the table directly) and **deliberately have no foreign key to `users`**, so the store works without a user row. Ids are ASCII by construction, so SQLite's binary text order is the code-unit order the suite requires. Every method runs without awaiting; anything with more than one statement is one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction that does the checks (last owner, both caps, accept-with-membership, the hand-over) inside it, so other processes cannot interleave either; a failure is a `STORAGE_ERROR` with a fixed message (never SQL or a value), tested with a value that makes the insert fail. `removeUser` is one transaction using the same hand-over order in SQL (`CASE role ... , joined_at, user_id`) and returns the same outcome as the memory store. Tested beyond the suite (14 tests): same file, application id and version as the user store, both stores working on one file, **a version 2 file is upgraded without touching its users or sessions**, refusal of the graph database and of a newer schema with the files untouched, mode 0600 and one file after a clean close, everything kept across close and reopen, the database's own checks (role, lower-case name, one invitation per name, a membership needs its circle), a lock held past the timeout is a clear error and the store works again, **kill -9 inside a write transaction** (only the committed circle remains; a companion run proves the child does write), **four processes accepting one invitation produce one membership**, and two processes creating circles and invitations at once lose none. Mutation-checked with 41 defects across the store and the schema: 38 caught at first; of the 3 survivors one is equivalent (a mutant that still returned the same not-found answer) and two got tests (the invitation listing at exactly its expiry time, which the suite now also checks for every store, and the exact fixed error text). 3,754 tests in the gate. Next: T-100, account deletion, which adds the database trigger and proves it equals `removeUser`.
- 2026-10-07 — Account deletion and circles (T-100): **schema version 4** of the user database adds the trigger `circles_leave_with_user` (`AFTER DELETE ON users`), so that **whoever deletes an account (the person, an admin, anything with the file open), its place in every circle goes with it in the same transaction**: (1) a circle the person solely owned passes to the longest-standing manager, else member, else observer (earliest join, then lowest user id); (2) a circle with nobody else in it ceases to exist (its members and invitations go with it by the cascade); (3) their memberships go; (4) invitations addressed to their username or sent by them go. The order matters and is commented in the schema: the heir is chosen while the person is still a member, and the circle is dissolved while they are still its only one. The trigger is the same operation as the circle store's `removeUser`, and **they are tested against each other**: 60 seeded random worlds (7 people, 6 circles, 25 invitations, random accepts and role changes, built identically in the memory store and in SQLite) have people removed one by one, the memory store through `removeUser` and SQLite by deleting the user row, and the **whole state must be equal after every removal** (circles, members, invitations and each person's circle list), plus an owner is always present and a removed person is nowhere. Also tested (12 further tests): memberships and both kinds of invitation removed with everything else untouched; the hand-over order and its ties; several owners need no hand-over; alone means the circle goes; a delete that is rolled back, or refused as `LAST_ADMIN`, changes nothing in any circle; deleting a user who does not exist, or whom no circle knows, changes nothing; **a version 3 file is upgraded and then behaves the same** (before the upgrade a deletion leaves orphans: they are not cleaned retroactively, noted in the Backlog); and **through the real user controller on the SQLite stack, both `deleteMe` and an admin's `deleteUser`**. Mutation-checked with 15 defects in the trigger; 13 caught; the 2 survivors are equivalent (the owner count test `= 1` versus `>= 1`, because the heir order puts another owner first, so promoting changes nothing; and swapping the last two statements). `USERS_SCHEMA_VERSION` is 4 (tests use the constant). **Limit, stated plainly**: this holds only with the SQLite stack (which is what the service uses); the memory user store and memory circle store are not linked, so tests that use both must call `removeUser` themselves. 3,826 tests in the gate.
- 2026-10-07 — Circle controller, part 1 (T-101): **`createCircleController({ users, directory, store, limits?, clock?, newCircleId? })`** in `src/circles/controller.ts` (public with `CircleView`, `MemberView`, `Page`, the defaults and the types; the API report is additive). `users` needs only the user controller's `resolve`; **`directory` is the user store's `get`** (the only way to put a username and display name to a user id, and only those two fields are ever copied out); nothing about the caller except their user id is used. Methods, each by session token: `create` (the creator becomes the owner; an id the controller mints, retried up to 3 times if taken; any `id`, `owner`, `role` or other field in the input is refused by name; 20 circles per person, settable, and a circle joined by invitation counts), `get`, `list` (my circles by id, with my role in each; a page is 50 by default and 100 at most; bad limits and cursors are `INVALID_INPUT` naming `limit` or `cursor`), `update` (owners and managers; `description: null` clears it), `delete` (owners; takes members and invitations with it), `members` (user id, username, display name, role, joined; a member whose account the directory no longer knows is listed by id without names), `leave` (anyone, but the only owner gets `LAST_OWNER` with the message "make someone else an owner, or delete the circle"). **Order of checks**: session, then "are you in this circle" (the one `NOT_FOUND` answer for a stranger, a made-up id, a malformed id and a non-text id, **identical for every method**, and a non-text id never reaches the store), then permission (`FORBIDDEN`), and only then the input is looked at, so a member sending nonsense is told `FORBIDDEN`, not `INVALID_INPUT`. **The role is read from the store on every request**, so a demoted manager loses the power at once. **Fixed messages**: any failure of the store, the session check or the directory is `STORAGE_ERROR` "the circle service could not complete the request", and a store that reports a storage error in its own words (a lock, a path) is replaced by the same fixed message. A signed-out request gets one `UNAUTHENTICATED` answer for every kind of bad token and never touches the store. Test fixtures in `controller.test-util.ts` (a fake user controller and directory, an injectable clock, and `addMember` which uses the store so tests do not wait for the invitation routes). 36 tests; 41 deliberate defects, 38 caught at first; of the 3 survivors, two got tests (a non-text circle id reaching the store, and the number of id attempts) and one is equivalent (the `leave` permission check, since every role may leave: kept so the table is the single place that says so). 3,862 tests in the gate.
- 2026-10-07 — Circle controller, part 2: invitations (T-102): six methods added to `createCircleController`, each by session token: **`invite(token, circleId, { username, role })`** (answer is always `{ invited: true }`), **`invitations(token, circleId, page?)`** (the circle's open ones, owners and managers), **`revokeInvitation(token, circleId, invitationId)`**, **`myInvitations(token, page?)`** (addressed to my username), **`accept(token, invitationId)`** (returns the circle as I now see it) and **`decline(token, invitationId)`**. New views: `InvitationView` (id, username, role, who sent it as user id plus display name, created, expires) and `MyInvitationView` (id, circle id and name, role, the sender's display name only, created, expires: **no sender id, no email, nothing about other members**). New options: `limits.maxMembersPerCircle` (50), `maxOpenInvitationsPerCircle` (50), `invitationsPerHour` (30) and `invitationDays` (7), all validated with a `TypeError`, and `newInvitationId` for tests. **Enumeration safety (D9, CR-NFR-03)**: `invite` never looks anyone up (a test records every directory lookup: none) and answers identically for an account that exists, an unknown name, someone already in, the inviter herself, a repeat and a repeat with another role; what the inviter can list afterwards shows invitations for all of them alike. **Order of checks** for `invite`: session, then "are you in this circle" (stranger gets `no such circle`), then the base right to invite at all (owner or manager, so a member sending nonsense is `FORBIDDEN`, not `INVALID_INPUT`), then the input, then the right to give *that* role (a manager offering `owner` or `manager` is `FORBIDDEN`), then the hourly allowance, then the store. **The hourly allowance (30) is used only by invitations that reach the store**: a refused, forbidden or invalid one does not use it; `THROTTLED` carries the wait. `revokeInvitation` checks the base right first (so a member is `FORBIDDEN` even for an id that does not exist), then that the invitation belongs to *this* circle before looking at its role (so what another circle holds is not hinted at by a `FORBIDDEN`), then the right to withdraw that role (a manager cannot withdraw an owner or manager invitation). `accept` and `decline` answer `no such invitation` for everything that is not the caller's own open invitation: someone else's, made-up, non-text, expired, withdrawn, already used, or for a circle that has gone; a full circle or a person at their circle limit is `LIMIT_REACHED` and leaves the invitation open; someone already in keeps their role; non-text invitation ids never reach the store. 38 further tests (74 in the controller files); 40 deliberate defects, 32 caught at first, then five survivors got tests (the base right to withdraw, the other-circle hint, non-text ids, the hourly default and the 50-people default); the remaining three are equivalent or defensive (the store answers the same message for a circle that vanished mid-request and for a missing invitation; a circle that vanished mid-listing is skipped, which the store's cascade makes impossible). 3,900 tests in the gate.
- 2026-10-07 — Circle controller, part 3: roles and removal (T-103), which completes the controller: **`changeRole(token, circleId, userId, { role })`** (returns the person as the roster shows them) and **`removeMember(token, circleId, userId)`**. Everything goes through the single permission table: owners give anyone else any role and remove anyone else; managers only move `member`/`observer` people between those two and remove only them; **nobody changes their own role** (a specific message: "nobody changes their own role: ask another owner") **and nobody removes themselves** ("to leave a circle, leave it"); members and observers can do neither. **Order of checks** (both): session, "are you in this circle" (a stranger gets `no such circle`), the base right (owner or manager, so a member is `FORBIDDEN` before anything else is looked at, even for a made-up person), then for `changeRole` the input (`INVALID_INPUT` naming `role`; any other field refused by name), then the person (`no such member` for someone not in the circle, a made-up id or a non-text id, which never reaches the store), then self, then the table for that person and that role. **The last-owner rule is the store's**, so it holds under races: two owners demoting or removing each other at the same moment leave exactly one success and one `LAST_OWNER` ("a circle must keep an owner: make someone else an owner first, or delete the circle"), and three owners removing the next one in a ring leave at least one owner; two owners removing the same person at once give one success and one `no such member`. The role is read afresh every request, so a demoted manager loses the power at once (tested for both methods) and a promoted member gains it. The roster now builds its rows with the same `memberView` helper. 26 tests in `controller-roles.test.ts` (99 across the controller files); 30 deliberate defects, 27 caught at first, the 3 survivors (a non-text id reaching the store when removing, the last-owner message on a removal race, and the result of a removal that lost the race) each got a test and are now caught. **Controller complete: T-101 to T-103.** 3,925 tests in the gate.
- 2026-10-07 — Circle and member routes (T-104): `createCircleRoutes({ circles, cookieName?, secureCookies? })` in `src/api/circle-routes.ts` (public with `CIRCLE_STATUS_OF`, `circleErrorResponse` and `CircleRoutesOptions`; API report additive). Nine routes: `POST|GET /api/circles` (201 `{ circle }`; list is `{ items, nextCursor }` with `limit` and `cursor`), `GET|PATCH|DELETE /api/circles/:id` (204 on delete), `GET /api/circles/:id/members` (paged), `PATCH /api/circles/:id/members/:userId` (`{ role }` → `{ member }`), `DELETE /api/circles/:id/members/:userId` (204) and `POST /api/circles/:id/leave` (204). **One status table for all circle routes (and for T-105's)**: 401 `UNAUTHENTICATED`, 403 `FORBIDDEN`, 404 `NOT_FOUND`, 409 `CONFLICT` and `LAST_OWNER`, 422 `INVALID_INPUT` (with `field`), 429 `LIMIT_REACHED` (no Retry-After) and `THROTTLED` (with a whole-second Retry-After, rounded up), 500 `STORAGE_ERROR`; a test pins that the table has exactly the controller's codes. The routes only translate: the session cookie is the only credential (an Authorization header or a `?token=` is ignored), a refused session clears a stale cookie (with `Secure` when configured), **unknown query parameters are refused by name on every route**, a GET or DELETE that carries body fields refuses them by name, and a POST or PATCH body is passed whole to the controller, which refuses unknown fields by name. A stranger gets `{ error: { code: NOT_FOUND, message: "no such circle" } }` from **every** route, identical to the answer for a made-up id (tested route by route). Ids in paths are service-generated so the router's pattern suffices: `..`, an encoded `..` or slash, a 200-character id, a double slash and a trailing slash are plain 404 routes that never reach a handler. Inherited and re-tested: cross-origin writes 403, a form post 415, `Cache-Control: no-store`, 405 with `Allow`. The roster is in user id order, which is random, so clients sort by name if they want that. **Scan at the end of the file** over every circle-route response of the run: no password, session token, `scrypt$`, `passwordHash`, email, `graphId` or `user-u...`. Helpers shared with T-105's tests are in `circle-routes.test-util.ts` (`startCircleApp`, `call`, `signIn`, `addMember`). 26 tests; 33 deliberate defects, 32 caught, the survivor is equivalent (an empty `limit=` read as NaN or as 0 is refused either way, as for the read routes). 3,951 tests in the gate.
- 2026-10-07 — Invitation routes (T-105): `createInvitationRoutes({ circles, cookieName?, secureCookies? })` in `src/api/invitation-routes.ts` (public; additive API report), built on the helpers of `circle-routes.ts` (`circleRoute`, one status table, `pageOf`), so both route sets behave identically: `POST /api/circles/:id/invitations` (**202 `{ invited: true }`**), `GET /api/circles/:id/invitations` (paged), `DELETE /api/circles/:id/invitations/:invitationId` (204), `GET /api/invitations` (mine, paged), `POST /api/invitations/:id/accept` (200 `{ circle }`) and `POST /api/invitations/:id/decline` (204). **The invite response is byte-for-byte the same** (status, headers apart from the date, and body `{"invited":true}`) for an existing account, an unknown name, someone already in, the inviter herself, a repeat and a differently-cased repeat (tested over six targets; what the inviter lists afterwards shows all four distinct names alike). Accepting or declining anything that is not your own open invitation is `404 no such invitation` whatever the reason (someone else's, made-up, expired, withdrawn, used, circle gone), and a stranger on the circle routes is told `no such circle`. A full circle, or a person already in the most circles allowed, is `429 LIMIT_REACHED` and leaves the invitation open; the hourly allowance is `429 THROTTLED` with `Retry-After: 3600` for a person who has used it, and someone else is not affected. `accept`, `decline` and withdrawing take no body (a field is refused by name and nothing happens); unknown query parameters are refused by name; odd ids (`..`, encoded slashes, 200 characters, a double slash) are plain 404 routes; wrong methods are 405 with `Allow`; stale cookies are cleared. A lesson for tests that move the clock a week: sessions idle out after 30 minutes, so they sign in again (a helper does). The end-of-file scan of every circle and invitation response finds no password, session token, hash, email, graph id or `user-u...`; the inviter sees who sent each invitation by user id and display name, the invitee only the sender's display name. 21 tests; 19 deliberate defects, 17 caught at first, then the 2 survivors (a withdrawal carrying body fields; a decline that actually accepted, unnoticed because the status looked right) each got a test and are caught. 3,972 tests in the gate.

The open-invitation cap (50) and the hourly invitation allowance (30) stay fixed in v1, as the spec says. **`docs.test.ts` carries `NOT_DOCUMENTED_YET` again** with the three names, exactly as for T-092: they are read and checked but the README does not describe them until T-108 (the docs test fails if one is documented early or left out late). Tested through the running service on real files over real HTTP (9 tests in `circles.test.ts`): create, invite, accept, change a role, remove and leave, with the only owner refused (409); a stranger and **the platform admin** both get "no such circle" from the circle routes; a restart keeps circles, members and open invitations; a clean close leaves only `graphs.db` and `users.db`; the three settings take effect (one circle per person, two people per circle, invitations lasting two days, and a full circle leaves the invitation open); two accounts with same-named circles stay apart; **deleting an account through `DELETE /api/me` and through an admin's `DELETE /api/users/:id` leaves every circle, hands a sole-owned circle to the only other person, dissolves one the person was alone in, and removes the invitations sent to or by them** (the trigger of T-100, exercised through the real service); stale cookies on circle and invitation routes are cleared with `Secure` when configured; a scan of every circle and invitation response finds no password, hash or email. Lesson: the first account is the admin and cannot delete itself while it is the only one, so tests that delete an account register someone first. Config tests (3 new) and the full-environment, `VARIABLES` and defaults tests were extended. 20 deliberate defects across the wiring and the parser; the 2 survivors (the secure flag on the circle and invitation routes) got a test. 3,984 tests in the gate.

declining, withdrawing and waiting ending an invitation and an invitation not usable twice; **the permission table over HTTP for every role** (members and observers forbidden everywhere, a manager over members and observers only, an owner anything, nobody over their own role); **a stranger and the platform admin getting exactly the answer for a made-up circle from all ten routes**; **byte-identical invite answers** (status, body, content-type and length) for an existing account, an unknown name, someone already in, the inviter, a repeat and a differently-cased repeat; **eight simultaneous accepts of one invitation making one membership**; the 30-an-hour invitation allowance (counting the invitation sent while the team formed), the 50-open-invitations cap and the 20-circles cap, each answered with 429 and the right code; a restart keeping circles, members and open invitations with a clean close leaving two files; and a final scan of every circle and invitation response of the file (over 150) finding no password, hash, email, token, graph id or `user-u...`. **R-004's review**: the threat table has a *Covered by* column on all 11 threats, a *Where each criterion is checked* table for CR-AC-01 to 12, and nine residual risks (an invitation to a name with no account can reach a later registrant within 7 days, which is the price of not revealing names; registration already reveals taken names (R-002) so the identical answer protects less than it seems; every member sees the others' usernames and display names; the hourly allowance is in memory and many accounts multiply the limits; HTTP races are best effort, the strict ones are proved at the store and controller; nobody is notified of an invitation; the files are readable in plain text by whoever can read them; sharing needs its own review; a departed person remembers what they saw). **`review.test.ts` now covers R-002, R-003 and R-004** (241 tests): every referenced test file and name must exist, every threat and criterion needs a reference, the 12 criteria must all be there, and the risks must be listed; checked by renaming a reference, emptying a threat's references, deleting an acceptance row and renaming the risks heading: each failed. Mutation-checked: 12 production defects through the end-to-end test (a manager inviting owners, no membership check, no hourly allowance, no last-owner rule on removal, an accept that keeps the invitation, no circle cap, no invitation cap, the account-deletion trigger without removing members, a wrong heir order, no invitation routes, a blank directory) — 11 caught; the survivor (the table's own self check) is equivalent at this level because the controller refuses a change to oneself before the table is asked, and the table check is covered by `authorise.test.ts`. 4,076 tests in the gate.

Seven new docs tests check the section against the code: the routes listed are exactly the fifteen the server has, the role names are exactly the four in the code, the statuses mentioned are exactly the values of the status table, the numbers (20 circles, 50 people, 50 open invitations, 30 invitations an hour, 7 days, name 80 and description 500 characters) come from the code's own defaults, the sentences a reader must not miss are present, and every setting and command it names exists. 23 deliberate README edits (a route removed or invented, a role removed or invented, a status added or dropped, every number, the no-sharing, admin, acceptance, same-answer, stranger, owner, own-role, plain-text and notification sentences, a settings row, a default and a misspelt variable) were each caught. Also brought up to date: `spec.md` (C2 is done; what comes next is restricted access between circle members, which needs pseudonymisation and the legal groundwork first) and the container diagram (the Circle Controller no longer says "being built" or "planned"). **C2 is complete: T-096 to T-108.** 4,083 tests in the gate.

Proposed by me, to confirm in T-109 (a new `specs/R-005-web-app.md`): served by the same service on the same origin (no CORS, because the cookie is `SameSite=Strict` and cross-origin writes are refused), source in `web/`, served from a fixed list of files read at start, a strict Content-Security-Policy on pages (own-origin scripts and styles only, no inline code, no framing) while API answers keep `default-src 'none'`, DOM built from text only with guard tests, the password never stored, the session token never visible to the page, signed-in state from `GET /api/me`, sign-in after registering, and an on-screen note that the first account becomes the administrator. Needs one change in the API kit (T-110: non-JSON answers with a route-chosen CSP); nothing new on the account API. Order: T-109 spec and diagram, T-110 kit, T-111 web file routes, T-112 client logic, T-113 pages, T-114 serve it and a hand check in Chrome, T-115 security review, T-116 README. **Backlog items added by the owner**: 6 enhance the PWA with circles; 7 enhance it with text note capture and categorisation into the brain; 8 enhance it with "request update", confirmed as *a circle member asks another member for an update on a person* (depends on items 6, the restricted sharing under item 3, a request-and-notice mechanism, and the pseudonymisation and legal groundwork); and 9 (mine, from the owner's choice to leave it out) make the PWA installable and offline-aware.
- 2026-10-08 — Web app specified (T-109, documentation plus one test change): **`specs/R-005-web-app.md`** holds 13 decisions (D1 to D3 the owner's: only register, sign in and out and a temporary home page; plain HTML, CSS and JavaScript with no build and no dependency; a plain web app first with no manifest, service worker or offline; D4 to D13 mine, standing unless changed: served by the same service on the same origin because the cookie is `SameSite=Strict` and there is deliberately no CORS; source in `web/`, served from a fixed list of files read at start; a strict Content-Security-Policy on pages while API answers keep `default-src 'none'`; elements built with `textContent` only; the password only in the field and the one request; no browser storage or cookie access at all and the page never sees the token; sign-in after registering; an on-screen note that the first account becomes the administrator; a home page marked temporary showing only display name and username; one page whose screens are states, not addresses), goals and non-goals, definitions, ten functional requirements (WA-FR), eight non-functional (WA-NFR, including the exact CSP and accessibility, responsive and light/dark requirements), a nine-row threat table (covering tests come in T-115), a table of what the person is told for each situation (422 with and without a field, 409, 401, 403 closed registration, 429 with the wait, 500, unreachable), and ten acceptance checks (WA-AC). Only routes that exist are used: register, login, logout and `/api/me`. `spec.md` links R-005 and lists `web/` in its architecture mapping. **Container diagram**: a Web App container (HTML, CSS, JavaScript; marked Planned until T-113 creates `web/`) between the person and the HTTP API, with the API serving its files. **Architecture test** now also checks `Code: web/...` folders as well as `src/...` (a container marked Planned is exempt; unmarking the Web App makes the test fail because `web/` does not exist yet, and an orphaned or mistyped `src/` container still fails; a typo in the planned `web/` is exempt by design until the word is removed). 4,085 tests in the gate.
- 2026-10-08 — Non-JSON answers in the API kit (T-110): a route may now reply with a **document**: `ApiResponse.document = { kind, text, policy? }`, built with **`documentResponse(document, status = 200)`** (public, with `DOCUMENT_TYPES`, `policyProblem` and the types; the API report is additive). **A route names a kind, never a header value**: `html`, `js`, `css`, `text` or `json`, each mapped to one fixed `Content-Type` (UTF-8), so a content type cannot be made up from anything a request said. The route may also choose the **`Content-Security-Policy`**, but `policyProblem` accepts only a policy that **begins with `default-src 'none'`** (so it can only allow more by naming it), is **plain printable ASCII** (no CR, LF, tab, DEL or other control character, so it cannot split a reply), is at most 2,048 characters, and allows **no inline or evaluated code** (`'unsafe-inline'`, `'unsafe-eval'`, `'unsafe-hashes'`, `'wasm-unsafe-eval'`, case-insensitive) and **no wildcard origin, whole scheme (`https:`, `http:`) or `*.` host**. `documentResponse` checks everything at once and throws `TypeError` (a kind that does not exist, text that is not a string, a status of 204, 304 or outside 200 to 599, a bad policy), so a mistake in a route stops the program when the route is made; **the server checks again when it sends** and turns anything wrong (a body and a document together, a bad kind, text or policy, status 204) into the plain JSON 500 "internal error" with the API's own lock-down, no cookie and no trace of the mistake. Every document still carries `Cache-Control: no-store`, `nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, a correct `Content-Length` (bytes, not characters), and the Host, Origin, method (HEAD and OPTIONS are 405 with `Allow`) and error rules of every other route apply unchanged; **API answers and every error keep `default-src 'none'` and stay JSON**. The plan said "refused at build time of the route"; that is the `TypeError` in `documentResponse`, with the send-time check as the second line, since a handler's reply can be built later. 21 tests in `documents.test.ts` (241 in the API folder); 32 deliberate defects, 27 caught at first; of the 5 survivors two are equivalent (an empty policy is also refused by the character rule; a bad kind or non-string text makes Node throw before any byte is written, which the server turns into the same 500) and two exposed gaps (a DEL character, and control characters after the first directive, which the start check had been hiding) that now have tests. 4,106 tests in the gate.
- 2026-10-08 — Web file routes (T-111): **`createWebRoutes({ files })`** in `src/api/web-routes.ts` (public with `WEB_POLICY` and the types) turns a given list of files `{ path, kind, text }` into one GET route each and nothing else, every file sent with the web policy `default-src 'none'; script-src 'self'; style-src 'self'; img-src 'self' data:; connect-src 'self'; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'` (pinned exactly by a test, and checked by the kit's own `policyProblem`). The list is checked when the routes are made (`TypeError`): there must be a page at `/` and only the page is html; a path is `/` or one segment of lower-case letters, digits, `.`, `_` and `-` starting with a letter or digit, at most 64 characters, with no `..`, no trailing dot, no leading dot or underscore; no path twice; a kind of html, js, css or text only; text is a string of at most 512 K characters. **Because the files are given, not looked up, there is no path to walk**: unlisted names, other cases, folders, dot segments, encodings (`%2e%2e`, `%2f`, `%00`), double slashes and long names are all plain JSON 404s that keep the API's `default-src 'none'` (tested over 23 paths); HEAD, OPTIONS, POST, PUT, PATCH and DELETE are 405 with `Allow: GET`; the Host rule applies (a look-alike host does not get the page); and API routes beside them keep the lock-down. **Reading the files is the service's job** (the API and the other components do not read files): **`loadWebFiles(dir, specs)`** in `src/service/web-files.ts` reads exactly the listed names, once, as UTF-8; each must be a plain name (letters, digits, `.`, `_`, `-`, no folder, no `..`), an ordinary file (`lstat`: a link or a folder is refused, so a link cannot point outside), at most 512 KB; a missing or odd file stops the start with a message naming the file and the folder; anything else in the folder (a `secret.txt`, a `.env`, a subfolder) is never read, and an end-to-end test shows the server sends the listed files' bytes and 404s the unlisted one. **Two router changes were needed and are tested**: the root path `/` can now be matched (it has no segments; `//` is still refused) and a route path may contain dots in literal segments (`/app.js`, `/style.v2.css`) while `.` and `..` segments, a trailing slash and dots in a parameter name are refused when the routes are checked. The actual list of files and the folder arrive in T-114 (the file names are chosen in T-112 and T-113). 21 tests in `web-routes.test.ts`, 7 in `web-files.test.ts`, 3 more in `router.test.ts`; 31 deliberate defects, 28 caught at first; of the 3 survivors one exposed a gap (a leading underscore, now refused and tested) and two are equivalent (text that is not a string is also refused by `documentResponse`; a dot in a parameter name is also refused by the name check). 4,123 tests in the gate.
- 2026-10-08 — Web client logic (T-112): three browser modules in `web/` (plain ES modules, no dependency, no page access), unit-tested in the gate by `web/*.test.mjs` (vitest and eslint now cover `web/`; the tests must stay out of the package, which T-114 handles with an exclusion in `files`). **`api-client.js`**: `createApiClient({ fetchFn })` gives `me`, `register`, `login`, `logout`, each returning `{ ok: true, value }` or `{ ok: false, error: { kind, message, field?, retryAfterSeconds? } }` and **never throwing** (a fetch that rejects, a body that cannot be read, a non-response, bad JSON and every odd shape are handled). Requests go to absolute paths on the same address only, `credentials: 'same-origin'`, `cache: 'no-store'`, `redirect: 'error'`, no Authorization or token, a body and content type only when there is a body, only the fields the call is meant to send. Answers: a 2xx with a user shaped `{ id, username, displayName }` becomes **a frozen user with only those three fields** (no email, role or graph id); anything else, including a 3xx with a user in it, is a server problem. Kinds and words: `signed-out` (401, not shown), `credentials` (401 on sign-in, the service's single message), `invalid` (422: the service's message, with the field only if it is one of username, displayName, email or password), `taken` (409 on register, on the username field), `closed` (403 on register), `forbidden` (other 403), `throttled` (429: "Wait N seconds" with N from a strictly parsed `Retry-After` of 1 to 86,400, else a general wait message), `server` (everything else, with a fixed message and nothing from inside) and `network`. The service's own words are shown only after control characters, separators and the byte order mark become spaces and the text is cut at 300 characters; markup stays the text it is. **`forms.js`**: early feedback only, the service stays the authority: the username (trimmed, lower-cased, 3 to 32 of `a-z0-9._-`), the display name (trimmed, 1 to 80 characters counted as characters, no control characters), an optional email (the service's own shape, at most 254) and the password (12 to 128 characters **counted after NFKC normalisation, as the service counts**, never trimmed); every wrong field reported at once, and a login check that only asks for something to send. **`session.js`**: `createSession({ api })` holds `{ screen: loading | signed-in | signed-out | unreachable, user, busy, notice }`, with `start` (also the retry), `register` (checks, registers, then **signs in with the same values**, and says "your account was made, but signing you in failed" if that step fails), `signIn`, `signOut` (done also when the service says you were not signed in; if it cannot be reached you stay signed in and the notice says you may still be), `getState` and `subscribe` (frozen snapshots, a broken listener does not matter, unsubscribing works). **One request at a time**: a second call meanwhile is refused as `busy` and sends nothing, and busy is cleared after a failure or a throwing API. **The password is in no state, snapshot or result.** The file names are `api-client.js`, `forms.js` and `session.js`; T-113 adds `index.html`, `style.css` and `app.js`. 69 tests; 68 deliberate defects across the three modules, 60 caught at first, then 7 survivors got tests (the separator and DEL characters in messages; a response that is not a response; a 3xx treated as success; the NFKC count, tested with a ligature and with letters plus separate accents; a password trimmed on the way to sign-in; unsubscribing) and are caught; the last, a stale notice kept when starting again, is equivalent because `start` clears the notice first. 4,192 tests in the gate.

- 2026-10-08 — The circles API client (T-118): **`web/circles-client.js`**, `createCirclesClient({ fetchFn })`, wraps the fifteen circle and invitation routes (`listCircles`, `createCircle`, `getCircle`, `updateCircle`, `deleteCircle`, `listMembers`, `changeRole`, `removeMember`, `leaveCircle`, `invite`, `listInvitations`, `withdrawInvitation`, `myInvitations`, `acceptInvitation`, `declineInvitation`), never throwing, returning `{ ok, value }` or `{ ok: false, error: { kind, message, ... } }`. **Every request now goes through one shared requester**: `api-client.js` was refactored (behaviour unchanged, all its tests untouched) to export `createRequester(fetchFn)`, with the same settings for every call (same address by path only, `same-origin` credentials, no cache, no redirects, no Authorization), and the account client and the circles client both use it. **Ids are checked against the exact shapes the service makes** (`c`, `i` or `u` plus 16 lower-case letters or digits) **before any path is built**: a bad id (empty, wrong length or case, a path part such as `../x`, `%2f`, `?x=1`, a non-string, an invitation id where a circle id goes) returns a not-found and sends nothing; the same shapes are checked again on every id in an answer. **Only the fields each call is meant to send are sent** (an update sends only what it is given, and `description: null` clears it; an update with nothing in it is not sent), and **every answer is cut down to the fields the app uses** (no graph id, no email, no hash; an invitation to me carries only the sender's display name, never their id); a page is refused whole if any item, role, count or cursor is wrongly shaped, and a missing description stays a missing key. Paging builds `?limit=&cursor=` only when given; a limit outside 1 to 100, or a cursor that is not made of letters, digits, `_` and `-`, is refused before sending, naming the field. **Refusals become kinds and plain words (R-006)**: 401 `signed-out` ("Your session has ended. Sign in again."), 403 `forbidden` (our words, never the service's), 404 `not-found` with `what` set to circle, invitation or member and the three sets of words, 409 `last-owner` (the service's words, with a fallback) or `conflict`, 422 `invalid` with the field named only if it is a field of that call (so a stray field name from the service is dropped), 429 `limit` (the service's words) or `throttled` (the wait from `Retry-After`, as whole seconds), everything else `server`, an unreachable service `network`. The service's own words are cleaned (controls become spaces, cut at 300 characters, markup stays text). **Against the real service** (four accounts over real HTTP; 5 more tests in `web/service.test.mjs`): a whole circle (create with a trimmed name, invite with the name in capitals, the invitee sees the sender's display name and accepts, another declines, a withdrawn invitation disappears for the invitee, roles change and a manager is refused over an owner, a removal, leaving, the only owner refused in the service's words, delete), a stranger's circle and a made-up one reading the same with a hostile id never leaving the page, a limit (`at most 1 circle`) and field problems beside the right field, and markup in a display name, circle name and description coming back as text. **The served list, the README table and the package check now say nine files**; `pages.test` now expects the account, circle and invitation routes by path (and R-005's review reference to that test was renamed to match; the docs test derives the file count from the list). 68 tests in `circles-client.test.mjs` (the file has 29 cases) plus the 5; 57 deliberate defects in the two modules: 55 caught at first and then the shape of a circle id with an upper-case body, now tested; the last (`encodeURIComponent` on a cursor that is already limited to URL-safe characters) is equivalent. 4,402 tests in the gate.

Round trips and 200 random odd inputs always give one of four address forms. A length cap was removed as dead code (mutation showed it equivalent: exact matching already refuses long input). 11 mutants, all caught. Ten web files now; served list, README, and file-list tests updated.

Nothing beside your own row, and an unknown self flag is no. 16 mutants: 14 caught; two equivalent (a type check that falls to the default case anyway, removed; invite and withdraw share one rule). - 2026-10-08 — Circles list and invitations logic (T-121): `web/circles-session.js`, `createCirclesSession({ client, onSignedOut? })` over the circles client, plus `checkCircle` (early checks: trimmed name 1 to 80 characters counted as characters, no control characters; description up to 500). **Two areas, each one request at a time** (`circles`: list, show more, create, leave; `invitations`: list, count, show more, accept, decline); a second call meanwhile returns `busy` and sends nothing. State is a frozen snapshot `{ circles, invitations, busy }`. **The invitation count is only a number the last answer gave** (`items.length` of the answer, `atLeast` when there is another page); a failed refresh or "more" sets it to null, never keeping the old number. Views are as old as their last load: `createCircle` and a successful accept mark the circle list `stale` (the page reloads it; `loadCircles` clears the mark), `leaveCircle` and a gone invitation/circle (`not-found`) reload the list so the stale row disappears, accept/decline reload invitations and count, and a reload that fails after a good action is reported as `listProblem` while the action still counts. A full circle (`limit`) on accept reloads and keeps the invitation. `reset()` (sign-out) forgets everything and drops answers still on their way, and an old request finishing after a reset does not clear a newer request's busy mark (found by mutation). Twelve web files now. 35 unit tests over a fake client plus 2 against the real service (create, show more with a real second page, invite, count not live until refreshed, accept, decline, withdrawn-behind-your-back, leave, the only owner, the circle limit, signed-out). 40 mutants: 36 caught at first; the survivors were two equivalents (a redundant outer drop and a redundant `!result.ok`, both removed) and two real gaps (busy mark after reset, frozen snapshot) that now have tests. 4,461 tests in the gate.
- 2026-10-08 — One circle's logic (T-122): `web/circle-session.js`, `createCircleSession({ client, onSignedOut? })` (a separate file from the list logic; `circles-session.js` now exports `failed`), plus `checkInvite` (trimmed lower-cased username of 3 to 32 of `a-z0-9._-`, and a role the person may give). `open(circleId, selfUserId)` loads the circle, the roster (50 a page, "show more") and, only for owners and managers, the open invitations; a circle that cannot be found, whatever the reason, is `gone` with the one set of words. The snapshot carries `controls`, `rolesToOffer` and, on every roster row, `self` and `controls` (from `permissions.js`, hints only). **The person's own role is whatever the service last said**, so a demotion shows at the next load; a refusal for lack of rights shows the service's words and refreshes everything (circle, roster, invitations), a gone person reloads roster and circle, a gone invitation reloads the invitations, a gone circle becomes `gone`. Actions: `update` (checks early; an empty description removes it; shows the service's answer), `invite` (the notice is only `Invitation recorded for "name".`, the same for every name; a manager cannot pick owner or manager and nothing is sent), `withdraw`, `changeRole`, each reloading what it changed. **Two-step questions are state**: `askRemove(userId)`, `askLeave()`, `askDelete()` (refused while a request is out or no circle is open), `cancel()` (silent when nothing is asked), `confirmAction()` (closes the question whatever the answer; delete or leave success gives `goTo: 'list'` and status `deleted`/`left`; the only owner is refused in the service's words and the circle stays). One request at a time; `open` or `reset` drops answers for the circle that was open and an old request does not clear a newer one's busy mark. Thirteen web files now. 50 unit tests plus 2 against the real service with three accounts and the administrator (the whole story with every refusal in the service's words, a stranger's circle reading the same as a made-up one, markup staying text, a hostile id never leaving the page). 56 mutants: 49 caught at first; 7 survivors were real gaps (busy mark after another open, onSignedOut for good answers, status after a failed refresh, invitations hidden after a demotion found by another action, asking while busy for remove and delete, silent cancel) and each got a test. The roster arrives in user-id order, which is random, so the page should sort by name if it wants an order (tests sort). 4,513 tests in the gate.
- 2026-10-08 — Pages for home, circles and invitations (T-123): `index.html` gains a navigation bar (Home, Circles, Invitations; the current one has `aria-current`), four views inside the signed-in screen (`view-home`, `view-circles`, `view-circle`, `view-invitations`), the create-circle form and two `<template>` row elements (no ids, no hrefs; the script sets the link address from `hashFor`). New modules: **`router`-driven `mount.js`** (`mount(document, fetchFn, env)` where `env = { getHash, setHash, onHashChange }` is given by the entry point; without it the app stays on home; **only `app.js` touches `window.location`**, a guard test says so), **`circles-pages.js`** (circles list with show more, create form with early checks and the service's words beside the field or at the top, invitations list with accept/decline, the home count and Refresh; rows cloned from templates and filled with `textContent`, lists swapped with `replaceChildren` since `appendChild` is forbidden by the guard), **`circles-view.js`** (pure words: titles, the heading id, which link is current, row sentences, the count sentence, UTC dates). On each change of screen the main heading takes the focus and the tab title follows; showing the same address again does not take the focus back; a hostile or unknown hash is home; while signed out the address is kept and applies after signing in. Each visit loads the list again (views are as old as their last load); there is no polling (tested). Accepting goes to that circle, creating goes to the new circle, declining returns the focus to the heading, a failed accept/decline puts the service's words in `invitations-error` and focuses it. **A found and fixed fault**: a service that says `/api/me` is fine but circle requests are 401 would have made the page loop forever (a circle request that finds the session ended re-asks `/api/me`, which re-showed the screen, which asked again); now it re-checks **once per sign-in** (`rechecked`, reset when the sign-in screen shows). Another real fault found by the tests: a route called `toString` or a role called `__proto__` read inherited properties; the view helpers now use own keys only. The circle screen is a placeholder ("This screen is being built.") until T-124. - 2026-10-09 — The notes API client (T-130): **`web/notes-client.js`**, `createNotesClient({ fetchFn })` over the shared requester, never throwing: `mode`, `propose`, `getProposal`, `approve`, `reject`, `categories`, `categoryItems(id)`, `item(id)`. **Ids**: a proposal id must match `prop-` plus lower-case letters, digits and hyphens before it reaches a path (anything else, including a missing one, is "gone" and nothing is sent); **a category or item id is any non-empty text of at most 256 characters (counted as characters, no lone surrogate) and goes only in the query string, encoded** (tested with `a/b?c=d&e#f %é😀<b>..` round-tripping through the real service and a 257-character one refused locally). **The note is checked first**: trimmed, 1 to 8,000 characters counted as characters (an 8,000-emoji note is sent, 8,001 is refused naming `text`), and only `{ text }` is sent. **Answers are cut down** to the fields the page uses, frozen all the way down (operations and their data too); a proposal is `{ id, createdAt, expiresAt, mode, text, summary, operations, rationale? }` (an empty rationale is left out), approve gives `{ id, applied, summary }`, categories give `{ id, name?, itemCount, itemCountCapped? }`, category items and one item carry `dataTruncated` and `moreCategories` only when true; anything not shaped right (bad mode, an operation that is not `upsertNode` or `link`, a wrong-typed summary list) is a server problem. **Refusals**: 401 `signed-out`; 404 `not-found` with `what` proposal, category or item in **our** words ("That proposal is gone or has expired. Make it again.", "Not found: it may have been removed.") so the three kinds of missing read the same as they do for someone else's; 410 `expired`; 409 `refused` (the service's cleaned words); 422 `invalid` naming a field only if that call has it; 429 `limit` with the service's words and the wait when given; 502 and 503 `model` with a `reason` (timeout, refused, busy, error), the service's fixed words and the wait. **Against the real service with two accounts** (demo model): mode, propose (the 15-minute expiry shows), look, approve, browse categories, a category's items and an item with weights; the other person gets exactly the answer for a made-up proposal on get, approve and reject, an empty brain, and "not found" for the first person's category and item ids; a pending limit of 1 is the service's own `limit` words, rejecting writes nothing, signed-out calls say so. **Served files are seventeen**; the page-guard test's list of requested paths gained the capture and graph routes (its title is unchanged because the R-005 review points at it). 62 mutants: 40 caught at first; the survivors were a note limit tested against its own constant (now literal), unfrozen nested data (now asserted), a no-op `encodeURIComponent` on an already URL-safe cursor (equivalent, kept as defence) and a redundant guard (simplified). One earlier run of the mutation helper used a test filter that only ran 3 tests and reported false survivors; the helper now runs the unit file alone. 153 files, 4,739 tests in the gate.
- 2026-10-09 — Capture logic (T-131): **`web/capture-session.js`**, `createCaptureSession({ client, onSignedOut?, onWritten? })` over the notes client. State (frozen snapshots): `mode` (`unknown`, `loading`, `known` with `demo` or `anthropic`, or `error` with the service's words), the one current `proposal`, the last `outcome` (`written` with how many operations and what they were, `rejected`, `expired`, or `gone`), `phase` (`form`, `preview`, `done`), `canPropose` (the mode is known, no proposal is pending, nothing is in flight) and `busy`. **Nothing can be proposed until the mode is known** (`mode-unknown`, and no request of any kind is sent), and a second proposal while one is pending is refused; the note is checked by the client, and a field problem comes back beside its field. **Approve** writes once: success clears the proposal, records the outcome and calls `onWritten` (so the brain view can be marked out of date); a refused write (409) keeps the proposal in the service's words for a retry or a reject; a 410 is `expired` and a 404 is `gone` (the service forgot it, for example after a restart), both ending the proposal in the same words and writing nothing; other failures (server, network, signed out) keep it. **Reject** discards (a gone one ends in words too). `startAgain()` closes the outcome without sending anything (not while a proposal is pending or a request is out). One request at a time; an answer that arrives after `reset()` becomes `dropped` and is neither shown nor reported as a sign-out (a distinction the list logic of T-121 does not make; harmless there because the page re-checks only once per sign-in). `reset()` forgets everything including the mode (sign-out, or leaving the capture screen). Eighteen web files now. 27 unit tests over a fake client and 3 against the real service with the demo model (reject leaves the brain empty, approve writes three operations and the brain gains its category, a proposal the service dropped ends in words, signed-out is reported). 38 mutants, all caught. 154 files, 4,769 tests in the gate.
- 2026-10-09 — Brain logic (T-132): **`web/brain-session.js`**, `createBrainSession({ client, onSignedOut? })` over the notes client; read-only. State (frozen snapshots): `view` (`categories`, `category` or `item`, from the selection), `needsLoad` (never loaded, or marked out of date), `categories` (status, items with `{ id, name?, itemCount, itemCountCapped? }`, `nextCursor`, error), `category` (`{ id, name?, status: loading | loaded | gone | error, items, nextCursor, error }`) and `item` (`{ id, status, detail: { item, categories with weights, moreCategories? }, error }`). **The selection is page state, never an address**: `selectCategory(id)` opens one (the name from the list, else from the service; leaves any open item), `selectItem(id)` opens one, `back()` goes item to its list and list to the categories without sending anything (refused while a request is out), `load()` reloads the categories and returns to the list, `markStale()` makes the next visit reload (the capture screen's `onWritten` will call it). A category or item that is gone ends in the one set of words with a way back; other failures show the service's words and keep what was already shown when a "more" fails; shortened data keeps its mark. Pages are 50. One request at a time; an answer after `reset()` is dropped quietly (kind `dropped`, not a sign-out); `reset()` forgets everything. **Against the real service**: 60 plain categories plus five awkward ids (a slash, `?`, `&`, `#`, spaces, `%`, an accent, an emoji, markup, `..`; leading and trailing spaces; capitals; CJK; 256 characters) seeded through a second connection to `graphs.db`: the categories page (50 then 15, all distinct), each awkward category's items, each item with its category and weight, the way back at each step; another person sees an empty brain and "not found" for every one of those ids; and an approved note shows after `markStale()` and a reload. **Seeding lesson**: a mutation needs `kind: 'mutation'`, and two items cannot share an id, so the 256-character case needed items with different ids. Nineteen web files now. 28 unit tests and 2 service tests; 39 mutants, 34 caught at first and the 4 real survivors got tests (loading again leaves the selection, the list's name is kept when the service gives none, listeners hear the loading step and each back step); one is equivalent (clearing the item twice in `back`). 155 files, 4,799 tests in the gate.

Run five times for flakiness; 11 production defects broken through the journey, 8 caught by it and the 3 survivors are covered by unit tests (the mode guard, the submit button, the expiry words) or by the awkward-id tests (a no-op encoding). Two clicks by element reference worked this time and one by coordinates needed a retry (the page had not finished loading), as before. A temporary folder of the hand-check data was left under the system temp directory (`$TMPDIR/tmp.dGYdas3LbN`; removal is refused by the tool's safety check): please delete it if you wish; it holds only throw-away accounts. 159 files, 4,877 tests in the gate.

- 2026-10-09 — **Shared circle brains planned (S1, T-139 to T-152), owner decisions**: a grant is **read-only**; it names **chosen categories** (the items filed under them come with them, the rest stays private); **the brain's owner grants to a named circle member, one direction per grant**, can narrow or withdraw at any time, and roles do not carry access; **pseudonymisation and the legal groundwork are release gates**, recorded and not built here (read-only sharing sends nothing new to a model). Proposed by me, to confirm in T-139 (a new `specs/R-008-shared-brains.md`, also amending R-004's "circles share no data yet"): grants live inside one circle between two current members, are stored with the circle store (users.db schema v5, so membership and grants change in one transaction), end automatically when either person leaves or is removed, an account is deleted or the circle is deleted, hold at most 200 categories, are read live and re-checked on every read; an item is visible if it is filed under at least one shared category and its detail names only the shared categories (a private category's name or existence never leaks, counts include only shared categories); no re-sharing; every not-shared case reads like a made-up id; the platform admin has no access; no notification; a new component `src/sharing/` composes the circle store, the user controller and the graph reads so that `src/circles/` still cannot read a graph. Order: T-139 spec, T-140 and T-141 grants in the circle store (memory and SQLite), T-142 and T-143 the sharing component (grant management, reading), T-144 and T-145 the routes, T-146 and T-147 the web client and logic, T-148 and T-149 the two pages (T-148 replaces the "circles share no data yet" notice with accurate words and updates the tests that assert it), T-150 serve and hand check, T-151 security review, T-152 README. Backlog item 3 now points at S1 and lists what is still later (writing via approval with "request update", relationship labels, invitation notifications). Release gates recorded: pseudonymisation, a data processing agreement, lawful basis, a risk assessment and a statement of who may use the service.
- 2026-10-10 — Shared circle brains specified (T-139, documentation only): **`specs/R-008-shared-brains.md`** holds 16 decisions (D1 to D4 the owner's: read-only; chosen categories; the owner grants to a named member, one direction, roles carry nothing, withdrawable at any time; pseudonymisation and the legal groundwork as release gates; D5 to D16 mine, standing unless changed: a grant lives in one circle between two current members and never to oneself; it ends in the same transaction as leaving, removal, account deletion or circle deletion and is stored with the circle store in users.db schema v5; at most 200 categories, setting replaces the set, empty withdraws; categories must exist in the owner's graph when granted; reads are live and re-checked every time; an item is visible if filed under at least one shared category, its detail names only the shared categories and counts include only them; no re-sharing; one not-found answer for every not-shared case; the admin has no access; no notification; the new `src/sharing/` component joins grants to graph reads so `src/circles/` still cannot read a graph; item data is returned as it is, capped), goals and non-goals, definitions, a visibility table, 12 functional (WS-FR) and 7 non-functional (WS-NFR) requirements, seven routes, a 9-row threat table (covering tests come in T-151), what the person is told, the **release gates** (pseudonymisation, a data processing agreement and the provider's terms, a lawful basis and a risk assessment, a statement of who may use the service and the consent wording) and 10 acceptance criteria (WS-AC). - 2026-10-10 — Grants in the circle store port and the memory store (T-140): the `CircleStore` port gains `setGrant(circleId, ownerId, granteeId, categories, now, { maxCategoriesPerGrant })`, `getGrant`, `listGrantsBy` (what an owner shares, one grant per grantee, by grantee id), `listGrantsTo` (what is shared with a grantee, one per owner, by owner id) and `withdrawGrant`, with `Grant` and `GrantLimits` exported from `circles` (both API reports changed). **Rules in the store**: both people must be current members (`NOT_FOUND`, also for a missing circle and also for an empty set, so even a withdrawal by a stranger is refused), never to oneself (`INVALID_INPUT`, field `granteeId`), each category an id of 1 to 256 characters counted as characters (`INVALID_INPUT`, field `categories`), duplicates collapsed, at most the limit after collapsing (`LIMIT_REACHED`, nothing changes), the set is **replaced** not merged, an empty set removes the grant and answers `ok(undefined)`, categories are kept in plain code-unit order, and the store never looks at a graph (an unknown category is accepted: checking is the caller's job). **A grant ends in the same step** when either person leaves or is removed (`removeMember`), when `removeUser` takes a person out of any circle (including a hand-over and a dissolved circle), and when the circle is deleted; a refused removal (the only owner) leaves grants alone; a role change keeps them; rejoining resurrects nothing. **Conformance**: 28 new cases in a new `grants` group (`ConformanceOptions.groups` lets a store that does not keep grants yet run only `core`, and the SQLite store does that until T-141 removes the exclusion; its grant methods answer `STORAGE_ERROR` / empty for now), including simultaneous replacements of one grant (exactly one of them survives, never a mixture), a grant racing with the person leaving and with the circle being deleted (nothing is left behind). **24 new deliberately broken stores** (no member check, self grant, merging, limit off or counting duplicates, no dedupe, unsorted, id length ignored, an empty set keeping the grant, a symmetric grant, shared copies, grants surviving leave or circle deletion or `removeUser`, leave dropping other circles or only one direction, grants hidden but not deleted so they come back, a role change dropping them, listings crossing circles, and two racy ones) are each caught. 22 mutants of the memory store, all caught. 159 files, 5,008 tests in the gate. - 2026-10-10 — Grants in the SQLite store (T-141): users.db **schema version 5** adds `circle_grants`: one row per owner and grantee in a circle holding the categories as a JSON array (so any id round-trips exactly, a lone surrogate included, and replacing a grant is one statement), with `CHECK` that the categories are valid and not empty and that owner and grantee differ, a primary key on the pair, a cascade from the circle, an index by grantee, and the trigger **`circle_grants_end_with_member`** (after a membership row is deleted, the grants that person made or held in that circle go), so a grant never outlives a membership whoever deletes it: leaving, removal, the account-deletion trigger of version 4 or a cascade. The rule for what a grant may hold now lives in one shared pure function, `src/circles/grants.ts` (`normaliseCategories`, the self-grant error), used by both stores so they cannot disagree. `setGrant` runs in one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction that checks the circle, both memberships and the set before writing. The **whole 28-case `grants` group passes on the SQLite store on `:memory:` and on a file**, and the temporary `groups: ['core']` exclusion is gone (the `groups` option stays for other stores). Extra tests (`grants.test.ts`, 25): the schema and the trigger exist, a version 4 database is upgraded without touching users, circles, members or invitations, the database itself refuses a self-grant, an empty set, non-JSON and a second row for one pair, deleting a membership row or a circle row or a user row ends the right grants and no others, a refused step and a rolled-back transaction change nothing, a lone surrogate and plain code-unit order are kept, a reopened file and a second store on one file agree, **another process replacing a grant 400 times while the person leaves** (no `ok` ever follows a refusal and no grant is left), and **50 random worlds with grants and mixed removals (a member leaving, a circle deleted, an account deleted, a grant set) where the memory store and the database must be equal after every step**, with no grant ever left between two people who are not both members. Mutation-checked: 25 mutants, 23 caught; the two survivors are equivalent (removing the cascade on the grant table, since the membership trigger already clears them first, and the grantee index, which is speed only). The two version assertions now say 5 and the table list includes `circle_grants`; the users-sqlite API report changed (the version constant). 160 files, 5,125 tests in the gate.
- 2026-10-10 — The sharing component: grant management (T-142): new component **`src/sharing/`** with entry point **`bipartite-graph/sharing`** (its own API report; package export; both api scripts; `dist/sharing` in the package check; a container in the architecture diagram marked "Being built"). `createSharing({ users, directory, store, graphAdapter, limits?, clock? })`: `users` only needs `resolve`, `directory` only `get`, `store` is the circle store (membership and grants), `graphAdapter` is read-only. **Boundary rules** (about 20 new cases): sharing may use only the indexes of `users`, `circles` and `graph_store` (its tests may also use the memory adapter and the SQLite stores), never the LLM, the application, the CaCi controller, the API, the service or the SQLite wrapper; only the API and the service may import it, and only through `sharing/index`; no other component may; the real tree is checked for it. **Methods** (all by session token, results use the circles error type): `setGrant(token, circleId, granteeId, { categories })`, `grants(token, circleId, page?)` (what I share, one entry per member, paged), `withdraw(token, circleId, granteeId)` (idempotent, `true` if there was one). **Order of checks**: session; the caller is a member of the circle (or the one "no such circle", also for a malformed, made-up or non-text id); the grantee is a current member (or "no such member", also for a malformed id); never oneself (`INVALID_INPUT`, `granteeId`); the input is an object with only `categories` (any other field refused by name, a long key cut); ids of 1 to 256 characters, duplicates collapsed, at most 200 (`LIMIT_REACHED`, configurable); **every category must exist in the caller's own graph** (read through the public `query`, partition `category`, 50 at a time; an item's id or another person's category is refused as `INVALID_INPUT` on `categories` without repeating the id; a missing graph holds none); then the store sets it. **The owner always comes from the session**: there is no way to name whose categories are shared. Views carry the grantee's user id, username and display name (names left out if the account is gone) and the shared categories that still exist (id, name when there is one); a category deleted later is simply not shown. Any failure inside, and any storage error from a store in its own words, is the one fixed message "the sharing service could not complete the request"; a user service that answers with anything other than "not signed in" is a storage failure, not a sign-out. **Read-only**: a recording adapter shows no write primitive is ever called by any operation, successful or refused. Tests (59, each run over the memory store and a SQLite store): happy path and views, replace, empty withdraws, duplicates, own-categories rule, no id echoed, bad ids, limits, opaque ids, strict input, owner-from-session, one direction and roles carrying nothing, stranger and made-up and malformed all the same, non-member grantee, other circle, sessions, listing pages and bad pages, vanished categories, a grantee gone from the directory, withdraw, leaving ends it, no writes, failures, a missing graph, construction. Mutation-checked: 29 mutants; 5 survived at first: two are equivalent (the sharing component's own member and self checks, which the store repeats with the same answer, kept so that a refused request never reads the graph) and three got tests (names, a non-sign-out failure of the user service, frozen results) and are caught. `circles` now also exports `normaliseCategories` and `MAX_CATEGORY_ID` (its API report changed). 161 files, 5,206 tests in the gate. Note: `package.json` also holds an edit I did not make (a `live` script that starts the service with the real model); the commit stages my changes to it only.
- 2026-10-10 — Reading a shared brain (T-143): `Sharing` gained `sharedWithMe`, `sharedCategories`, `sharedCategoryItems` and `sharedItem` (public views `SharedBrainView`, `SharedCategoryEntry`, `SharedItemView`, `SharedCategoryItems`, `SharedItemDetail`). Every call resolves the session, checks the caller is a member (else `no such circle`), then re-reads the grant from the store (`getGrant(circle, owner, me)`) so narrowing, withdrawing, leaving and removal take effect on the next call; **every way of having no grant (not a user id, oneself, not a member, no grant, made-up) is the one answer `no such shared brain`**, a category not named in the grant or not existing is `no such category` and an item filed under no shared category is `no such item`, identical for real and made-up ids. Reads go to the owner's graph (`userGraphId(owner)`, never a parameter) through the public `query` only; an item's detail lists only edges to shared categories and counts are over shared categories only. Categories are paged by an opaque, strictly re-encoded cursor over the grant's sorted ids (shared categories deleted from the owner's brain are skipped; `categoryCount` counts only those that exist); items use the graph store's own cursor (a bad one is `INVALID_INPUT` on `cursor`). Data is capped as in the owner's own browsing (4,096 characters of JSON, strings cut to 300, dropped to `{}` if still over, flagged; the cap is duplicated in `sharing.ts` because the component may not import `caci/`). A recording adapter proves no write primitive is called. 36 new tests over memory and SQLite; 25 deliberate defects, 7 survivors all equivalent (self check, membership re-check and empty-grant check are backed by the store, which never holds those; other-item edges and non-item nodes cannot occur in a one-item subgraph; a no-op mutant; non-item nodes in a filtered listing). 5,242 tests in the gate.

Tested at route level (13) and through the running service on real files (3): set, list, narrow, withdraw, a restart keeping a grant, a stranger, removal, account deletion and circle deletion ending grants, and the Secure flag on a cleared cookie. 9 mutants in the routes, 2 in the wiring, all caught (the secure-flag one after a test was added). `startCircleApp` in the test util now exposes its graph adapter and stores to `more`. 164 files, 5,258 tests in the gate.

Circle and person ids are checked by the exact shape before a path is built (a bad one is a not-found and nothing is sent: "no such circle", "that person is no longer in this circle" for grant calls, "nothing is shared with you by that person" for reads); category and item ids are any text of 1 to 256 characters (no lone surrogate) and go only in the query string, encoded. Only `{ categories }` is ever sent (a list over 200, a non-text id or a non-list is refused locally on `categories`); the empty list sends and the 204 answer is `{ grant: null }`; every answer is cut down (no email, graph id or hash), frozen, and a wrongly shaped item refuses the whole page. **A 404 is classified by the service's fixed words** ("no such circle" means the circle, everything else is what the call asked about), with our words; every not-shared case therefore reads as one not-found, and 403 uses our words, 422 names a field only if the call has it, 429 is a limit in the service's words or a wait, anything else is a fixed server message.
