})`): `open(circleId)`, `refresh`, `morePeople`, `selectPerson`, `moreCategories`, `selectCategory`, `moreItems`, `selectItem`, `back` (item, category, categories, people; refused while busy, sends nothing); the selection is page state; **when what you were looking at is no longer shared the view says so in the one set of words (a gone category or item keeps a way back) and the list of people is reloaded**, a person no longer sharing returns to the list with the words, and choosing a category or item before a person sends nothing. `web/sharing-world.test-util.mjs` (a real service with three accounts and ann's seeded brain, now also used by `sharing-service.test.mjs`) and `sharing-sessions-service.test.mjs` (6 tests against the real service: the grants walk-through, a member who left, a stranger, the shared walk including awkward ids, withdrawal making the open item gone, a private category and an item reachable only through it reading as a made-up one). 30 + 30 unit tests; about 70 mutants, 12 survived at first and each led to a test (ticking while busy, back while busy, a gone circle emptying everything, choosing another person dropping the open category, a late answer's busy mark, a failed refresh showing no people); the rest are equivalent (a categories first-load failure resets items already). **Twenty-six web files** (list, tests, package check, page guard and README updated). 170 files, 5,358 tests in the gate.

- 2026-10-10 — R-009 revised and ready to build (T-153, documentation only): **observers may read a circle brain and cannot capture** (D4, the definitions now name reading roles and capturing roles, SC-FR-05, SC-FR-07, SC-FR-09, the authorisation matrix, the threat row for observers, SC-AC-04 and the build-order text; an observer's capture request gets the same not-found as a stranger's); **editing and deleting approved shared items is nobody in v1**; **no repair table**: an idempotent reconcile of circle records against circle graphs, run at start-up and after an account deletion, with a failed compensation a fixed storage failure that the next reconcile repairs (the two open decisions became *Decisions settled for the first release*, which also records that tasks and actions are out of scope and go to external tools at categorisation time); the status line says it is being built as S2 and states its **relation to R-008** (R-008 shares chosen categories of a personal brain, R-009 is a circle-owned brain; they coexist and never touch each other's data). `spec.md` gains the S2 milestone line, R-004's residual risk 8 points at R-009 as well, and the container diagram gains a **Scopes** container marked *Planned* with its three relationships (the architecture test exempts a planned container until its folder exists, so T-154 must remove that word). 173 files, 5,484 tests in the gate.
- 2026-10-10 — The scopes component, scaffold and scope types (T-154): new component **`src/scopes/`** with entry point `bipartite-graph/scopes` (API report `bipartite-graph-scopes.api.md`, package export, both api scripts, `dist/scopes` in the package check; the container's *Planned* word is gone). **Boundary rules** (20 new violation cases, clean layouts, and a real-tree test): scopes may use only the indexes of `users`, `circles`, `graph_store` and `app` (its tests may also use the memory adapter and the SQLite stores) and not the LLM component, the CaCi controller, the API, the service, the SQLite wrapper or the sharing component; **only the CaCi controller and the service may depend on scopes, and only through `scopes/index`**; nothing may depend on the API or service from it; it is added to the no-environment-or-files list. Contents: `Scope` (`{ kind: 'personal' }` or `{ kind: 'circle', circleId }`, closed) and `Provenance` (adds the circle name, filled by the resolver), `parseScope` (**`undefined` means personal**; anything else must be exactly one of the two shapes; any other key, including a graph id, user id or `__proto__`, is refused by name, a long key is cut in the message and named `scope`; a circle id must have the service's exact shape; circle scopes are frozen), `circleGraphId` (`circle-<circleId>`; a non-circle-id throws `TypeError` because the id never comes from a request; checked against the real graph store for 200 ids) and `circleIdOfGraph` (the reverse, `undefined` for any other graph id, for the reconcile), and `ScopesError` (codes `INVALID_INPUT`, `NOT_FOUND`, `STORAGE_ERROR`, fixed messages, frozen). 38 tests; 16 mutants, 15 caught and 1 equivalent (an array is refused as having no kind either way); one gap (`circleIdOfGraph` without its prefix check) got a test. `package.json` was staged without the owner's uncommitted `live` script. 174 files, 5,521 tests in the gate.
- 2026-10-10 — Brain access policy and the scope resolver (T-155): **`authoriseBrain(role, action)`** in `src/scopes/policy.ts`, one pure table: owner, manager and member may `read` and `capture`, **an observer may read and not capture**, and anything not in the table (an unknown or non-text role, an unknown action, a prototype name) is refused, tested over every role and action and against lookalikes. **`createScopeResolver({ users, store })`** (`src/scopes/resolver.ts`; `users` needs only `resolve`, `store` only `membershipOf` and `getCircle`): `who(token)` and `resolve(token, scope, action)` turn a session and a scope into exactly one `ResolvedScope { userId, scope, graphId, provenance, role? }` or one fixed answer. A personal scope is the caller's own graph for either action and **asks the circle store nothing**; a circle scope needs the caller to be a **current member whose role allows the action**, and a stranger, a person in another circle only, a made-up circle, a malformed or upper-cased id, a non-text id, a deleted circle and (for capture) an observer **all get the identical `NOT_FOUND` "no such circle"**; the role is read from the store on every call (5 membership calls for 5 resolutions in the test), so a demotion, removal and promotion take effect at once; a session that does not work is `UNAUTHENTICATED` and nothing else is asked, while a throwing user service or a user service that answers anything other than "not signed in" is a `STORAGE_ERROR`, never a sign-out; a store that throws is the one fixed storage message and never the thrown text; a scope that is not one of the two shapes is `INVALID_INPUT` on `scope` before any store call; the resolver has no graph adapter and reads nothing from any graph; its results are frozen. `ScopesError` gained `UNAUTHENTICATED` (four codes). Shared test support: `src/scopes/scopes.test-util.ts` (five people, a memory or in-memory SQLite circle store that records its calls, a fake session check). Every test over the resolver runs on both stores. 39 tests; 31 mutants, all caught or removed (a redundant role pre-check was deleted as equivalent; a lint rule needed the role tables written as `as const`). 175 files, 5,543 tests in the gate.
- 2026-10-10 — Reconciling circle records and circle graphs (T-156): **`reconcileCircleGraphs({ circles, graphAdapter, maxDrops? })`** and the pure **`planReconcile(circleIds, graphIds)`** in `src/scopes/reconcile.ts`. Every circle that exists gets its graph (created if missing) and every `circle-<circle id>` graph whose circle is gone is dropped; **only graphs named exactly `circle-<valid circle id>` are ever considered**, so user graphs, `circle-x`, upper-cased, prefixed and suffixed look-alikes and other names are never touched (and data in graphs that stay is intact). **Graphs are listed first and circles second, and a circle record is always made before its graph**, so a circle created during a run is never mistaken for a missing one (tested with a circle and graph injected between the two listings). **Both listings must complete before anything changes**; a listing that throws, a graph listing that fails (then the circle store is never asked) or a cursor that does not move on or goes back (found after two pages, not after 100,000) is the one fixed storage error and nothing is created or dropped; a creation or drop that fails is the same error and leaves what was done before it (the next run continues); a graph that appears between planning and creating (`CONFLICT`) or is already gone when dropped is not an error. **A safety cap: more than `maxDrops` (default 100) graphs to drop means none is dropped** (the report's `skipped` names them), because that many orphans at once is more likely a faulty listing than real. Invalid settings throw `TypeError` synchronously. To list circles the circle store port gained **`listCircleIds(page)`** (every circle id by id, keyset paging like every listing), implemented in the memory store, the SQLite store and the naive fixture store, with a new conformance case (empty, order, a circle owned by someone else, a deleted circle gone, the exact-fill cursor rule, bad limits and cursors) that every store runs; the circles API report changed by one line. Tests (27 + the store case, memory and SQLite): the pure plan, creating and dropping, idempotence, frozen reports, more than a page of each (450 circles and 450 orphan graphs: three circle pages), 40 random worlds (the circle graphs end up exactly as the circles need, other graphs and every note intact), the injected-circle race, and **the real SQLite stack: deleting a sole owner's account through the user store dissolves the circle by the database trigger, the next reconcile drops its graph, and a circle handed to a surviving member keeps its graph and its note**. 27 mutants, all caught after the loop guard, the order of listing, a vanished graph, the default cap and the page size got tests. 176 files, 5,573 tests in the gate.
- 2026-10-10 — Circle graph lifecycle, wired into the service (T-157): **`withCircleGraphs(circles, graphAdapter)`** and **`afterAccountDeletion(users, after)`** in `src/scopes/lifecycle.ts`. *Create*: the circle record first (so a graph never exists without its record), then the graph; if the graph cannot be made the record is deleted again, so a success never leaves only one of the two, and if even that fails the answer is still the one fixed failure and the next reconcile makes the graph; a graph that already exists is not an error; a refusal by the circle controller (a bad name, no session, an extra field) passes through and makes no graph. *Delete*: the record first (the controller checks the caller is an owner, so **a manager, a member or a stranger cannot cause the graph to be dropped**), then the graph, tried twice; a graph that cannot be dropped leaves the circle deleted (nobody can reach the leftover) and answers in fixed words ("the circle was deleted, but its notes could not be removed yet; they will be removed at the next clean-up"), and the reconcile then removes it; a graph already gone is not an error. Every other method of the controller is the given controller's own (identity checked), and both wrappers are frozen and refuse bad arguments with `TypeError`. `afterAccountDeletion` runs a function after a **successful** `deleteMe` or `deleteUser` (awaited before the answer returns, never after a refusal, and a failure or a synchronous throw of it never changes the answer), because deleting an account can dissolve a circle through the database trigger and leave its graph. **The service** now builds the circle controller through `withCircleGraphs`, the user controller through `afterAccountDeletion(…, reconcile)`, and **runs the reconcile at start-up** (so circles made before circle graphs existed get theirs and graphs of vanished circles are dropped); `ServiceOptions.onReconcile` is told every result (a throwing observer cannot stop the service), and `npm run serve` says "Circle graphs brought in step: N made, M removed." only when it did something, warns when graphs were left because they exceeded the safety cap, and warns when the check itself failed (`describeReconcile`, tested directly). Tests: `lifecycle.test.ts` (28 over both circle stores, plus the account wrapper) and `src/service/circle-graphs.test.ts` (8 through the running service on real files over real HTTP: create and delete with a second connection looking at `graphs.db`, a refused request changing nothing, a manager and a stranger unable to drop a graph with notes in it, **deleting a sole owner's account (by the owner and by an administrator) removing the circle's graph before the answer returns while a circle handed to another member keeps its graph and note**, a start-up that creates a missing graph and drops an orphan with its notes while a note in a good circle survives, a database from before circle graphs getting all of them on the first start, an empty reconcile report, and a throwing observer). 24 mutants of the wrappers, 5 of the service wiring and 7 of the command-line text, all caught after a test for exactly one leftover graph. SC-FR-01, SC-FR-02, SC-AC-08, SC-AC-09 and SC-AC-14's graph half are covered; the scoped capture and browse that use these graphs follow in T-158 and T-160. 178 files, 5,609 tests in the gate.
- 2026-10-10 — Scoped proposals (T-158): `CaciController.propose(token, { text, scope? })` takes an optional scope (`createCaciController` gained an optional `scopes: ScopeResolver`); **omitted or `{ kind: 'personal' }` is exactly R-003** (the proposal view has the same keys, no `scope`). A circle scope is parsed strictly (a malformed scope names its field as `scope`, `scope.circleId` or `scope.<stray key>`, and a stray top-level key such as `graphId` is refused by name; the note is validated first), then resolved with the **capture** right, so owner, manager and member may and an observer, a stranger, a made-up circle and a controller with no resolver all get the identical NOT_FOUND "no such circle" and **no model call is made**; a store failure is the fixed graph-source STORAGE_ERROR and also no model call. **The resolved scope and graph are bound to the proposal when it is made and never change** (held beside its owner): approval ignores any scope sent with it (tested with an extra argument), two proposals for two circles each land in their own circle in either approval order, and nothing reaches the personal graph or another circle. **Membership and the capture right are re-checked from the store at every `get` and `approve`**: a removed member, an observer (demotion), a deleted circle all read as "no such proposal", write nothing, discard the proposal and free its place, while a store that cannot answer keeps the proposal pending with the fixed storage error and works when the store is back; a role change that still allows capture changes nothing; **a person may always reject their own proposal**; another member of the same circle, an administrator and a stranger get the same "no such proposal" for get, approve and reject. A circle proposal's view carries `scope: { kind: 'circle', circleId, circleName }` (never a graph id). **The categorisation prompt holds only the selected scope's categories**: with distinctive categories seeded in the personal graph and two circles, the captured provider requests for each scope contain its own marker and neither of the others (SC-AC-07). Pending and hourly limits are per account across scopes. 25 new tests; 21 mutants, 18 caught at first and the rest fixed (a redundant graph-id comparison removed; the personal-view and resolver-failure mappings got direct tests). `controller.test-util.ts` gained a circle store, a resolver and a `circle()` helper. The routes still refuse a `scope` in the body until T-163. SC-FR-03 to 08 and SC-AC-01 (capture half), 06, 07, 11 covered. 179 files, 5,628 tests in the gate.
- 2026-10-10 — Contribution metadata (T-159): `ProposeOptions.stamp: { contributedBy }` on the capture controller (`src/app/`), used by the CaCi controller **only for circle-scope proposals** with the session's user id. After the model has answered and the guard has accepted, the mutation is rebuilt: `contributedBy` and `contributedAt` (the controller's own clock) are **removed from every upsert, on items and categories alike, and then set on the one item the proposal creates**, so the model cannot set, replace or forge them (a model that tries to put `u-forged` and a time of 1 on every node ends up with neither; categories carry no such fields; link operations are untouched and gain no `data`). A personal capture is not stamped and not cleaned (R-003 unchanged). The preview's operations show the stamp, an approved circle item stores it (readable through browse, where it is part of the item's data), and **removing the contributor from the circle leaves the record and its stamp in place** (SC-FR-02, SC-FR-14, SC-AC-14's record half). 4 new tests (26 in `controller-scopes.test.ts`); 12 mutants over the stamping and its use, all caught. A slip worth recording: reverting a file with `git checkout` to undo a mutation helper's edit discarded my own uncommitted change; I redid it. 179 files, 5,631 tests in the gate.
- 2026-10-10 — Scoped browse (T-160): the CaCi controller's `summary(token, scope?)`, `categories(token, page?, scope?)`, `categoryItems(token, id, page?, scope?)` and `item(token, id, scope?)` take an optional trailing scope. **Omitted or personal is exactly the old behaviour (no `scope` key in any answer)**; a circle scope is parsed strictly, resolved with the **read** right (so **an observer can browse**, as can owner, manager and member) and the answer names the circle (`scope: { kind: 'circle', circleId, circleName }` on the summary, category page, category items page and item detail; the results are frozen). A stranger, an administrator who is not a member, a person in another circle only and a made-up circle get the identical NOT_FOUND "no such circle" from all four calls; a removal, demotion to observer (still readable), promotion and deleted circle take effect on the next request; a malformed scope names its field (`scope`, `scope.circleId`, `scope.graphId`); a controller with no resolver finds no circle; a store failure is the fixed graph-source STORAGE_ERROR; a circle whose graph is missing is a graph error and never anyone else's data. **Identical node ids in the personal graph and two circles never cross** (SC-NFR-02: each answer is checked for the other scopes' labels); awkward ids (spaces, `/ ? & #`, `%`, markup, emoji, `..`) work in a circle, an id that exists only in another scope is not found, paging works inside a circle and **a cursor from one scope is refused in another**, and what a member captured into a circle is readable by an observer with `contributedBy` in the item's data. 13 tests; 12 mutants all caught. SC-FR-09, SC-NFR-02, SC-AC-01 (browse half), SC-AC-04, SC-AC-05, SC-AC-12 (browse half) covered. 180 files, 5,644 tests in the gate.
- 2026-10-10 — My Brain, part 1 (T-161): `ScopeResolver.readable(token)` (own brain first, then every circle the person is in, any role, by circle id, read from the store each call, paged past 100 with a loop guard, **more than `maxScopes` (default 25) is `LIMIT_REACHED` naming the number, never cut short**; `ScopesError` gained `LIMIT_REACHED`) and three CaCi methods built in `src/caci/brain.ts`: **`brainScopes`** (each scope with its provenance, the person's role and whether they may capture; circles they are not in do not appear), **`mineSummary`** (totals over every readable scope and the counts of each, tagged; identical ids are never merged; a circle whose graph is missing counts as empty while a missing personal graph is an error; counts are read `maxConcurrentReads` (default 4) at a time) and **`mineCategories`** (every scope's categories tagged with where they came from, in a fixed order: own brain, circles by id, categories by id; the same id in two scopes stays two rows; paging fills each page from the scopes in turn, so any page size gives the same sequence with no gaps or repeats, a page of exactly the right size ends cleanly, no page is ever longer than its limit, a circle with no graph is skipped). **Cursors are opaque and bound to the person, the view and exactly which scopes were readable, in order**: one person's cursor is refused for another even when both read the same circles, a cursor taken when the list of scopes was different (a circle joined that sorts first) is refused instead of landing in the wrong scope, and forged versions (other view, fingerprint or version, positions out of range, odd types, non-canonical spellings, garbage) are refused naming `cursor`; the same request twice gives deep-equal results and cursors (SC-AC-10). A person in no circle makes **no read of any circle graph** (a recording adapter shows only their own graph read, SC-AC-13), a removal shows on the next request, an administrator who is not a member sees only their own brain, a store failure is the fixed storage error, too many scopes is `INVALID_INPUT` on `scope`, and with no resolver only the personal brain exists. 19 + 12 tests (the resolver's `readable` runs on both circle stores); the readable-scopes and aggregate code were mutation-checked with about 40 mutants, all caught except three equivalents (the version, view and fingerprint checks are subsumed by re-encoding the cursor and compared back to it; the view part of the fingerprint is the same for the only view there is until T-162; a length cap is subsumed by the parse). In My Brain a category or an item is identified by **where it is and its id**: the reference has exactly `scope` and `id` (a missing scope is `INVALID_INPUT` on `scope`; a stray key such as `graphId` is refused by name; a non-text or empty id is `INVALID_INPUT` on `id`), and the scope must be one the person may read now (their own, or a circle in any reading role; a stranger, an administrator who is not a member, a circle they are not in and a made-up circle all get the identical NOT_FOUND "no such circle"; no resolver means only the personal scope). - 2026-10-11 — A consistent snapshot primitive (T-172): `snapshotDatabase(source, destination, { applicationIds })` and `inspectDatabase(path, applicationIds)` in `src/sqlite/snapshot.ts` (internal for now; the service gets it in T-173). `VACUUM INTO` reads the source in one read transaction, so a live writer cannot leave half a transaction in the copy and the service need not stop. The copy is written under a hidden temporary name created owner-only (0600) **before** any byte is written, opened and checked (integrity, application id, schema version, number of tables and indexes against the source), and only then renamed to the name asked for, so a name that exists is a complete verified snapshot. Refused, leaving everything as it was and creating nothing: a destination that exists or whose folder is missing, a missing source (checking never creates a database), a directory, a file that is not a database, a database whose application id is not in the allowed list (an empty list allows nothing), empty or NUL-containing paths. Tested (11): contents, mode, a committed-but-only-in-the-log state, a key that is not valid text kept byte for byte, **twelve snapshots taken while a child process commits 4,000 two-row transactions (every copy has equal row counts and the writer was moving)**, each refusal, a damaged file not called intact. Mutation-checked (15): 9 caught; the survivors are defence in depth that cannot fail without a corrupt copy (integrity, id, version, object count after `VACUUM INTO`), a friendlier message that the later open would also produce (the missing-folder check), an empty-source check the existence check repeats, a lock wait that is not exercised, and the cleanup after a failure that cannot be forced. 187 files, 5,905 tests in the gate.
- 2026-10-11 — The backup command (T-173): `backupData(args, env, io)` in `src/service/backup.ts` (public) and `npm run backup -- --dest DIR [--keep N]` (`scripts/backup.mjs`; **deliberately without `npm run build`**, because a rebuild deletes `dist/` under a running service, so the Pi runs it after a deploy has built). It snapshots `graphs.db` and `users.db` from `CACI_DATA_DIR` (read directly, default `./data`; the other settings and the API key are not needed and never printed) into `<dest>/<UTC time, like 20261011T020000Z>/` (folders 0700, files 0600; a second backup in the same second gets `-2`, `-3`, which sort after), each file verified by T-172 against its own application id (a graph database and a user database swapped are refused). **Decision recorded: `--dest` is a required argument and not a setting**, because an unknown `CACI_` name makes the service refuse to start and the env file is shared; the systemd unit passes it. **Rotation**: only after a good new snapshot exists are leftovers of failed runs, incomplete timestamp-named folders and the oldest complete snapshots beyond `--keep` (1 to 1000, default 14) removed; a failing backup removes nothing, so a failing night cannot shorten the history; folders and files that do not look like snapshots (`pre-restore-*`, notes) are never touched. Refused (exit 1, nothing created or removed): a missing database (never created), a destination inside the data folder (also through a symbolic link, also the folder itself, a sibling with the same prefix is fine) or that is a file, an unwritable destination; wrong arguments exit 2 with the usage (unknown, duplicate, missing or odd `--dest`/`--keep`). Tested over real files (29), including **a real running service with two registered accounts, whose snapshot holds both**; 25 mutants, 3 survivors at first (`--dest` followed by another flag, the application-id check, a destination that is a file) of which two got tests and one is equivalent (`mkdir` already fails on a file). **Boundary rule widened**: the service may use `sqlite/snapshot` (and only that file of the SQLite wrapper), with a clean-layout case and the old violations kept. The graph SQLite entry point now exports `GRAPH_APPLICATION_ID` and `GRAPH_SCHEMA_VERSION`; two API reports re-baselined; `service.test-util.ts` gives tests a complete `ServiceConfig`. `package.json` was staged without the owner's `live` script. 188 files, 5,934 tests in the gate.
- 2026-10-11 — The restore command (T-174): `restoreData(args, env, io)` in `src/service/restore.ts` (public) and `npm run restore -- <snapshot folder> --yes` (`scripts/restore.mjs`, no rebuild). **Everything is checked before the first change**: the folder exists, is outside the data folder, holds both files, each with its own application id (a database of the wrong kind under the right name is refused, tested with a user database given a graph-sized version), intact (a damaged file is refused), and **not newer than this build understands** (graph schema 1, user schema 5; one above either is refused, exactly the current version is fine). Without `--yes` it says the snapshot is good and what it would replace, changes nothing, exit 2. A second line of defence refuses if it cannot take a write lock on either current file within 300 ms (the service must be stopped; an idle service holds no lock, so this is not the control). Then it **copies the current files aside** (also their `-wal`, `-shm`, `-journal`) into `pre-restore-<UTC time>[-n]` **beside the snapshots**, folder 0700 and files 0600 (a machine with no data yet has nothing to put aside), writes each replacement beside its target under a temporary name and verifies it, removes stale side files, and only then renames both into place; a failure cleans the temporary files and says where the originals are. Creates a missing data folder owner-only. Tested end to end (16): **a real service with an account, a circle and an approved note is backed up, then a second account, a deleted circle and another note are made, the service is stopped and restored: it starts, the first account logs in, the second does not exist, the circle and the note count are the snapshot's, and the changed state is in the `pre-restore` folder**; a fresh machine; two restores in one second; no temporary or side files left; every refusal leaves the data files byte-identical and creates no aside folder; usage errors. - 2026-10-11 — Deploy logic, pure and injected (T-175): `deploy/deploy.mjs` (new top-level `deploy/` folder: never published, covered by vitest, eslint and the not-published guard, which now also names `deploy/`). `runDeploy({ ref, host, log, secrets?, timeoutMs? })` where **`host` is a set of named operations** (`nodeVersion`, `sqliteLoads`, `workingTreeClean`, `currentCommit`, `snapshot`, `fetchAndCheckout`, `checkout`, `install`, `build`, `stopService`, `startService`, `restartService`, `waitHealthy`, `restore`; each answers `{ ok, detail? }` or throws), not one raw `exec` as the plan wrote: clearer, and the real wiring in T-176 stays small. Sequence: refuse before any change (ref shape: 1 to 200 letters, digits, dots, dashes, underscores or slashes, starting with a letter or digit, no `..`, `//`, trailing `/` or `.`, not `.lock`; Node at least 22.13 and `node:sqlite` loads; no local changes; a readable current commit; **all problems listed at once**), then **snapshot (no snapshot, no deploy)**, then prepare (fetch and check out, `npm ci`, build) without touching the running service; a failure there checks out the previous commit and rebuilds it (a failed build leaves `dist` deleted) and exits 10, or 11 with the by-hand text if that fails too; then restart and wait for health; if either fails: stop, previous code back and rebuilt, **restore the snapshot**, start, check health, exit 10, or 11 at the first rollback step that fails. Exit codes 0, 10, 11, 12 (`EXIT`). Every step has a time limit (default 20 min install, 10 min build, 90 s health, 5 min other), and a throw, a refusal and a hang are all the same failure; nothing printed can contain an `sk-ant-…` key or a string the caller names as a secret (short ones are ignored so ordinary words are not blanked), a logger that throws changes nothing. Tested (65) with a pretend machine that records every call and fails, throws or hangs on demand: success order, the service untouched until built, each refusal, each prepare failure (service never stopped, previous commit rebuilt), each rollback step failing, a hung health check, redaction. Mutation-checked (30): all caught after the rollback test was fixed to let the first install succeed (a string result means failure in my fake, so the first version of that test failed in the wrong place). 190 files, 6,015 tests in the gate.

- 2026-10-13 — **Status updates planned (Q1, T-181 to T-194), owner decisions; this corrects my earlier reading of roadmap item 8.** I had read "request update" as one member asking another, with a request, an answer, an inbox and consent. The owner's answers say otherwise: **a status update is a request to CaCi**. The person (who must have an account, in 0..M circles) types free text ("I am heading into town", "Give me a morning status check"); CaCi combines **the person's personal brain and every circle brain they can read** as background context (and, later, other information such as location or weather, not now) and answers **only that person**, **briefly and in a standard format**; **nothing is filed in any brain**; **observers can ask** (it only reads); **in-app only** (no inbox, no notifications, no inter-person requests, no consent mechanism: those parts of the old item are dropped). It is the first answering capability of the LLM component. Personal-data worries are parked as for D1 (the page says plainly that everything you can read is sent to the model with the request; the release gates stand for anything wider). Proposed by me, to confirm in T-181: a new capability `status` (default tier `balanced`), the model cites numbered context entries and the server maps numbers to provenance, a fixed output (headline up to 160 characters, up to five points of up to 240, or an explicit "nothing relevant"), newest-first context within a size budget (said when cut) with the current time in a validated optional time zone, a read-only path proved by a recording adapter, `CACI_STATUS_PER_HOUR` (default 20) and one request in flight per account, a deterministic demo stand-in, a `#/status` screen, no history. Tasks: spec, prompt and guard, `status()` in `createLlm`, demo model, context reader, controller, route and setting, wiring and HTTP end-to-end, web client and logic, page, serve and hand check, an evaluation set for status, security review, README. Backlog triaged: item 8 rewritten, the Answering line under Later milestones points at Q1, item 3's link to item 8 removed, and a note of what comes after the first status added.
- 2026-10-13 — **D1 confirmed working on the real Pi (owner)**: the Pi deployment is a working proof of concept, and the owner and their wife use the web app from the home Wi-Fi for testing. This closes the pending hand run recorded under T-180. **The real run found one bug in my install script, which the owner fixed in `1164616` ("Updated installer bug")**: with `--deploy-key` the script cloned the private repository *before* telling git which key to use (it set `core.sshCommand` only after the clone), so the first clone could not authenticate; the clone now runs with `GIT_SSH_COMMAND` set, and the test asserts the exact clone command. My stand-in test used a local repository, which needs no key, so it could not have caught it: a lesson that a test double hides exactly the part it replaces. Real family use now exists, which raises the value of the Backlog items about data on the Pi (snapshots copied off the device, HTTPS, pseudonymisation) and of Q1, whose status screen would be the first daily-use feature.

- 2026-10-11 — The `status` prompt and output guard (T-182): new capability folder `src/llm/capabilities/status/` (internal until T-183), pure and never throwing. **`buildStatusPrompt({ text, entries, now, timeZone }, options?)`**: a fixed trusted system prompt that depends only on the entry count and the current time in the person's zone (`Thursday 15 October 2026, 08:30 (Europe/London)`) and contains none of the person's or the entries' text (tested); the request and the numbered entries (`[n] (where, day) text`, `where` being the scope's name, which is user-written) only inside escaped `<entries>` and `<request>` blocks, with line breaks inside an entry flattened so it cannot start a fake entry, and the same escaping as the categoriser; refused, never cut: a blank request, one over 500 characters (counted as characters), more than 500 entries, an entry over 1,500, more than 30,000 in all, an unknown zone (checked with `Intl`), malformed input of any kind (a CONFIG error naming where); the output schema (closed objects; `headline` 1 to 160, `points` up to 5 of `text` up to 240 and `refs` 1 to 5 integers within this request's 1 to N, `nothing` boolean; all three required); golden files for the system prompt, user message and schema. **`inspectStatusReply`/`guardStatusReply`**: exactly one JSON object with only `headline`, `points` and `nothing`; every text is one line of plain text (no control characters or breaks), trimmed, counted as characters; **every cited number must be a whole number between 1 and the number of entries, each once per point** (so provenance cannot be invented); `nothing: true` only with no points and a reply with no points must say it; **every problem is listed** (the rejection message numbers the first five, like the categoriser's); a throwing or hostile object is a refusal. **Kernel change**: `src/llm/text-blocks.ts` now holds `escapeForBlock` and `rejectionError`, which the categoriser re-exports unchanged, so no capability imports another (the boundary test still passes). 62 new tests (6,209 in the gate); 54 mutants, 49 caught; the survivors are equivalent or defence in depth (a redundant `headline === undefined`, `slice` on a list already reported as too long, a push condition that only matters when an error is returned anyway, and the time-zone name pattern and length that `Intl` itself enforces).

Same flow as `categorise`: validate options and input before any call (a bad time zone, unknown field, blank or over-long request, bad entries are CONFIG errors with no model call), `resolveModel` for the new capability `status` (**default route: the `balanced` tier**, provisional until T-192; `CAPABILITIES` is now `categorise`, `status`), build the prompt with **the clock's time as now** and the person's zone (default UTC), one call, the guard, **one bounded repair** (the rejected reply echoed back cut at 4,000 characters and the numbered reasons), **one time limit over both attempts** (the repair gets what is left; none left is TIMEOUT), usage summed, cancellation, errors from the client returned as they are and never repaired, a client that throws or returns rubbish is MODEL_ERROR, and `status` never throws. The result is a frozen-all-the-way-down `StatusAnswer { headline, points: [{ text, refs }], nothing, usage, model, attempts }`. Defaults: 30,000 ms, 1,000 output tokens. **Kernel change**: the trace event type and the emitter moved to `src/llm/trace.ts` (`TraceEvent`, `makeEmitter`), shared by both capabilities; `CategoriseEvent` is now an alias and the categoriser's 100+ existing tests pass unchanged. The LLM lab keeps listing only the categoriser (a status lab would be new work). Tested (42 in `status.test.ts` plus the touched config and categorise tests): the happy path, model choice by call, route and default, one repair and its usage, every provider error, client misbehaviour, the shared time limit, hang and cancel, refusals of bad input, text trying to be instructions, the trace in order and a trace that throws, and the signal and remaining time reaching the client. - 2026-10-11 — The status context reader (T-185): `src/caci/status-context.ts` (internal until T-186), `createStatusContext(adapter, limits?).read(readable, now)` over the scopes `readable()` returns, through the public `query` only. Per scope it pages the items (500 at a time, at most 2,000 read), takes the **newest 200** (a circle item's `contributedAt`, else the time inside a minted id read as base 36, else unknown and last; a stamp from the future or an id from before 2020 is not believed), merges **newest first across scopes** (ties by scope order, then id in code-unit order) and takes entries **until the character budget (12,000, counting text and where-from) would be exceeded, stopping at the first that does not fit** so what is used is always the newest; category names are read only for the chosen items (50 seed ids at a time, up to 8 shown, sorted by name, the id when a category has no name). An entry is one line: `title: summary`, up to five other data keys as short `key: value`, `filed under …`, cut by character (600), control characters and line breaks to single spaces, `(no text)` for nothing; **`contributedBy` (a user id) and `contributedAt` never appear in the text**. Where from: `personal` or `circle "Name"` (name one line, cut at 60, JSON-quoted so quotes and markup stay text); the provenance is kept beside the entries for the controller to map. Result: frozen `{ entries, provenance, entriesLeftOut }`; a circle without its graph contributes nothing, a missing personal graph is a graph error, any other store failure fails the read, a person with no circle touches no circle graph, a scope it is not given is never read, identical ids in different scopes stay separate entries, and **a recording adapter proves no write primitive is ever called**. Settings are validated (positive whole numbers, `TypeError`). Tested (31): ordering and time sources, budget, caps, paging over 1,100 items, categories beyond 50 items, text details, isolation, failures, no writes. The `prompt` trace event of both capabilities now carries **`parts: { controls: { system, frame }, request, context }`** (the request and the context as they sit in the user message: escaped, in their block; the controls as the rendered system message and the frame text), built by the prompt builders from the same blocks that make the user message (`userBlocks`, `statusBlocks`) and **deep-frozen** (the trace's copies were frozen only at the top before). The LLM lab shows them in three labelled panels in order (1 the request, 2 the context, 3 the controls with their frame), above the as-sent system, user message and schema. Proofs (`src/llm/parts.test.ts`, 9): with **sentinel controls**, the system message is exactly the sentinel text with code's slot values and the user message is exactly the sentinel frame around the two blocks, for both capabilities; the three parts add up to what was sent; **changing only the request, only the context or only the controls changes only that part** (each both ways, with an empty context still a block); and the repair turn is the first message, the rejected reply, and the guard's numbered reasons plus one fixed closing line, in that exact shape. **A source scan** (`src/llm/parts-scan.test.ts`, 10): no production file but the two capabilities calls a model client (nothing in app, caci, api, service, scopes, sharing, circles or users does); each sends only `prompt.value.system` and the built messages (and the repair turn); and **no line of the shipped `system.txt` or `frame.txt` files appears in any production source file** (so no wording is hidden in code; the scan is shown able to see a sentence in code). R-012's D17 and risks now say plainly that the repair turn's guard reasons and closing line are code-written text sent to the model. 14 mutants, all caught after one gap in the lab test. 225 files, 6,725 tests in the gate.

- 2026-10-11 — Scenario format, loader and validator (T-206): new dev tool folder `dev/brain-eval/` (never published). `text.mjs` is the shared matcher: `normalise` (case, numbers as digits, '2x', '2 x', '2 ×', 'two times' as one thing, punctuation and hyphens as spaces), `tokens` (a trailing plural s removed from words of four letters or more, never 'ss'), whole-word phrase matching (`hasPhrase`, `meetsGroups`: every group needs one alternative). `scenario.mjs`: `validateScenario` lists **every** problem at once, each starting with the scenario, note or question it is about: unknown fields refused at every level; ids of lowercase letters, digits and hyphens, unique; notes with an ISO date or date-time (a date alone is 09:00 UTC) in non-decreasing order and 1 to 8,000 characters; questions with a known kind (`recall`, `override`, `retract`, `multi`, `distractor`), a checkpoint that is a note id, an ask of 1 to 500 characters, an optional time not before its note; `must` as non-empty groups of non-empty alternatives (required except for a distractor), `stale` only on override and retract questions (required on override), a multi question needs two groups, a distractor sets `expectNothing` and has no must or stale; **answerable** (every must group has an alternative in a note at or before the checkpoint) and **the override is real** (each stale phrase first appears in an earlier note than the note that first holds the newest of the must groups, so an unchanged fact that is also named does not count as the new one). `loadScenarios(dir)` reads every `.json` file in name order, ignores other files and refuses a missing folder, no scenarios, bad JSON, a scenario problem or a duplicate id, naming the file; `askedAt` gives a question's time (its own, or an hour after its note). 77 tests; 33 mutants, 5 survivors at first (an equivalent early return in the matcher that the loop makes redundant and was removed; boundaries for 500 characters, out-of-order notes beyond the first, an alternative present in an early note, and a group with one of two alternatives) each got a test and all are caught. 227 files, 6,876 tests in the gate.
- 2026-10-11 — 2026-10-14 — Scoring by rules (T-207): `dev/brain-eval/score.mjs` `scoreAnswer(question, answer)` (must groups met in the headline or any one point; each stale phrase is `surfaced` unless every unit holding it also holds a cue such as no longer, previously, instead of, then `superseded`; `nothing:true` passes only distractors and fails every other question) and `aggregate` (pass rate, stale-surfaced rate, superseded rate). 50 tests including 2 x Tablet A to 1 x Tablet B in many wordings.
- 2026-10-11 — 2026-10-14 — The brain arm (T-208): `dev/brain-eval/brain-arm.mjs` `runBrainArm` builds the real stack in memory (user controller with long sessions, capture controller, CaCi controller) on an injected clock, files each note through propose and approve at its own date with the filing model (a failure is recorded, not fatal), and asks each question as a status request per answering model (one CaCi controller per model over the same stores, models chosen by route config) and repeat, recording answer, tokens, attempts and a diagnostic (must phrases in the stored items, in the entries actually sent, in the answer; `lostAt` filing/context/answer). It never creates a client: the caller supplies `makeClient`. Notes: the brain stores both the old and the new fact (no supersession); the demo model's summaries are cut at 120 characters.
- 2026-10-11 — 2026-10-14 — The replay arm (T-209): `dev/brain-eval/replay-arm.mjs` `runReplayArm` answers each question with the same `status` capability, controls and answering model, given every raw note up to the checkpoint as an entry with its date (`oldest-first` default, `newest-first` option), the entry and size limits lifted per call (with 200 characters of room for the 'where' label, which the limits also count); a transcript estimated above the window (default 150,000 tokens, four characters to a token) is marked `over-window` and not sent. Nothing is filed; same record shape as the brain arm (`inBrain` is true by definition, so `lostAt` is context or answer).

A real run prints its plan (about N calls split into filing / brain / replay, models, controls) and stops unless --yes; --demo is free and refuses --yes; the key is read once by run.mjs, handed to one client and never printed or saved (tested on every path); Ctrl+C stops between calls and still reports and saves. The report: pass, old-fact-as-current and old-fact-mentioned-as-old rates and tokens per arm and model, the same by question kind, brain-minus-replay, where failed answers lost the fact (filing / context / answer / stale), filing results, notes left out of the context, a per-question table and the failures with answers. The saved JSON (git-ignored `brain-eval-results/`) holds scenario hashes and question lists so T-213 can compare runs.

With 300 filler notes the demo run shows the status context leaving out the oldest entries.

- 2026-10-14 — **First real brain-eval run (owner; `brain-eval-results/2026-10-14T10-49-07-873Z-real.json`, git-ignored)**: 8 scenarios, 44 questions, brain and replay arms, fast/balanced/deep answering, filed with fast, 3 repeats, 792 answers, 107 of 107 notes filed, no errors. Raw pass rates: brain fast 83%, balanced 61%, deep 70%; replay fast 87%, balanced 61%, deep 75%; **those numbers are dominated by a scoring artefact**: 151 answers were marked 'old fact given as current' and 136 of them contain a change word ('moved from the original', 'originally', 'replaces'), which the cue list did not know, and `balanced` was failing distractors by saying 'nothing mentions piano' with `nothing: false`; stronger models explain the change more, so they were punished more. **Real signals that survive**: replay recall is 100% against about 90% for the brain (17 failures lost at filing, 11 in the answer; the `mum-tablets` question failed in all 9 brain runs and the ward fact was lost at filing twice), and the brain is level with or slightly behind replay on every model, even with notes far under the context budget. No answer gave an old value as current without saying it had changed. Conclusion: the brain does not yet beat replay; fix the scoring (T-215) and then change what the brain stores (V1).
- 2026-10-19 — **The current-view brain planned (V1, T-215 to T-231), owner decisions**: the brain keeps only the current view and **a new fact deletes the old information it replaces** (keeps the graph clean, small and accurate over years; history is not the graph's job and old notes can be analysed elsewhere); the replacement is **automatic**, with **a log of what was added, removed and updated for review** (the ledger, as a review log and not history); it applies to **personal and circle brains**; and **the brain-eval scoring fix comes first**. Proposed by me, to confirm in T-216 (a new `specs/R-014-current-view-brain.md`): the model never writes delete operations; the controller finds candidate items by word overlap, the categoriser may only name candidate ids in a `replaces` field (at most 5, checked by the guard), and the controller turns them into `deleteNode` operations in the same atomic write as the new note; only items are replaced and emptied categories are tidied afterwards; the preview lists what will be removed (not a second approval); anyone who may capture in a circle may replace there; the ledger (`src/ledger/`, users.db schema 6, kept `CACI_LEDGER_DAYS` default 90) records one entry per change with the removed text so a wrong deletion can be spotted and re-entered by hand; the graph and the ledger are in different databases, so a failed append leaves the change in place and is reported. Honest risk: an automatic deletion by a model can be wrong; the brain-eval gains scenarios that add a different fact and a 'lost at replace' diagnostic to measure it. Order: scoring fix and re-score, spec, ledger store (memory, SQLite), candidates, categoriser and demo changes, proposal and preview, tidy and circle rules, ledger writing and reading, wiring and end-to-end, web preview, ledger client, ledger screen, eval measurement, security review and README.
2026-10-19 — Brain-eval scoring fixed and the first run re-scored (T-215): the superseding cues now include word beginnings (replac, origin, earlier, older, previous, correct, cancel, reschedul, mov, updat, chang and others, `CUE_STEMS`), and a question that expects nothing passes when its headline says nothing was found (`NOT_FOUND_PHRASES`) even if other notes follow; `RULES_VERSION` is 2. `npm run brain-eval -- --rescore FILE [--force]` re-scores a saved result offline (no model, no key), refuses changed scenarios, rebuilds the summary and diagnostic and saves `NAME-rescored.json`. **Corrected baseline of the first real run (792 answers, 143 verdicts changed)**: pass rate brain fast 86%, balanced 89%, deep 87%; replay fast 96%, balanced 96%, deep 92%; old fact given as current 3 to 6% in every cell; brain minus replay -10, -8, -5 pp (fast, balanced, deep). Recall: replay 100%, brain 89 to 93%; brain failures lost at filing 6, 5, 6 and at answer 8, 2, 1 (fast, balanced, deep); the weakest kind in both arms is retract (56 to 67%). So with fair scoring the old fact rarely resurfaces and the brain is a few points behind replay, mostly on recall lost at filing, which V1 and later work address. 29 mutants of the scorer, re-scorer and command, all caught.

2026-10-19 — The ledger store (T-217): new component `src/ledger/` with entry points `bipartite-graph/ledger` and `bipartite-graph/ledger/testing` (two API reports, package exports, both api scripts, `ledger` in the dist list; the container diagram gains a Ledger container). `LedgerEntry` = id, at, scope (`personal` user or `circle`), actor, action (`added`, `updated`, `removed`), kind (`item`, `category`), subject, optional title (200 characters) and summary (400), proposalId; one shared `entryProblem` check (strict fields, ids of 1 to 128 letters, digits, - or _, subject up to 256 characters, limits counted as characters), `capText` for callers, `newLedgerId` (`l` + 16). `LedgerStore`: `append(batch)` is all or nothing (a bad entry, a used id or a repeated id keeps none of the batch; an id used in another scope also counts), `list(scope, { limit, cursor })` newest first (same time by id, highest first) with a cursor bound to its scope and re-encoded strictly (a bad limit or cursor is a RangeError), `purgeBefore(time)` (strictly older), `removeScope(scope)`; every read and write is a copy. Memory store plus the shared suite `runLedgerStoreConformance` (24 cases: order, paging at every size, stability under newer appends, cursors, scope isolation, all-or-nothing batches, the field limits at their exact edges, odd text, copies, purge, remove scope, 600-entry batch, simultaneous appends, two stores sharing nothing) and **21 deliberately broken stores each caught**. Boundary rules (about 20 new cases plus a real-tree test): the ledger may use only the users index (and `users/sqlite/index` in its SQLite store and tests), nothing may depend on it but the CaCi controller (index only) and the service (index and sqlite index), it joins the SQLite wrapper users and the no-environment-or-files list. 45 mutants of the memory store, cursor and entry check, all caught. 7,284 tests in the gate.
2026-10-19 — The SQLite ledger store (T-218): `createSqliteLedgerStore` in `bipartite-graph/ledger/sqlite` (its own API report and package export; `node:sqlite` is only loaded through it) and users.db **schema version 6** (`USERS_SCHEMA_VERSION` 6, owned by the users schema so every store on the file knows every migration): table `ledger` (text columns hold JSON so any subject, title or summary, a lone surrogate included, is kept exactly; checks on action, kind, scope kind, time and JSON), indexes by scope and time, and triggers that remove a circle's entries when the circle row goes and a person's personal entries when their user row goes, by any route. A batch is one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction, all or nothing; a failure is a `STORAGE_ERROR` with a fixed message. **It passes the whole 24-case conformance suite unchanged, on `:memory:` and on a real file per test.** Also tested: the schema and its triggers, a version 5 file upgraded without touching its users or circles, the user, session, circle and ledger stores sharing one file, the graph database and a newer schema refused, the database's own checks, deleting a circle row or a user row (a circle's entries a person wrote stay), reopening, owner-only mode and one file after a clean close, a lone surrogate, two stores on one file, a held lock giving a clear error, **kill -9 inside a batch leaving none of it**, and two processes appending 80 entries losing none. The circle store tests' version and table-list assertions follow (6; the table list gains `ledger`). Boundary rules: ledger tests (not production code) may use the graph SQLite adapter, the circle SQLite store and the users schema file. 31 mutants of the store and the migration, all caught. 7,345 tests in the gate.

2026-10-19 — The demo model replaces too (T-221): when the categoriser's prompt carries an `<existing>` block, the demo model (reading it, not any wording) names the one existing note that the new note replaces: the note must read like a change (a word such as now, changed, moved, rescheduled, corrected, correction, cancelled, instead, switched, replaced, new, actually) and share at least three words with the existing note (a word counts once, entities are read as characters, the same word rule as the demo's category matching); the best match wins, the lowest number on a tie, at most one; otherwise the reply has no `replaces`. The reason says which note it replaces. It is a stand-in, not a judge of meaning. Tested (12): the dose, a moved date, a correction and a cancellation replace; a note that only adds (same person, other fact), a cue without enough shared words, shared words without a cue, and no existing notes replace nothing; best match and tie; determinism; odd characters and long text; a controls folder with other wording still works with it; **1,500 random notes and existing lists (markup, forged blocks, emoji, control characters) all accepted by the real guard first time with `replaces` always in range**. 15 mutants, all caught (two needed new tests: a repeated word counts once, an entity is read as a character). 7,414 tests in the gate.
2026-10-19 — The replacement in the proposal and its preview (T-222): the capture controller (`src/app`) now, in `propose`, reads the **candidate items of the target graph only** (T-219) after the categories, offers them to `categorise` as `existing`, maps the numbers in `proposal.replaces` back to the ids it found and **builds the `deleteNode` operations itself**, placed before the new note's operations, then re-checks the whole mutation with the real `parseMutation` under the graph options (over the limits is a graph error before anything is held). `ControllerInit.candidates` is `false` (off; the service will set it from the controls' `maxReplaces`) or the search options; the model never sees an id. `summarise(mutation, existing, offered?)` now understands a `deleteNode` of an offered item (listed in `ProposalSummary.removed` as { id, title, summary }, once each) and reports as a problem a delete of anything else; without `offered` any delete is refused as before. `describeSummary` gains 'This note replaces, and these will be removed when you approve:' with one line per removed note, so the CaCi proposal view already carries it in `summary.removed` and `text` (no separate `removes` field; R-014 amended at T-231). Tested (27 new): the dose story end to end on the real memory store (the old note and its links go and the new note arrives in one write; proposing writes nothing), all or nothing when a link is bad, a note that only adds replaces nothing, an unshown number refused and repaired, hostile ids in the note and reply ignored, two proposals replacing the same note, a note already gone, stamps, another graph never read or touched, categories never candidates or deleted, off switches, the ops limit, a failing read. Mutants of the controller and summary all caught or equivalent (a cast-only mutant; copying a candidate's three fields). **Side effect handled here**: with the demo model now replacing, the brain-eval's brain arm replaces too (its default is the product's behaviour); its older tests (both facts stored, ideal and parrot answerers over the library, the padding and prompt comparisons) now say `replace: false`, new tests cover `replace: true`, and the arm switches itself off when the controls' `maxReplaces` is 0. The command-line switch and the measurement of wrong deletions are T-230. One read of up to 2,000 items per propose when on. 7,448 tests in the gate.
2026-10-19 — Tidy and the circle rules (T-223): after a successful approval of a proposal that removes notes, the capture controller removes **categories left with no notes**: before the write it reads which categories the removed notes were filed under (with their names, while their links still exist), after the write it counts the notes in each and removes those with none (one `deleteNode` per category, idempotent); a category the new note links to has a note and stays, one the removed notes were never in is never looked at, and an approval that removes nothing does no tidy work at all (one transaction, the write). `Approved.tidied` lists the removed categories ({ id, name? }, frozen); the CaCi `ApprovedView` carries `tidied` only when something was tidied. A failed read or tidy never fails the approval and calls `ControllerInit.onWarning` with one fixed sentence (no ids or text); a throwing hook changes nothing. **Circle rules, tested through the CaCi controller with the demo model**: a member replaces another member's note, owners and managers may too, an observer's attempt gets exactly the stranger's answer and nothing changes, a member demoted to observer after the preview cannot approve (the old note stays), and a circle never reads or removes the personal brain or another circle. Tested: 10 tidy tests at the app level (a shared category stays, a category with no name, the failure cases, a failed write tidies nothing) and 9 through the CaCi controller. 16 mutants of the tidy and the view, all caught or equivalent (an empty-ids read that cannot reach the adapter; depth 2 reaching the same categories). The grant case ('a grant naming a removed category shows nothing') is covered by the existing sharing test that a category deleted later is simply not shown. 7,468 tests in the gate.
2026-10-19 — Writing the ledger (T-224): the CaCi controller takes an optional `ledger` (a `LedgerStore`) and `onLedgerFailure`; after an **approved, successful write** (never for a proposal only made or rejected, a failed write, or a second approval) it appends one batch: the tidied categories, the removed notes (title and summary as the candidate search cut them: 100 and 300 characters), changed existing categories (`updated`, with their name), new categories (`added`) and the new note (`added`, with its title and summary cut to 200 and 400 characters by character), each with the time (one millisecond apart so the newest-first listing shows the new note on top), the scope (the circle for a circle proposal, otherwise the person's own), the actor (the session's user) and the proposal id. **A failed or throwing append never undoes or refuses the change**: the approval still succeeds and the hook gets one fixed sentence ('the change was made but could not be recorded in the change log'), no ids and no text; a throwing hook changes nothing; with no ledger nothing is recorded and the hook is never called. (Existing notes cannot be 'updated' by a note, since the note's item id is minted new, so that case is not logged.) Tested (18): the first note, the dose story with the exact order and the removed text, add-only notes, caps, nothing recorded for propose, reject, a failed write or the losing second approval, circle versus personal logs never mixing, both failure modes, and the model-decided details (an updated category, long titles). 20 mutants, all caught or equivalent. 7,486 tests in the gate. (The previous entry's count 7,468 should read 7,463.)

Newest first, paged (limit 1 to 100, default 50, the cursor bound to its scope by the store; a cursor from another scope is a 422 naming `cursor`), read-only, session cookie only (no cookie is a 401 setting nothing, a stale one is cleared, an Authorization header and a `?token=` are ignored; `scope=mine`, an unknown scope, an unknown parameter, a bad limit or cursor are 422 naming the field). The personal log is the caller's own; a circle's log is readable by any role; **a person in no circle, the administrator who is not a member, and a made-up circle get the identical 404 'no such circle'**; a malformed circle id is a 422 naming `scope.circleId`. An entry shows id, time, action, kind, subject, title and summary (the removed text for a removal) and `by` { username, displayName } when the directory can name the actor (a failing or unknowing directory leaves the name out; one lookup per person per page); never a graph id, proposal id or user id; the circle's name is on a circle answer; views are frozen. - 2026-10-21 — **The input ledger and the brain export planned (L2, T-258 to T-265), from the owner's tests on the Pi**: the Pi stores the extracted facts but not the text typed, so real inputs cannot be recovered. Owner answers: the change ledger also keeps **the text of each update (Send) as sent** (questions and Pulses are not kept), readable by **everyone who can read that brain's change log**; the export is an **operator command on a snapshot** (no member-facing download); **no redaction for now**. The owner's aim is to use real brains as test input to improve LLM answers that are 'a bit mechanical, very long and contain information that is not relevant'. Also put on the Backlog: **context review and timely reminders** (the owner's example: a holiday in France in August 2027 should prompt a passport check in January 2027), improving answers from real brains (an evaluation of length and relevance, tuning `controls/status/`), pseudonymised and member-facing exports, and recent changes in the minimal user app. Nothing built.

Mutation-checked: all caught.
Recording the input on approval (T-260): after an approved, successful Send the CaCi controller appends one input entry (the proposal's note exactly as typed, actor, scope, proposal id) as the oldest of the batch, so in the newest-first log it sits directly under the facts it produced; nothing for a proposal only made, rejected, refused or failed, for questions or Pulses, or for the second of two approvals; a failed or throwing append still never undoes the change and gives the one fixed warning; a personal update is in the personal log and a circle update in the circle's. Existing ledger tests and end-to-end tests updated for the extra entry. The live files are never changed (byte-identical before and after, also with the service running) and no password, hash, session, email, user id or graph id is in the document. 11 tests over a real service's files: a user brain, a circle brain with member authors, the same words in two brains never mixing, a deleted account (circle facts stay without a name, the own brain is no longer found), a deleted circle, odd ids and a lone surrogate, 450 facts and 250 entries over pages, no temporary folder left. - 2026-10-01 — Build order follows spec: contracts, memory adapter and write, conformance suite, then query, file, SQLite.

## Blockers / open questions
- Privacy: captured text goes to an external model (Anthropic). Accepted for the proof of concept, which holds only the owner's own data. A pseudonymisation milestone is in the PLAN Backlog and must be reopened before anyone else's data is used or the tool is offered to others.
- Model defaults: `categorise` runs on the `fast` tier (Haiku 4.5); a first real evaluation supports it (see the decision log) but is a small sample. Heavier future capabilities default to `deep` (Opus 5.5) and are untested. Override per capability, per call or per run in the lab.
- Confirm `upsertNode` `replace` semantics: with no `data` it currently clears existing data. Alternative: omitted `data` leaves existing data untouched in `replace` mode. Spec says the default must never change within a major version, so settle before 1.0.0. Not blocking T-018 to T-022.
- Remaining open question from R-001 spec: expected scale per graph and number of graphs (confirms NFR-05 targets); not blocking until M6.
